# Garuda-purana 2

## Chapter 1

## \|\| 1.1 \|\|

*dharmandṛḍhabaddhamūlo vedaskandhaḥ purāṇaśākhāḍhyaḥ \|*  
*kratukusumo mokṣaphalo madhusūdanapādapo jayati \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. One should begin the recital of Jaya (this holy Purana) after bowing to Narayana, Nara the most excellent among men, Goddess Sarasvati and Vyasa. The renowned Visnu shines victoriously like the wish-giving Kalpa tree with Dharma (virtue) for its firmly fixed roots, the Vedas for its main stem, the Puranas for its branches, the sacrifice for its flames and salvation for its fruits.

## \|\| 1.2 \|\|

*naimiṣe 'nimiṣakṣetre śaunakādyā munīśvarāḥ \|*  
*karmaṇāmantare sūtaṃ svāsīnamidamabruvan \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. In Naimisa forest, the solitary region, the sages Йаипака and others asked Suta who was sitting comfortably on his seat after going through his daily rites.

## \|\| 1.3 \|\|

*sūta jānāsi sakalaṃ vastu vyāsaprasādataḥ \|*  
*tena naḥ sandihānānāṃ sandehaṃ chettumarhasi \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. О Suta, you possess perfect knowledge (of the Puranic lore) through sage Vyasa's favour. Hence, you can clear off our doubts we have in our mind.

## \|\| 1.4-5 \|\|

*yathā tṛṇajalauketi nyāyamā śritya kañcana \|*  
*dehino 'nyatanuprāptiṃ kecittvevaṃ vadanti hi \|\| 4 \|\|*

*kecitpunaryātanānāṃ yāmīnāmupabhogataḥ \|*  
*paścāddehāntaraprāptiṃ vadanti kimu tatrasat \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. Following the maxim of caterpillar, some sages say that the soul attains another body after leaving the previous one. Others hold that the soul enters another body after going through Yama's torture. What is true?

## \|\| 1.6 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*sādhu pṛṣṭaṃ mahābhāgāḥ śṛṇudhvaṃ bhavatāṃ punaḥ \|*  
*sandeho nopapadyeta lokārthaṃ kila pṛcchatām \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Suta said: — Gentlemen, you. have put the right question. Now hear. To allay your doubts.

## \|\| 1.7 \|\|

*apākariṣye sandehaṃ bhavatāṃ bhāvitātmanām \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. I shall take recourse to a dialogue between Krsna and Garuda. I shall try to remove your doubts thereby.

## \|\| 1.8 \|\|

*namaḥ kṛṣṇāya munaye ya enaṃ samupāśritāḥ \|*  
*añjastaranti saṃsārasāgaraṃ kunadīmiva \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Obeisance to sage Krsna. They who take recourse to him can easily cross the ocean of the world as it were a small river.

## \|\| 1.9 \|\|

*ekadā vainateyasya lokānāṃ lokanaspṛhā \|*  
*babhūva so 'tha babhrāma teṣu nāma harergṛṇan \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Once upon a time, Garuda desired to go round the world. He started on journey, wandered all over, muttering the the name

## \|\| 1.10 \|\|

*sa pātālaṃ bhuvaṃ svargaṃ bhrāntvālabdhaśamāśayaḥ \|*  
*lokaduḥ khenātiduḥ khī punarvaikuṇṭhamāgamat \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. He wandered over the earth, in hell and heaven but could not get peace. He returned Vaikuntha, more distressed by the sight of people's sorrows.

## \|\| 1.11 \|\|

*na rajo na tamaścaiva sattvaṃ tābhyāṃ ca miśritam \|*  
*yatra pravartate naiva sattvameva pravartate \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. There in Vaikuntha; neither Rajas exists, nor Tamas, nor Sattva mixed, with either of these two. Sattva alone exists there.

## \|\| 1.12 \|\|

*na yatra māyā nāśaśca na cai rāgādayo malāḥ \|*  
*śyāmāvadātāḥ surucaḥ śatapatravilocanāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Neither Maya nor destruction nor the evils like anger, attachment etc. exist there.

## \|\| 1.13 \|\|

*surāsurārcitā yatra gaṇā viṣṇoḥ supeśasaḥ \|*  
*piśaṅgavastrābhāraṇā maṇiyuṅniṣkabhūṣitāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. There dwell glorious - Visnu's ganas dark and fair, bright, lotus-eyed, worshipped by deities and demons, clad in tawny robes and adorned with ornaments, bedecked with garlands of gold coins set up with jewels.

## \|\| 1.14 \|\|

*caturbhujāḥ kuṇḍalino maulino mālinastathā \|*  
*bhrājiṣṇubhirvimānānāṃ paṅkibhirye mahātmanām \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Possessed of four arms and tufts of hair, wearing earrings and garland, they shone by the rows of resplendent aerial cars.

## \|\| 1.15 \|\|

*dyotante dyotamānānāṃ pramadānāṃ ca paṅktibhiḥ \|*  
*śrīryatra nānāvibhavairhareḥ pādau mudārcati \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. There LaksmI gladly adorns Lord Visnu's feet by offering many precious gems. Herself surrounded by the rows of beautiful damsels and friendly associates she sits in a moving swing and praises Hari.

## \|\| 1.16-21 \|\|

*hariṃ gāyati dolāsthaṃ gīyamānālibhiḥ svayam \|*  
*dadarśa śrīhariṃ tatra śrīpatiṃ sātvatāṃ patim \|\| 16 \|\|*

*jagatpatiṃ yajñapatiṃ pārṣadaiḥ pariṣevitam \|*  
*sunandanandaprabalārhaṇamukhyairnirantaram \|\| 17 \|\|*

*bhṛtyaprasādasumukhamāyatāruṇalocanam \|*  
*kirīṭinaṃ kuṇḍalinaṃ śriyā vakṣasi lakṣitam \|\| 18 \|\|*

*pītāṃśukaṃ caturbāhuṃ prasannahasitānanam \|*  
*abhyarhaṇāsanāsīnaṃ tābhiḥ śaktibhirāvṛtam \|\| 19 \|\|*

*pradhānapuruṣābhyāṃ ca mahatā cāhamā tathā \|*  
*ekādaśondriyaiścaiva pañcabhūtaistathaiva ca \|\| 20 \|\|*

*svarūperamamāṇaṃ tamīśvaraṃ vinatāsutaḥ \|*  
*taddarśanāhlādayutasvānto hṛṣyattanūruhaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

16-21. There he saw Hari All-powerful, Lord of LaksmI, Lord of the good. Lord of the world, lord of the sacrifices, surrounded by the courtiers: Sunanda, Nanda, Prabalarhana, chief among them possessing a face delightful to the servants, possessing big reddish eyes, bearing, crest and ear-rings, being looked at the chest by LaksmI. Wearing yellow garment; possessing four arms, and.a delightful smiling face, sitting on a costly seat surrounded by the powers. Pradhana, Purusa, Mahat, Ahama, the eleven organs, the five elements and delighting in Self. Garuda was pleased to see the Lord. His hair thrilled with joy and with tears comingout of his eyes he bowed to him with the feeling of love.

## \|\| 1.22 \|\|

*locanābhyāmaśru muñcanpremamagno nanāma ha \|*  
*namāgataṃ nataṃ svīya vāhanaṃ viṣṇurabravīt \|*  
*bhūmiḥ kā laṅghitā pakṣiṃstvayeyantamanehasam \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Visnu spoke to Garuda who had cume to see him and who had bowed to him; "O Garuda, tell us how much of this earth you have wandered over and within such a short time."

## \|\| 1.23 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*tava prasādādvaikuṇṭha trailokyaṃ sacarācaram \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Garuda said: — My Lord, by your grace, I have visited the three worlds and seen all beings — the movable and the immovable.

## \|\| 1.24 \|\|

*mayā vilokitaṃ sarvaṃ jagatsthāvarajaṅgamam \|*  
*bhūrlokātsatyaparyantaṃ puraṃ yāmyaṃ vinā prabho \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. From Bhuloka to Satyaloka, I have visited all regions, except the region of Yama, О my lord.

## \|\| 1.25 \|\|

*bhūrlokaḥ sarvalokānāṃ pracuraḥ sarvajantuṣu \|*  
*mānuṣyaṃ sarvabhūtānāṃ bhuktimuktyālayaṃ śubham \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Among all the worlds, the bhuloka is thickly populated by various kinds of creatures. This region is the most auspicious of all inasmuch as it affords a place of enjoyment to all, and is celebrated as the place where they can strive for salvation,

## \|\| 1.26 \|\|

*ataḥ sukṛtināṃ loko na bhūto na bhaviṣyati \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. A region better than this for those who perform good actions has neither been before nor will there ever be.

## \|\| 1.27 \|\|

*gāyanti devāḥ kila gītakāni dhanyāstu ye bhāratabhūmibhāge \|*  
*svargāpavargasya phalārjanāya bhavanti bhūyaḥ puruṣāḥ suratvāt \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. It seems that the gods sing songs to this purport. Those who live in this part of the world called Bharata are in fact blessed. In order to enjoy the fruits of heaven and salvation they are born as human beings, casting off their deityhood.

## \|\| 1.28 \|\|

*pretaḥ kaukṣipyate kasmātpañcaratnaṃ mukhe katham \|*  
*adhastāccālitā darbhāḥ pādau yāmyāṃ vyavasthitau \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Why is the corpse tied to the bamboosticks? Why are the five jewels kept in the mouth? Why is the grass placed beneath? Why.are the feet kept southward?

## \|\| 1.29 \|\|

*kimarthaṃ putrapautrāśca tasya tiṣṭhanti cāgrataḥ \|*  
*kimarthaṃ dīyate dānaṃ godānamapi keśava \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Why do the sons and grandsons walk ahead? Why are the gifts given, including the gift of a cow, О Kesava?

## \|\| 1.30-31 \|\|

*bandhumitrāṇyamitrāśca kṣamāpayanti tatkatham \|*  
*tilālohaṃ hiraṇyaṃ ca karpāsaṃ lavaṇaṃ tathā \|\| 30 \|\|*

*saptadhānyaṃ kṣitirgāvo dīyante kenahetunā \|*  
*kathaṃ hi mriyate janturmṛto vai kutra gacchati \|\| 31 \|\|*

30-31. Why do the relatives; the friends, and even the foes forgive him. Why are the gingelly seeds, iron, gold, cotton, salt, seven types of grains, land (or house) and cow are given? How does a creature die? Having died, where does he go?

## \|\| 1.32 \|\|

*ativāhaśarīraṃ ca kathaṃ hi śrayate tadā \|*  
*śavaṃ skandhe vahetputro agnidātā ca pautrakaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. How does he take shelter in the body being carried? Why does the son carry the corpse on his shoulders and the grandson lights the fire?

## \|\| 1.33 \|\|

*ājyenābhyañjanaṃ kasmātkuta ekāhutikriyā \|*  
*vasundharā kimarthaṃ ca kutaḥ strīśabdakīrtanam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Why is the anointment done with the butter? Wherefrom is the ekdhuti-kriya? Why is Vasundhara performed and wherefrom the lamentations from women?

## \|\| 1.34 \|\|

*yamasūktaṃ kimarthaṃ ca udīcyā diśamāharet \|*  
*pānīyamekavastreṇa sūryabimbanirīkṣaṇam \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Why is the Yama-sukta recited? Why is water carried to the north? Why is the sun looked through a cloth?

## \|\| 1.35 \|\|

*yavasarṣapadūrvāstu pāṣāṇe nimbapatrakam \|*  
*vastraṃ naraśca nārī ca vidadhyādadharottaram \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Why barley, mustard-seed, durva, the two stones, the leaves of margosa? Why do men and women wear one lower and one upper garment.

## \|\| 1.36 \|\|

*annādyaṃ gṛhamāgatya na bhoktavyaṃ janaiḥ saha \|*  
*navakāṃścaiva piṇḍāṃśca kimarthaṃ dadate sutāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Why should the meals, etc. be not taken alongwith other persons after returning home? Why do the sons give nine pindas.

## \|\| 1.37 \|\|

*kimarthaṃ catvare dugdhaṃ yātre pakve ca mṛnmaye \|*  
*kāṣṭhatrayaṃ gaṇābaddhaṃ kṛtvā rātrau catuṣpathe \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Why is the milk in an earthern pot placed on catvara? Why are the three wooden sticks tied together and placed on the cross-roads in the night?

## \|\| 1.38 \|\|

*niśāyāṃ dīyate dīpo yāvadabdaṃ dinedina \|*  
*dāhodakaṃ kimarthaṃ ca kimarthaṃ ca janaiḥ saha \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Why is a lamp placed every night for one year? Why should the water be offered in the name of the deceased by the relatives and other people?

## \|\| 1.39 \|\|

*bhagavannāti vāhaśca nava piṇḍāḥ pradāpayet \|*  
*kathaṃ deyaṃ pitṛbhyaśca vāhasyāvāhanaṃ katham \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Why should the nine pindas be offered? How is the gift to be given to the manes? Why is the call for the bearers of the corpse?

## \|\| 1.40 \|\|

*idañcetkriyate deva kasmātpiṇḍaṃ pradāpayet \|*  
*kiṃ tatpradīyate tasya piṇḍadānādyanantaram \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. When this is done, О lord! then why is the rite of pinda repeated? What should be given next after the gift of pinda is made?

## \|\| 1.41 \|\|

*asthisañcayanaṃ caiva ghaṭasphoṭaṃ tathaiva ca \|*  
*dvitīye 'hni kutaḥ snānaṃ caturthe sāgnike dvije \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Why are the bones collected? Why is the earthen pot broken? Why is a bath taken on the second day? Why on the fourth day?

## \|\| 1.42 \|\|

*daśame kiṃ malasnānaṃ kāryaṃ sarvajanaiḥ saha \|*  
*kasmāttailodvartanaṃ ca skandhavāhagṛhaṃ nayet \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Why is it taken on the tenth day alongwith the other mourners? Why the anointment with oil?

## \|\| 1.43-44 \|\|

*tailodvartanakaṃ cāpi dadhuḥ sthūlajalāśaye \|*  
*daśame 'hani yatpiṇḍaṃ taddadyā dāmiṣeṇa tu \|\| 43 \|\|*

*piṇāñcaikādaśe kasmādvṛṣotsargādipūrvakam \|*  
*bhājanopānahau cchatraṃ vāsāṃsi tvaṅgulīyakam \|\| 44 \|\|*

43-44. The pinda given on the tenth day is done so with meat, why? Why is, the pinda on the eleventh day given after releasing the bull?

## \|\| 1.45-46 \|\|

*trayodaśe 'hni deyaṃ syātpadadānaṃ kimarthakam \|*  
*śrāddhāni ṣoḍaśaitāni abdaṃ yāvatkuto ghaṭaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

*annādyenodakenaiva ṣaṣṭyādhikaśatatrayam \|*  
*dinedine ca dātavyaṃ ghaṭānnaṃ pretatṛptaye \|\| 46 \|\|*

45-46. Why are vessels, shoes, umbrella, clothes and ring given on the thirteenth day. Why the sixteen sraddhas? Why are food, water, etc. given everyday for three-hundred and sixty days of the year? Why is the food put in a vessel for satiating the deceased?

## \|\| 1.47-48 \|\|

*prāpte kāle vai mriyate anityā mānavāḥ prabho \|\| 47 \|\|*

*chidraṃ tu naiva paśyāmi kuto jīvaḥ sa nirgataḥ \|*  
*kuto gacchanti bhūtāni pṛthivyāpo manastathā \|*  
*tejo vadasva me nātha vāyurākāśameva ca \|\| 48 \|\|*

47-48. О lord, the mortal man, dies at the appointed time. I do not see an outlet, then from where does the soul go away? From where do the elements-earth, water, mind, fire, air and sky pass away; tell me, О lord?

## \|\| 1.49 \|\|

*kutaḥ karmendriyāṇīha pañcabuddhīndriyāṇi ca \|*  
*vāyavaścaiva pañcaite kathaṃ gacchanti cātyayam \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. From where do the five organs of action, the five organs of perception and the five vital airs away and how?

## \|\| 1.50 \|\|

*lobhamohādayaḥ pañca śarīre caiva taskarāḥ \|*  
*tṛṣṇā kāmo hyahaṅkāraḥ kuto yānti janārdanā \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. From wher do the five thieves of the body — greed affection, desire, love and pride go away and how?

## \|\| 1.51 \|\|

*puṇyaṃ vāpyathavāpuṇyaṃ yatkiñcitsukṛtaṃ tathā \|*  
*naṣṭe dehe kuto yānti dānāni vividhāni ca \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Whatever little action has been done, whether good or bad or whatever gifts have been tendered which procure merit where do these go away after the body had decayed?

## \|\| 1.52 \|\|

*sapiṇḍanaṃ kimarthaṃ ca pūrṇe saṃvatsare 'pi vā \|*  
*pretasya melanaṃ keṣāṃ kiṃvidhaṃ tatra kārayet \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. Why is the rite of sapindana done withinthe year of death? With whom is the union of the deceased sought and how?

## \|\| 1.53-54 \|\|

*mūrchanātpananādvāpi vipattiryadi jāyate \|*  
*ye dagdhā ye tvadagdhāśca patitā ye narā bhuvi \|\| 53 \|\|*

*yāni cānyāni bhūtāni teṣāmante bhavecca kim \|*  
*pāpino ye durācārā ye cānye gatabuddhayaḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

53-54. What does in the end happen to the people who pass away, in conscious or unconscious state, burnt or unbumt or who have been sinners or bad-charactered.

## \|\| 1.55-56 \|\|

*ātmaghātī brahmahā ca steyī viśvāsaghātakaḥ \|*  
*kapilāyāḥ pibecchūdro yaḥ paṭhedidamakṣaram \|\| 55 \|\|*

*dhārayedbrahmasūtraṃ vā kā gatistasya mādhava \|*  
*śūdrasya brāhmaṇī bhāryā saṃgṛhītā yadā bhavet \|\| 56 \|\|*

55-56. О lord, what is the end of those who commit suicide, murder a Brahmana, or happen to. be thieves or deceivers by nature? What happens to that Shudra who drinks the milk of Kapila cow, who reads — the scriptures who wears the sacred thread or wt possesses a brahmin wife?

## \|\| 1.57 \|\|

*bhīto 'haṃ pāpinastasmāttanme vada jagatprabho \|*  
*anyacca śṛṇu viśvātmanmayā kautukinā rayāt \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. О lord, you tell me all this, for I am terribly afraid of the sinner. Hear something more, О "soul of the universe. While wandering with curiosity, I saw the entire world.

## \|\| 1.58-59 \|\|

*lokāṃllokayatā loke jagāhe viśvamaṇḍalam \|*  
*tatrājani janāndṛṣṭvā duḥ kheṣveva nimajjataḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

*svānte me durdharā pīḍā tatpīḍāto garīyasī \|*  
*tridive ditijātebhyo bhūmau mṛtyurugādibhiḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

58-59. After seeing people merged in sorrows, a,great pain arose in my heart. The affliction on the earth caused by death and disease is greater than affliction from the sight of asuras in paradise.

## \|\| 1.60 \|\|

*iṣṭavastuviyo gaiśca pātāle māmakaṃ bhayam \|*  
*evaṃ na nirbhayaṃ sthānamanyadīśa bhavatpadāt \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. I had the fear of losing my desired object in hell. There is no other place safer than your feet, О Lord.

## \|\| 1.61 \|\|

*asatyaṃ svapnamāyāvatkālena kavalīkṛtam \|*  
*tatrāpi bhārate varṣe bahuduḥ khasya bhāginaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. Time has consumed the unreality as it his consumed the dream-world. Still, I saw a number of people suffering-from sorrows in the land of Bharata.

## \|\| 1.62-63 \|\|

*janā dṛṣṭā mayā rāgadveṣamohādiviplutāḥ \|*  
*kecidandhāḥ kekarākṣāskhaladvācastu paṅgavaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

*khañjāḥ kāṇāśca badhirā mūkāḥ kuṣṭhāśca lomaśāḥ \|*  
*nānārogaparītāśca khapuṣpāccābhimāninaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

62-63. They were merged deep in attachment, envy, affection, etc. Some were blind, some squint-eyed, some dumb, some lame, some one- eyed, some deaf, some suffering from leprosy and other diseases and some from pride as false as the sky-flower.

## \|\| 1.64 \|\|

*teṣāṃ doṣasya vaicitryaṃ mṛtyorgocaratāmapi \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā prasumanāḥ prāptaḥ ko mṛtyuścitratā katham \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. After seeing the strangeness of their faults in the face of death. I have come to you with a mind to ask what is death? Why is this strangeness?

## \|\| 1.65 \|\|

*mṛtiryasya vidhānena maraṇādapyanantaram \|*  
*vidhinābdakriyā yasya na sa durgatimāpnuyāt \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. How does death occur? How is it that even after death, if rites are performed for one year one does not fall in the bad state? Formerly, Ihad heard from the sages in general; now I ask you specifically on these points, О lord.

## \|\| 1.66 \|\|

*ṛṣibhyastu mayā pūrvamiti sāmānyataḥ śrutam \|*  
*jñānāya taddviśeṣasya pṛcchāmīdamiti prabho \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. Formely, I had heard from the sages in general; now I ask you specifically on these points, О Lord.

## \|\| 1.67 \|\|

*mriyamāṇasya kiṃ kṛtyaṃ kiṃ dānaṃ vāsavānuja \|*  
*vāhamṛtyorantarāle ko vidhirdahanasya ca \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. O Visnu, what shall a dying roan do? What shall he give in charity? After death, what is the method of cremation?

## \|\| 1.68 \|\|

*sadyo vilambato vā kiṃ dehamanyaṃ prapadyate \|*  
*saṃyamanyāṃ kramyamāṇamāvarṣaṃ kā mṛtikriyā \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. Does he languish or obtain another body? When he lives in Yama's city, what are the rites to. be performed by his descendants in the year of his death?

## \|\| 1.69-70 \|\|

*prāyaścittaṃ durmateḥ kiṃ pañcakādimṛtasya ca \|*  
*prasādaṃ kuru me mohaṃ chettumarhasyaśeṣataḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

*sarvamantemayā pṛṣṭaṃ brūhi lokahitāya vai \|\| 70 \|\|*

69-70. What are the rites of propitiation for the common ignorant deceased? For one dead in pancaka? Be pleased, dispel my ignorance. I have asked all that I wanted to ask only for the welfare of the world. Please answer my queries.

## Chapter 2

## \|\| 2.1 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*sādhu pṛṣṭaṃ tvayā bhadra mānuṣāṇāṃ hitāya vai \|*  
*śṛṇuṣvāvahito bhūtvā sarvamevaurdhvadaihikam \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Sri Krsna said: — О gentle one, you have put up the right question, that too for the general good of the people. Now hear attentively. I shall tell you about the rite to be performed after death.

## \|\| 2.2 \|\|

*samyagvibhedarahitaṃ śrutismṛtisamuddhṛtam \|*  
*yanna dṛṣṭaṃ suraiḥ sendrairyogibhiryogacintakaiḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. It is without any controversy quoted bySrutis and Smrtis. It has not been heard by the gods including Indra, nor by yogins absorbed in yoga. Being the secret of secrets, it has not been conveyed to anyone, anywhere.

## \|\| 2.3 \|\|

*guhyadguhyataraṃ tacca nākhyātaṃ kasyacitkvacit \|*  
*bhaktastvaṃ hi mahābhāga vainateyaṃ bravīmi te \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. О Garuda of great accomplishment, I shall tell you about this since you are my devotee.

## \|\| 2.4 \|\|

*aputrasya gatirnāstu svargo naiva ca naiva ca \|*  
*yena kenāpyupāyena kāryaṃ janma sutasya hi \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. There is no place for a sonless person. The heaven is not for him. One must have a son by any means whatsoever.

## \|\| 2.5 \|\|

*tārayennarakātputtro yadi mokṣo na vidyate \|*  
*skandhaḥ putreṇa kartavyo hyagnidātā ca pautrakaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The son can take his father out of hell, if he cannot get him salvation. The son puts his shoulder to coffin and the grandson lights the fire.

## \|\| 2.6 \|\|

*tiladarbhaiśca bhūmyāṃ vai kuṭī ṛtumatī bhavet \|*  
*pañca ratnāni vaktre tu yena jīvaḥ prarohati \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. A woman in her menses shall stay in the but and lie on the bed bestrewed with sesame and darbha grass, on the ground. She shall put five gems in the mouth. Thus her foetus attains growth.

## \|\| 2.7-8 \|\|

*yadā puṣpaṃ pranaṣṭaṃ hi kva tadā garbhadhāraṇam \|*  
*ādarācca tato bhūmau yena garbhaṃ pradhāryate \|\| 7 \|\|*

*lepyā tu gomayairbhūmistilāndarbhānviniḥ kṣipet \|*  
*tasyāmevāturo muktaḥ sarvaṃ dahati kilbiṣam \|\| 8 \|\|*

7-8. If the seed is destroyed then whereat the conception? Hence, for the protection the foetus she shall lie on the ground enjoined in the sastras. Similarly, in reg to the dying person he is laid on the grog, besmeared with-cowdung and bestrewed with the darling grass. Thus the ailing pe, is released of sins which do not cling to any more.

## \|\| 2.9 \|\|

*darbhatūlī nayetsvargamāturasya na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*darbhāṃstatra kṣipedvātha tūlīgendukamadhyataḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. A bunch of darbha for his bed takes him to heaven no doubt. Darbha should be throve off after the corpse is removed from the plaet.

## \|\| 2.10 \|\|

*sarvatra vasudhāpūtā yatra lepo na vidyate \|*  
*yatra lepaḥ sthitastatra punarlepena śudhyati \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Where there is no besmearing, the ground remains impure. Where the earth is already besmeared it should be. smeared again for the sake of purification.

## \|\| 2.11 \|\|

*yātudhānāḥ piśācāśca rākṣasāḥ krūrakarmiṇaḥ \|*  
*alepaṃ hyāturaṃ muktaṃ viśantyete viyonayaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. If the corpse is kept on the unsmear -cad ground, the foul spirits enter the corpse.

## \|\| 2.12 \|\|

*nityahomastathā śrāddhaṃ pādaśaucaṃ dvije tathā \|*  
*maṇḍalena vinā bhūmyāmāturo mucyate na hi \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. A circle should be drawn around the corpse (to fix up the circumference); sraddha shall be performed where the feet of the Brahmanas should be washed. All these would not serve the corpse, if the same is nit placed on the ground.

## \|\| 2.13 \|\|

*brahmā viṣṇuśca rudraśca śrīrhutāśastathaiva ca \|*  
*maṇḍale copatiṣṭhanti tasmātkurvīta maṇḍalam \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Brahma, Visnu, Rudra, LaksmI and Fire Hence a circle is drawn stay within the circle, around the corpse.

## \|\| 2.14 \|\|

*anyathā mriyate vālo vṛddhastārkṣyayuvāthavā \|*  
*yonyantaraṃ na gacchetsa krīḍate vāyunā saha \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. If a circle is not drawn, the deceased person whether a child, youth or old does not get a womb but remains in the air, O Garuda.

## \|\| 2.15 \|\|

*miśritaṃ lohitāmiśraṃ tadevaṃ janma jīyate \|*  
*tasyaiva vāyubhūtasya na śrāddhaṃ nodakakriyā \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. He may not be born soon but live in the air for some time. He receives no sraddha no libation of water from his relatives.

## \|\| 2.16 \|\|

*mama svedasamudbhūtāstilāstārkṣya pavitrakāḥ \|*  
*asurā dānavā daityā vidravanti tilaistathā \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. О Garuda! Sesame is held sacred, for it is produced from my perspiration. The sesame can destroy the evil spirits.

## \|\| 2.17 \|\|

*tilāḥ śvetāstilā kṛṣṇāstilā gomūtrasaṃnnibhāḥ \|*  
*dahantu te me pāpāni śarīreṇa kṛtāni vai \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. The sesame is white, black or of the burn all colour of the cow's urine. They can sins committed by the deceased.

## \|\| 2.18 \|\|

*eka eva tilo datto hemadroṇatilaiḥ samaḥ \|*  
*tarpaṇe dānahomeṣu datto bhavati cākṣayaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. A single grain of sesame offered with the libation of water to the deceased or an article given in charity or a sacrifice held for his welfare becomes fruitful as drona- kalasa does in rewards.

## \|\| 2.19 \|\|

*darbhā romasamudbhūtāstilāḥ svedeṣu nānyathā \|*  
*devatā dānavāstṛptāḥ śrāddhena pitarastathā \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Darbhas are born from my hair and sesame from my perspiration. By performing sraddha one can please deities, asuras and the manes.

## \|\| 2.20 \|\|

*prayogavidhinā brahmā viśvaṃ cāpyupajīvanām \|*  
*apasavyādito brahmā pitaro devadevatāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20.The Universe and the creator of the universe live by the ceremonial form. By wearing the secred thread over the right shoulder, one can please the creator, the manes and the deities.

## \|\| 2.21-23 \|\|

*tena te pitarastṛptā apasavye kṛte sati \|*  
*darbhamūle sthito brahmā madhye devo janārdanaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

*darbhāgre śaṅkaraṃ vidyāttrayo devāḥ kuśe smṛtāḥ \|*  
*viprā mantrāḥ kaśā vahnistulasī ca khageśvara \|\| 22 \|\|*

*naite nirmālyatāṃ yānti kriyamāṇāḥ punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*tulasī brāhmaṇā gāvo viṣṇurekādaśī khaga \|\| 23 \|\|*

21-23. When the sacred thread is worn over the right shoulder the manes are pleased. Brahma stays at the root of Darbha, Janardana in its middle and Sankara at the point, of darbha. Thus the three deities are said To be stationed in Darbha. The Brahmanas, mantras, sacred grass, fire and TulasI are never spoiled though used again and again. TulasI, Brahmana, Cows, Visnu and EkadasI —

## \|\| 2.24 \|\|

*pañca pravahaṇānye bhavābdhau majjatāṃ nṛṇām \|*  
*viṣṇurekādaśī gītā tulasī vipradhenavaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. These five are the protection boats for the people drowning in the ocean of worl existence. Visnu, EkadasI, Gita, TulasI, Brahmana, and the Cow

## \|\| 2.25 \|\|

*asāre durgasaṃsāre ṣaṭpadī muktidāyinī \|*  
*tilāḥ pavitramatulaṃ darbhāścāpi tulasyatha \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. These are the six steps to salvation in this- universal fortress of little use. Sesame and Darbhas are sacred and so is TulasI.

## \|\| 2.26 \|\|

*nivārayanti caitāni durgatiṃ yāntamāturam \|*  
*hastābhyāmuddhareddarbhāṃstoyena prokṣayedbhuvi \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. These guard the deceased against misfortunes Darbhas should be taken out with hands. The earth should be sprinkled over with water:

## \|\| 2.27 \|\|

*mṛtyukāle kṣipeddarbhānkarayorāturasya ca \|*  
*darbhaistu kṣipyate yo 'sau darbhaistu pariveṣṭitaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. As the hour of death approaches, Darbhas should be placed in the hands of the deceased. He whose body is. spread over by Darbhas goes to Visnu-loka, no matter if he is not initiated in the Vedic lore.

## \|\| 2.28-29 \|\|

*viṣṇuloke sa vai yāti mantrahīno 'pi mānavaḥ \|*  
*tūlīṃ kṛtvā kṛtau pādau saṃsthitau kṣitipṛṣṭhataḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

*prāyāścittaṃ viśuddhāgnau saṃsāre 'sārasāgare \|*  
*gomayenopalimpettu darbhāstaraṇasaṃsthite \|\| 29 \|\|*

28-29. The feet of the deceased person are kept on the ground and the toe raised upward. In this ocean of universal existence, which is practically of little use, the atonement of the sin is made by sacrificing the body in the holy fire. Having-smeared the ground with the cowdung, they should spread the mattress of darbha for the dying person.

## \|\| 2.30-31 \|\|

*yane dattena dānena sarvaṃ pāpaṃ vyapohati \|*  
*lavaṇaṃ tadrasaṃ divyaṃ sarvakāmapradaṃ nṛṇām \|\| 30 \|\|*

*yasmādannarasāḥ sarve notkaṭā lavaṇaṃ vinā \|*  
*pitṝṇāṃ ca priyaṃ bhavyaṃ tasmātsvargapradaṃ bhavet \|\| 31 \|\|*

30-31. Salt is very much effective for the destruction of sins. Let them donate salt for the manes, for it suits their taste and takes them to heaven. As for the men in general, it fulfil their desires.

## \|\| 2.32 \|\|

*viṣṇudehasamudbhūto yato 'yaṃ lavaṇo rasaḥ \|*  
*etatsalavaṇaṃ dānaṃ tena śaṃsanti yoginaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Salt has come out from the body of Visnu, hence yogins recommend a gift of salt placing the same over other gifts.

## \|\| 2.33-34 \|\|

*brāhmaṇakṣattriyaviśāṃ strīṇāṃ śūdrajanasya ca \|*  
*āturasya yadā prāṇā na yānti vasudhātale \|\| 33 \|\|*

*lavaṇaṃ tu tadā deyaṃ dvārasyodvāṭanaṃ divaḥ \|*  
*anyacca śṛṇu pakṣīndra mṛtyo rūpaṃ prapañcataḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

33-34. When at the time of death, the soul does not depart but lingers on to the body, no matter whether the dying person is a Brahmana, Ksatriya, Vaisya or Shudra of either sex, they should donate salt for him whereby the soul leaves the mortal body and enters the doors of heaven which open immediately for him. О king of birds, now hear from me in detail, the various forms of death.

## \|\| 2.35 \|\|

*yasya kālena no yāyādviyogaḥ prāṇadehayoḥ \|*  
*prāṇinaśca svasamaye mṛtyuratyantavismṛtiḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. It is Kala which detaches soul from body. It causes death of the person, at the appointed hour, without a single exception.

## \|\| 2.36 \|\|

*yathā vāyurjaladharānvikarṣati yatastataḥ \|*  
*tadvajjaladavattārkṣya kālasyaiva vaśānugāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. just as the wind scatters clouds here and there, just like that Kala, the Destroyer, floats people from one end of the shore to another.

## \|\| 2.37 \|\|

*sāttvikā rājasāścaiva tāmasā ye ca kecana \|*  
*bhāvāḥ kālātmakāḥ sarve pravartante hi jantuṣu \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Whether they be sattvika or rajasa or tamasa — all these bhavas operate in man due to the influence of Kala,

## \|\| 2.38-39 \|\|

*ādityaścandramāḥ śambhurāpo vāyuḥ śatakratuḥ \|*  
*agniḥ khaṃ pṛthivī mitra oṣadhyo vasavastathā \|\| 38 \|\|*

*saritaḥ sāgarāścaiva bhāvābhāvau ca sarpahan \|*  
*sarve kālena sṛjyante saṃkṣipyante yathā punaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

38-39. Sun, Moon, Siva, water, air, Indra, fire, sky, earth, herbs, Vasus, rivers, seas, existent or non-existent, О Garuda, all these are created and destroyed by Kala in due course of time,.

## \|\| 2.40 \|\|

*kālena saṃhriyante ca nṛnaṃ mṛtyāvupasthite \|*  
*daivayogāttdā vyādhiḥ kaścidutpadyate khaga \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Indeed, О Garuda, when the hour of death approaches, then by chance, a certain disease raises up its head.

## \|\| 2.41 \|\|

*vaikalyamindriyāṇāṃ ca balau joraṃhasāṃ bhavet \|*  
*yugapadvaścikakoṭiśūkadaṃśo bhavedyadi \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. The Organs grow weak. Troubles start showing their powers, as if all of a sudden, millions of scorpions have stung the person.

## \|\| 2.42 \|\|

*tadānumīyate tena pīḍā mṛtyubhavā khaga \|*  
*tataḥ kṣaṇena caitanye vikale jaḍatāṃ gate \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. О bird, then the person begins to feel the pang of death. At the very hour his consciousness is replaced by unconsciousness.

## \|\| 2.43 \|\|

*pricālyante tataḥ prāṇā yāmyairnikaṭavartibhiḥ \|*  
*bībhatsaṃ tu tadā rūpaṃ prāṇaiḥ kaṇṭhagatairbhavet \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. The breath is pushed by Yama's messengers standing nearby. The person assumes a terrible form and the breath lingers to stay in the throat.

## \|\| 2.44-45 \|\|

*phemudgirate so 'tha mukhaṃ lālākulaṃ bhavet \|*  
*aṅguṣṭhamātrapuruṣo hāhā kurvaṃstatastanoḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

*tadaiva nīyate dvatairyāmyairvokṣansvakaṃ gṛham \|*  
*bhūya eva hite tāta mṛtyukāladaśāmimām \|\| 45 \|\|*

44-45. Froth comes out of the mouth full of saliva, just at the moment when every body is lamenting, the soul, as big as a thumb, is carried away by Yama's messengers, even as it looks towards its house. Now hear about the state of death.

## \|\| 2.46 \|\|

*aṣmā prakupitaḥ kāye tīvravāyusamīritaḥ \|*  
*bhinatti marmasthānāni dīpyamāno nirindhanaḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. The heat in the body is disturbed due to sudden commotion of winds. Though without fuel, it breaks up the vital points.

## \|\| 2.47 \|\|

*udāno nāma pavanastataścordhvaṃ pravartate \|*  
*bhaktānāmabubhukṣāṇāmadhogatinirodhakṛt \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. The Udana-wind moves upward. It stops the downward movement of the deceased who in his life-time had full devotion for the lord but no desire in return.

## \|\| 2.48 \|\|

*yairnānṛtāni coktāni prītibhedaḥ kṛto na ca \|*  
*āstikaḥ śraddadhānaśca sa sukhaṃ mṛtyumṛcchati \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. He who has never spoken falsehood, nor has any partiality in devotion but believes in God, obtains death peacefully.

## \|\| 2.49 \|\|

*yo na kāmānna saṃraṃbhānna dveṣāddharmamutsṛjet \|*  
*yathoktakārī saumyaśca sa sukhaṃ mṛtyumṛcchati \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Не who does not stray from Dharma, due to pleasure, wrath or envy but does what he says and is gentle obtains death peacefully.

## \|\| 2.50-52 \|\|

*mohajñānapradātāraḥ prāpnuvanti mahattamaḥ \|*  
*kūṭasākṣī mṛṣāvādī ye ca viśvāsaghātakāḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

*te mohaṃ mṛtyumṛcchanti tathā ye vedanindakāḥ \|*  
*vibhīṣakāḥ pūtigandhā yaṣṭimudgarapāṇayaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

*āgacchanti durātmāno yamasya puruṣāstadā \|*  
*prāpte tvīdṛkpathe ghore jāyate tasya vepathuḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

50-52. Those who preach ignorance pass through darkness. Those who are false witnesses, tiers and deceitful obtain death unconsciously, just as those who abuse the Vedas. The ferocious, foul-smelling messengers of Yama, with clubs and sticks in their hands, come and seize this wicked person. Having reached a ferocious path he begins to shiver.

## \|\| 2.53 \|\|

*krandatyavirataṃ so 'pi pitṛmātṛsutānapi \|*  
*sāsya vāgasphuṭā yatnenaikavarṇā vibhāsate \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. He cries for his father, mother and sons. His indistinct voice, spoken with effort, appears to be single-lettered.

## \|\| 2.54 \|\|

*dṛṣṭirvai bhrāmyate trāsācchvāsācchuṣyati cānanam \|*  
*sa tato vedanāviṣṭastaccharīraṃ vimuñcati \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Due to terrible suffering his looks waver and his mouth gets dry due to lard breath. Then wrapped up in suffering, the va departs from the body.

## \|\| 2.55 \|\|

*aspṛśyaṃ kutsanīyaṃ ca tatkṣaṇādeva jāyate \|*  
*uktaṃ mṛtyoḥ svarūpaṃ tu prasaṅgādanyadapyatha \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. Since that very moment the body becomes untouchable and despicable. Thus I have spoken to you about the form of death of the mortal man.

## \|\| 2.56 \|\|

*vaicitryasyottaraṃ praśre dvitīyasya vadāmi te \|*  
*karmaṇāṃ prāktanānāṃ tu tadasattvenaṃ bhadetaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. Now I shall answer your query about the peculiarities of form the body takes in different births.

## \|\| 2.57-58 \|\|

*bhavedbhogasya vaicitryaṃ bhrāmyatāṃ prāṇināmiha \|*  
*devatvamasuratvaṃ ca yakṣatvādisukhapradam \|\| 57 \|\|*

*mānuṣatvaṃ paśutvaṃ ca pakṣitvādyatiduḥ khadam \|*  
*karmaṇāṃ tāratamyena bhavatīha khageśvara \|\| 58 \|\|*

57-58. Due to different.actions performed in the previous births, the wandering jlvas attain different forms, as of a deity, a demon, a Yaksa, man, beast bird, etc. О lord of birds, these are due to the chain of actions, performed in different ages.

## \|\| 2.59 \|\|

*atra te kīrtayiṣyāmi vipākaṃ karmaṇāmaham \|*  
*vaicitryasya spuṭatvāyayairjovaḥ saṃsaratyayam \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Now, I shall tell you about the results of actions which a person obtains, so that the strangeness involved is explained clearly.

## \|\| 2.60 \|\|

*mahāpātakajānghorānnarakānprāpya dāruṇān \|*  
*karmakṣayātprajāyante mahāpātakinaḥ kṣitau \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. When the shiners have exhausted the fruits of their actions in dreadful hells, they ate born on the earth.

## \|\| 2.61 \|\|

*jāyante lakṣaṇairyaistutāni me śṛṇu sattama \|*  
*mṛgāśvasūkaroṣṭrāṇāṃ brahmahā yonimṛcchati \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. Now, hear-about their characteristics. The killer of a brahmana attains the form of a deer, horse, pig or camel.

## \|\| 2.62 \|\|

*kṛmikīṭapataṅgatvaṃ svarṇahārī samāpnuyāt \|*  
*tṛṇagulmatātvaṃ ca kramaśo gurutalpagaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. The stealer of gold becomes a worm or a germ or a fly. He who lies on the bed of a teacher becomes grass, plant or vine.

## \|\| 2.63 \|\|

*brahmahā kṣayarogī syātsurāpaḥ śyāvadantakaḥ \|*  
*hemahārī tu kunakhī duścarmā gurutalpagaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. The slayer of a brahmana suffers from consumption. A drunkard possesses brown teeth. A gold stealer has bad nails. The defiler of the teacher's bed possesses bad skin.

## \|\| 2.64 \|\|

*yo yena saṃvasatyeṣāṃ sa talliṅgo 'bhijāyate \|*  
*saṃvatsareṇa patati patitena sahācaran \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. That very thing becomes his mark with which he was associated in the previous birth. If he moves with the wretched people he himself becomes, wretched within a year.

## \|\| 2.65 \|\|

*saṃlāpasparśaniḥ śvāsasahayānāśanāsanāt \|*  
*yājanādhyāpanādyaunātpāpaṃ saṃkramate nṛṇām \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. The sins of the people spread by talk, touch, breathing, going together, eating together, sitting together, worshipping together, by teaching and sexual union.

## \|\| 2.66 \|\|

*gatvā dārānpareṣāñca brahmasvamapahṛtya ca \|*  
*araṇye nirjane deśe jāyate brahmarākṣasaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. After having sexual intercourse with the wives of other men or after killing a Brahmana, one becomes a Brahmaraksasa, in a lonely place.

## \|\| 2.67 \|\|

*hīnajātau prajāyeta ratnānāmapahārakaḥ \|*  
*patraṃ ca śākhino hṛtvā gandhāṃśchucchundarī pumān \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. The stealer of a jewel is born in a low caste. A man who steals plants becomes a shrew.

## \|\| 2.68 \|\|

*mūṣako dhānyahārī syādyānamuṣṭraḥ phalaṃ kapiḥ \|*  
*nirmantrabhojanātkāko gṛdhro hṛtvā hyupaskaram \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. The thief of com becomes a rat; that of vehicle, a camel and that of fruit a monkey. He who takes meals uninvited becomes a crow. He who steals utensils becomes a vulture.

## \|\| 2.69 \|\|

*madhudaṃśaḥ phalaṃ gṛdhro gāṃ godhāgniṃ bakastathā \|*  
*syācchvetakuṣṭhī strīvastra hyarucī rasahārakaḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. The stealer of fruit becomes a honey-bee; that of a cow, a vulture; that of a woman's clothes, a white leper and that of juice, possesses no taste at all.

## \|\| 2.70 \|\|

*kāṃsyahārī tu haṃsaḥ syātparasvasya ca hārakaḥ \|*  
*apasmārī gurorhantā krūrakṛdvāmano bhavet \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. The stealer of bronze becomes a swan; so also the stealer of other's possessions. The slayer of his teacher becomes epileptic and the performer of cruel deeds becomes a dwarf.

## \|\| 2.71-72 \|\|

*dharmapatnīṃ tyajañchabdavedhī prāṇī bhavetkṣitau \|*  
*devaviprasvāpahārī pāṇḍuraḥ paramāṃsabhuk \|\| 71 \|\|*

*bhakṣyābhakṣyo gaṇḍamālī mahārogī prajāyate \|*  
*nyāsāpahārī kāṇaḥ syāstrījīvaḥ khañjako bhavet \|\| 72 \|\|*

71-72. He, who discards his wife becomes an animal- to be killed by a hunter. He who snatches the possessions of a deity or a Brahmana suffers from jaundice. He who eats meat becomes a leper. He who does not return the deposit becomes one eyed. He who lives on the earning of his wife be-carves lame.

## \|\| 2.73 \|\|

*kaumāradāratyāgī ca durbhago 'thai kamiṣṭabhuk \|*  
*vātagulmī viprayoṣidgāmī vā jambuko bhavet \|\| 73 \|\|*

73\. He who discards progeny and wife encounters ill-luck. He who eats sweets only becomes rheumatic. He who has sexual union with a Brahmana's wife becomes a jackal.

## \|\| 2.74 \|\|

*śayyāhartā kṣapaṇakaḥ pataṅgo vastrahārakaḥ \|*  
*mātsaryādapi jātyandho kapālī dīpahārakaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

74\. A stealer of bed becomes ksapanaka, and that of clothes becomes a moth. He who envies is born blind. He who steals a lamp becomes a beggar.

## \|\| 2.75 \|\|

*kauśiko mitrahantā ca kṣayī pitrādinindakaḥ \|*  
*skhaladvāganṛtavādī kūṭasākṣī jalodarī \|\| 75 \|\|*

75\. He who kills a friend becomes an owl. He who abuses the ancestors suffers from consumption. He who speaks lies cannot speak properly. He who gives false witness suffers from dropsy.

## \|\| 2.76 \|\|

*maśakaḥ so 'tha cachinnoṣṭho vivāhe vighnakṛdbhavet \|*  
*syādvātha vṛṣalaḥ so 'yaṃ catvare vai viṇmūtrakṛt \|\| 76 \|\|*

76\. He who disturbs a marriage is born as a mosquito. He who eases himself on crossroads is born as a bull.

## \|\| 2.77 \|\|

*mūtrakṛcchrī dūṣakastu kanyāyāḥ klībatāmiyāt \|*  
*dvīpī syādvedavikretā varāho 'yājyayājakaḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. He who spoils a young girl becomes a eunuch. He who sells the Vedas becomes a leopard. He who performs a sacrifice but not in the prescribed manner becomes a pig.

## \|\| 2.78 \|\|

*yatastato 'śranmārjāro khadyoto vahadāhakaḥ \|*  
*kṛmiḥ paryuṣitādaḥ syānmatsarī bhramaro bhavet \|\| 78 \|\|*

78\. He who eats here and there becomes a cat. He who sets fire to a forest becomes a glowworm. He who eats the stale food becomes a worm. He who envies others becomes a bee.

## \|\| 2.79 \|\|

*agnyutsādī tu kuṣṭhī syādadattā'dānato vṛṣaḥ \|*  
*sarpo gohārako 'nnasya hārakaḥ syādajīrṇavān \|\| 79 \|\|*

79\. He who sets fire becomes a leper. He who does not give gifts becomes a bull. A stealer of cows becomes a serpent; that of food suffers from dyspepsia.

## \|\| 2.80 \|\|

*jalahārī tu matsyaḥ syātkṣīrahārī balākikā \|*  
*annaṃ paryuṣitaṃ vipre pradadatkubjatāṃ vrajet \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. A stealer of water becomes fish, that of milk a crane. He who gives stale food to a Brahmana becomes hunch-backed.

## \|\| 2.81 \|\|

*phalāni harate yastu santatirmriyate khaga \|*  
*adattvā bhakṣyamaśrāti hyanapatyo bhavennaraḥ \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. If he steals fruits, his progeny dies, О bird. If he eats alone without giving a morsel of it to anyone else, he becomes issueless

## \|\| 2.82 \|\|

*pravajyāgamanādrājan bhavenmarupiśācakaḥ \|*  
*cātako jalahartā syājjanmāndhaḥ puṃstakaṃ haran \|\| 82 \|\|*

82\. If he does not opt for Samnyasa he becomes an evil spirit in the desert. A stealer of water becomes a Cataka; that of book is born blind.

## \|\| 2.83 \|\|

*pratiśrutya dvijebhyor'thamadadajjambuko bhavet \|*  
*parivādādijātīnāṃ labhate kācchapīṃ tanum \|\| 83 \|\|*

83\. Не who promises but does not give it to the Brahmanas becomes a jackal. He who earns the blame of the people becomes a tortoise.

## \|\| 2.84 \|\|

*durbhagaḥ phalavikretā vṛkaśca vṛṣalīpatiḥ \|*  
*mārjāro 'gniṃ padā spṛṣṭvā rogavānparamāṃsabhuk \|\| 84 \|\|*

84\. A seller of fruits meets with bad luck. He who marries a Shudra woman, becomes a wolf. He who touches fire with foot becomes a cat. He who eats another's meat suffers from diseases.

## \|\| 2.85 \|\|

*jalaprastravaṇaṃ yastu bhindyānmatsyo bhavennaraḥ \|*  
*hareḥ kathāṃ na śṛṇvanti ye na sādhujanastavam \|\| 85 \|\|*

85\. He who breaks a water-reservoir becomes a fish. He who hears neither Hari's tale, nor the praise of the good suffers from ear disease.

## \|\| 2.86-87 \|\|

*tānnarānkarṇamūlo 'yaṃ vyāpnuyānnetarāñjanān \|*  
*parasyānanasaṃsthaṃ yo grāsaṃ hari mandadhīḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

*devopakaraṇānyenaṃ gaṇḍamālinamīhate \|*  
*dambhenācarate dharmaṃ gajacarmā bhavettu saḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

86-87. Не who takes out a morsel from another's mouth becomes a blockhead. He wtio steals the deity's utensils suffers from gandamala. He who observes religion without sincerity-suffers from a skin disease.

## \|\| 2.88 \|\|

*śiro 'rtipramukhā rogā yānti viśvāsaghātakam \|*  
*liṅgapīḍī śivasvaṃ ca śivanirmālyameva ca \|\| 88 \|\|*

88\. A treacherous fellow suffers from headache. He who is against Shiva suffers from the disease of genital organ.

## \|\| 2.89 \|\|

*striyo 'pyanena mārgeṇa hṛtvā doṣamavāpnuyuḥ \|*  
*eteṣāmeva jantūnāṃ bhāryātvamupajāyate \|\| 89 \|\|*

89\. And the women too who commit these sins suffer in the similar way or they become wives of persons suffering in the aforesaid manner.

## \|\| 2.90 \|\|

*bhogānte narakasyaitatsarvamityavadhāraya \|*  
*khagha pradarśyametattu mayoktaṃ te samāsataḥ \|*  
*dravyaprakārā hi yathā tathaiva prāṇijātayaḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

90\. After their present life they reach hell. Know that I have told you all this in short, О bird! Just as there are many kinds of disease so also there are many kinds of man.

## \|\| 2.91 \|\|

*evaṃ vicitrairnijakarmabhirnṛṇāṃ sukhasya duḥ khasya ca janmanāmapi \|*  
*vaicitryamuktaṃ śubhakarmataḥ śubhaṃ tathāśubhāccāśubhamīrayanti \|\| 91 \|\|*

91\. In this way, due to the variety of their actions, men attain happiness or grief. Thus, I have told you about the strangeness of human nature. Good actions give good results and bad actions give bad results.

## \|\| 2.92 \|\|

*etatte sarvaṃmākhyātaṃ yatpṛṣṭo 'hamiha tvayā \|\| 92 \|\|*

92\. In this way your queries are answered completely.

## Chapter 3

## \|\| 3.1 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*evamutsāhitaḥ pakṣī svarūpaṃ nirayasya tu \|*  
*papracchanarakāṇyevaṃ śrutvā cotkūlitāntaraḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Thus encouraged, Garuda asked about the О Lord, tell me about the nature and description of Hells, being curious to know division of hells where the evil-doers are about their nature and function.

## \|\| 3.2 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*narakāṇāṃ svarūpaṃ me vada yeṣu vikarmiṇaḥ \|*  
*pātyante duḥ khabhūyiṣṭhāsteṣāṃ bhedāṃśca kīrtaya \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. О Lord, tell me about the nature and division of hells where the evil-doers are thrown mercilessly.

## \|\| 3.3 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*narakāṇāṃ sahasrāṇi vartante hyaruṇānuja \|*  
*śakyaṃ vistarato naiva vaktuṃ prādhānyato bruve \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Bhagawan said: — О younger brother of Aruna, there are thousands of hells. It is not possible to describe all of them in detail. I shall tell you about the principal hells thoroughly.

## \|\| 3.4 \|\|

*rauravaṃ nāma narakaṃ mukhyaṃ tadvainibodha me \|*  
*raurave kūṭasākṣī tu yāti yaścānṛtī naraḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The principal hell is Raurava. The person who tells lies and gives false evidence goes to this hell.

## \|\| 3.5 \|\|

*yojanānāṃ sahasre dve rauravo hi pramāṇataḥ \|*  
*jānumātrapramāṇaṃ tu tatra gartaṃ sudustaram \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Raurava is two thousand yojanas in measurement. There is a knee-deep pit, difficult to cross.

## \|\| 3.6 \|\|

*tatrāṅgāracayaughena kṛtaṃ taddharaṇīsamam \|*  
*tatrāgninā sutīvreṇa tāpitāṅgārabhūminā \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. It is levelled upto the ground by a heap of burning coal. And the ground there is wellheated by fire.

## \|\| 3.7 \|\|

*tanmadhye pāpakarmāṇaṃ vimuñcanti yamānugāḥ \|*  
*sa dahyamānastīvreṇa vahninā paridhāvati \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. The servants of Yama leave the sinner there in the middle. Burnt by burning fire he runs helter-skelter, vkqk?

## \|\| 3.8 \|\|

*padepade ca pādo 'sya sphuṭyate śīryate punaḥ \|*  
*ahorātreṇoddharaṇaṃ pādanyāsena gacchati \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. At every step his foot is burnt. Day and night he moves here and there lifting and putting his feet.

## \|\| 3.9 \|\|

*evaṃ sahasraṃ vistīrṇaṃ yojanānāṃ vimucyate \|*  
*tato 'nyatpāpaśuddhyarthaṃ tādṛṅnirayamṛcchati \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. There he is left along a thousand Yojanas. Then for the purgation of remaining sins he goes to other hells.

## \|\| 3.10 \|\|

*rauravaste samākhyātaḥ prathamo narako mayā \|*  
*mahārauravasaṃjñaṃ tu śṛṇuṣva narakaṃ khaga \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Thus I have told you about the first hell named Raurava. Now, О bird, hear about the hell called Maharaurava.

## \|\| 3.11 \|\|

*yojanānāṃ sahasrāṇi santi pañca samantataḥ \|*  
*tatra tāmramayī bhūmiradhastasyā hutāśanaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. It is five thousand Yojanas in measurement. It has copper-coloured earth scorched below.

## \|\| 3.12 \|\|

*tayā tapantyā sā sarvā prodyadvidyutsamaprabhā \|*  
*vibhāvyate mahāraudrā pāpināṃ darśanādiṣu \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. When heated by it, the earth shines like lightning. It appears extremely fierce to the sinner.

## \|\| 3.13 \|\|

*tasyāṃ baddhakarābhyāṃ ca padbhyāṃ caiva yamānugaiḥ \|*  
*mucyate pāpakṛnmadhye luṇṭhamānaḥ sa gacchati \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. The servants of Yama tie hands and feet of die sinner and throw him in this hell where he goes down, rolling.

## \|\| 3.14 \|\|

*kākairbakairvṛkolūkairmaśakairvṛścikaistathā \|*  
*bhakṣyamāṇaistathā raudrairgato mārge vikṛṣyate \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. While rolling down in the way he is bit by fearful crows, cranes, ants, mosquitoes and scorpions.

## \|\| 3.15 \|\|

*dahyamāno gatamatirbhrāntastāteti cākulaḥ \|*  
*vadatyasakṛdudvagno na śāntimadhigacchati \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Thus being burnt, he loses his wits and cries restlessly, 'O father, О father; again and again, but attains no peace.

## \|\| 3.16 \|\|

*evaṃ tasmānnarairmokṣastvatikrāntairavāpyate \|*  
*varṣāyutāyutaiḥ pāpaṃ yaiḥ kṛtaṃ duṣṭabuddhibhiḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. The persons who have committed sins with bad intentions are thrown into this hell and get release from there only from myriads of years.

## \|\| 3.17 \|\|

*tathānyastu tato nāma so 'tiśītaḥ svabhāvataḥ \|*  
*mahārauravavaddīrghastathāndhatamasā vṛtaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Then there is another hell by name and nature Atisita (very cold). It is as big as Maharaurava and full of dense darkness.

## \|\| 3.18 \|\|

*śītārtāstatra badhyante narāstamasi dāruṇe \|*  
*parasparaṃ samāsādya parirabhyāśrayanti te \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. There in the fierce darkness, the sinners are tied and left, suffering from cold. Meeting each other they clasp lightly.

## \|\| 3.19 \|\|

*dantāsteṣāṃ ca bhajyante śītārtiparikampitāḥ \|*  
*kṣutṛṣātibalāḥ pakṣinnatha tatrāpyupadavāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Shivering with cold their teeth sound loudly. Even there the overpowering trouble of hunger and thirst exists, О Garuda.

## \|\| 3.20 \|\|

*himakhaṇḍavaho vāyubhinattyasthīni dāruṇaḥ \|*  
*majjāsṛgasthigalitamaśrantyatra kṣudhānvitāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. The fierce wind blowing over the blocks of ice pierces the bones. The hungry men consume decayed marrow, blood and bones.

## \|\| 3.21-22 \|\|

*āliṅgyamānā bhrāmyante parasparasamāgame \|*  
*evaṃ tatrāpi sumahānkleśastamasi mānavaiḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

*prāpyate śakuniśreṣṭha yo bahūkṛtasañcayaḥ \|*  
*nikṛntana iti khyātastato 'nyo narakottamaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. Meeting together and clasping each other they wander here and there. The persons who had been hoarding wealth suffer too much in that darkness, О the best of birds. Then there is another hell called Nikrntana.

## \|\| 3.23 \|\|

*kulālacakrāṇi tatra bhrāmyantyavirataṃ khaga \|*  
*teṣvāpāṣye nikṛṣyante kālasūtreṇa mānavāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. A series of potter's wheel is moving round and round there, О bird. There the sinners, whose feet and head are tied by a string are held by Yama's servants in their fingers.

## \|\| 3.24-25 \|\|

*yamānumāṅgulisthena āpādatalamastakam \|*  
*na caiṣāṃ jīvitabhraṃśo jāyate pakṣisattama \|\| 24 \|\|*

*chinnāni teṣāṃ śataśaḥ khaṇḍānyaikyaṃ vrajanti hi \|*  
*evaṃ varṣasahasrāṇi bhrāmyante pāpakarmiṇaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

24-25. О bird, their life is not destroyed but their organs are cut into hundred pieces and restored again to their original position. Thus for thousands of years, the sinners rotate till their sins are exhausted completely.

## \|\| 3.26-27 \|\|

*tāvadyāvadaśeṣaṃ ca tatpāpaṃ saṃkṣayaṃ gatam \|*  
*aprātaṣṭhaṃ ca narakaṃ śṛṇuṣva gadato mama \|\| 26 \|\|*

*tatrasthairnārakairduḥ khamasahyamanubhūyate \|*  
*tānyeva tatra cakrāṇi ghaṭīyantrāṇi cānyataḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

26-27. Now hear, I shall tell you about the hell named Apratistha, The people who go to this hell suffer intensely, There too the potter's wheels and the ghati-yantra operate constantly.

## \|\| 3.28 \|\|

*duḥ khasya hetubhūtāni pāpakarmakṛtāṃ nṛṇām \|*  
*cakreṣvāropitāḥ kecidbhāmyante tatra mānavāḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Those sinners who had caused untold sufferings to men are placed there on the potter's wheels and rotate incessantly.

## \|\| 3.29 \|\|

*yāvadvarṣasahasrāṇi na teṣāṃ sthitirantarā \|*  
*ghaṭīyantreṣu badvā ye baddhā toyavaṭī yathā \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. They remain there for thousands of years, tied as they are to ghati-yantra like the water- pots.

## \|\| 3.30 \|\|

*bhrāmyante mānavā raktamudgirantaḥ punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*antrairmukhaviniṣkrāntairnetrairantrāvalambibhaiḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. The sinners rotate there vomitting blood again and again. Their intestines come out through mouth and their eyes are suspended by intestines.

## \|\| 3.31 \|\|

*duḥ khāni prāpnuvantīha yānyasahyāni jantubhiḥ \|*  
*asipatravanaṃ nāma narakaṃ śṛṇu cāparam \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Now hear about another hell named Asipatravana, where the sinners suffer unbearable miseries.

## \|\| 3.32-33 \|\|

*yojanānāṃ sahasraṃ yo jvalatyagnyāśṛtāvaniḥ \|*  
*saptatīvrakarāścaiṇḍaryatra tīvra sudāruṇe \|\| 32 \|\|*

*pratapanti sadā tatra prāṇino narakaukasaḥ \|*  
*tanmadhye caraṇaṃ śītasnigdhapatraṃ vibhāṣyate \|\| 33 \|\|*

32-33. There over a thousand Yojanas a fierce, fire burns with seven fierce rays. The sinners thrown in this hell feel burnt without recess. In the midst is seen a tree with a big trunk, overgrown with cool and smooth leaves which are torn here and there, О Garuda.

## \|\| 3.34-36 \|\|

*patrāṇi yatra khaṇḍāni phalānāṃ pakṣisattama \|*  
*śvānaśca tatra subalāścarantyāmiṣabhojanāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

*mahāvaktrā mahādaṃṣṭrā vyāghrā iva mahābalāḥ \|*  
*tataśca vanamālokya śiśiracchāyamagrataḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

*prayānti prāṇinastatra kṣuttāpaparipīḍitāḥ \|*  
*mātarbhrātastāta iti krandamānāḥ suduḥ khitāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

34-36. There powerful dogs roam about, always eager to eat meat. They have fierce jaws, fierce mouths and are powerful like tigers. Then seeing in front, the forest with cool shade, the miserable sinners suffer from hunger and heat, run to it, crying 'O mother, О brother, О father.'

## \|\| 3.37-38 \|\|

*dahyamānāṅghriyugalā dharaṇisthena vahninā \|*  
*teṣāṃ gatānāṃ tatrāpi ati śītiḥ samīraṇaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

*pravāti tena pātyante teṣāṃ khaḍgāstathopari \|*  
*chinnāḥ patanti te bhūmau jvalatpāvakasaṃcaye \|\| 38 \|\|*

37-38. Then their feet burn by the ground-fire. Still then, a very cool wind blows forcefully which makes those sharp blades fall upon them. Cut into pieces they fall in the burning fire.

## \|\| 3.39-40 \|\|

*lelihyamāne cānyatra taptāśeṣamahītale \|*  
*sārameyāśca te śīghraṃ śātayanti śarīrataḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

*teṣāṃ khaṇḍānyanekāni rudatāmatibhīṣaṇe \|*  
*asipatravanaṃ tāta mayaitatparikīrtitam \|\| 40 \|\|*

39-40. Then the licking dogs tear their bodies into pieces even as they weep woefully. Thus I have told you about the Asipatravana.

## \|\| 3.41 \|\|

*ataḥ paraṃ bhīmataraṃ taptakuṃbhaṃ nibodha me \|*  
*samantatastaptakumbhā vahnijvālāsamanvitāḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Now hear about a more fierce hell called Tapta-kumbha, where all around are kept heated pitchers full of burning flames.

## \|\| 3.42 \|\|

*jvaladagnicayāstaptatailāyaścūrṇapūritāḥ \|*  
*eṣu duṣkṛtakarmāṇo yāmyaiḥ kṣiptā hyadhomukhāḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The pitchers are full of burning fire, hot oil and iron filings. Therein arc thrown the sinners by the attendants of Yama.

## \|\| 3.43 \|\|

*kvāthyante visphuṭadgātrā galanmajjājalānvitāḥ \|*  
*sphuṭatkapālenatrāsthicchidyamānā vibhīṣaṇaiḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. They (the attendants of Yama) boil their bodies disintegrating, marrow flowing out, heads, eyes and bones breaking.

## \|\| 3.44 \|\|

*gṛdhrairutpāṭya mucyante punasteṣveva vegitaiḥ \|*  
*punaścimacimāyante tailanaikyaṃ vrajanti ca \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Fierce vultures tear them taking them out and again throwing them in. They boil there till they are merged into oil.

## \|\| 3.45 \|\|

*dravībhūtaiḥ śirogātraiḥ snāyumāṃsatvagāsthibhiḥ \|*  
*tato yāmyairnarairāśu darvyāghaṭṭanaghaṭṭitāḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. When their head, limbs, sinews, flesh, skin and bones are liquefied, then Yama's servants stir them with a ladle.

## \|\| 3.46-47 \|\|

*kṛtāvarte mahātaile kvāthyante pāpakarmiṇaḥ \|*  
*eṣa te vistareṇoktastaptakumbho mayā khaga \|\| 46 \|\|*

*ādimo rauravaḥ prokto mahārauravako 'paraḥ \|*  
*atiśītastṛtīyastu caturtho hi nikṛntanaḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

46-47. The sinners are boiled in oil in Yama's abode. Thus I have narrated to you in detail about Tapta-Kumbha, О bird. So the first hell is Raurava, the second Mated-raurava, the third Atisita, the fourth Niskrntana, the fifth Apratistha and the sixth Asitapatravana and the seventh Taptakwnbha. These are the seven hells.

## \|\| 3.48 \|\|

*apratiṣṭhaḥ pañcamaḥ syādasipatravano 'paraḥ \|*  
*saptamastaptakumbhastu saptaite narakā matāḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. We hear about some other hells also where the sinners fall according to their sins.

## \|\| 3.50-53 \|\|

*tathā hi narako rodhaḥ śūkarastāla eva ca \|*  
*taptakumbho mahājvālaḥ śabalo 'tha vimohanaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

*krimiśca krimabhakṣaśca lālābhakṣo viṣañjanaḥ \|*  
*adhaḥ śirāḥ pūyavaho rudhirāndhaśca viḍrabhujaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

*tathā vaitaraṇī sū (mū) masipatravanaṃ tathā \|*  
*agnijvālo mahāghoraḥ sandaṃśo vāpyabhojanaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

*tamaśca kālasūtraṃ ca lohaścāpyabhidastathā \|*  
*apratiṣṭho 'pyavīciśca narakā evamādayaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

50-53. They are Rodha, SUkara, Tala, Taptakumbha, Maha-jvala, Sabala, Vimohana, Krmi, Krmibhaksa, Ldldbhaksa, Visanjana, Adhahsiras, Puyavaha, Rudhirdndha, Vidbhuja, Vaitarani, Suma, Asipatravana, Agnijvala, Mahaghora, Sandamsa, Abhojana, Tamas, Kalasutra. Loha, Abhida, Apratistha, Avici.

## \|\| 3.54 \|\|

*tāmasā narakāḥ sarve yamasya viṣaye sthitāḥ \|*  
*yeṣu duṣkṛtakarmāṇaḥ patanti hi pṛthakpṛthak \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. These dark hells are situated in Yama's country. Herein fall the sinners in the order of their sins.

## \|\| 3.55 \|\|

*bhūmeradhastātte sarve rauravādyāḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|*  
*rogho goghno bhrūṇahā ca agnidātā naraḥ patet \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. These hells, Raurava, etc., lie below the earth. Now hear about the sins which drive the sinners to particular hells.

## \|\| 3.56 \|\|

*sūkare brahmahā majjetsurāpaḥ svarṇataskaraḥ \|*  
*tāle patetkṣattrahantā hatvā vaiśyaṃ ca durgatiḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. A man who kills a cow, a foetus or indulges in arson, falls in Rodha. A killer of Brahmana falls in Sukara, So also a drunkard, a stealer of gold, a killer of Ksatriya or Vaisya falls in Tala.

## \|\| 3.57 \|\|

*brahmahatyāṃ ca yaḥ kuryādyaśca syādgurutalpagaḥ \|*  
*svasṛgāmī taptakumbhī tathā rājabhaṭo 'nṛtī \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. He who kills a Brahmana or defiles his teacher's bed or has sexual union with his sister falls in Taptakumbha. So also a soldier who speaks lies.

## \|\| 3.58 \|\|

*taptalohaiśca vikretā tathā bandhanarakṣitā \|*  
*mādhvī vikrayakartāṃ ca yastu bhaktaṃ parityajet \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. In the similar way, he who sells liquor or he who discards a devotee also falls there.

## \|\| 3.59 \|\|

*mahājvālī duhitaraṃ snuṣāṃ gacchati yastu vai \|*  
*vedo vikrīyate yaiśca vedaṃ dūṣayate tu yaḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. He who co-habits with his daughter or daughter-in-law, he who sells Veda, or he who blames the Vedas falls into Mahajvala.

## \|\| 3.60 \|\|

*guruṃ caivāvamanyante vākśaraistāḍayanti ca \|*  
*agamyāgāmī ca naro narakaṃ śabalaṃ vrajet \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. He who insults his teacher or abuses him or he who co-habits with an unworthy woman falls into Gabala.

## \|\| 3.61 \|\|

*vimohe patate śūre maryādāṃ yo bhinatti vai \|*  
*duriṣṭaṃ kurute yastu kṛmibhakṣaṃ prapadyate \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. He who transgresses codes of moral conduct prescribed for the conduct of war falls into Vimohana. He who does an undesirable act falls into Krmibhaksa.

## \|\| 3.62-63 \|\|

*devabrāhmaṇavidveṣṭā lālābhakṣe patatyapi \|*  
*kuṇḍakartā kulālaśca nyāsahartā cikitsakaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

*ārāmeṣvagnidātā ca ete yānti viṣañjane \|*  
*asatpratigrahī yastu tathaivāyājyayājakaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

62-63. He who holds no honour for deities or the twice-born brahmanas falls into Lalabhaksa. A potter who digs pits or a physician who flouts trust or a person who sets fire to parks, falls into Visanjana. So also a brahmana who receives prohibited gifts or performs sacrifice for a wrong person.

## \|\| 3.64-65 \|\|

*na kṣattrairjovate yastu naro gacchedadhomukham \|*  
*kṣīraṃ surāṃ ca māsaṃ lākṣāṃ gandhaṃ rasaṃ tilān \|\| 64 \|\|*

*evamādīni vikrīṇan ghore pūyavahe patet \|*  
*yaḥ kukkuṭānnibadhnāti mārjārān sūkarāṃśca tān \|*  
*pakṣiṇaśca mṛgāṃśchā gānso 'pyevaṃ narakaṃ vrajet \|\| 65 \|\|*

64-65. Or a Ksatriya who does not live by valour falls into Adhomukha. He who sells milk, liquor, meat or cuts wood pr deals in scents, juices,, gingelly seeds, falls into fierce Puyavaha. He who catches cocks, eats pigs, birds, deer and goats falls into this hell.

## \|\| 3.66-67 \|\|

*ājāviko māhiṣikastathā cakrī dhvajī ca yaḥ \|*  
*raṅgopajīviko vipraḥ śākunirgrāmayājakaḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

*agāradāhī garadaḥ kuṇḍāśī somavikrayī \|*  
*surāpo māṃsabhakṣī ca tathā ca paśughātakaḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

66-67. He who deals in buffaloes, or a juggler or he who sells liquor, an actor, or Brahmana who is a bird-hunter or a village-priest who indulges in arson or he who administers poison or he who is a pump or he who sells soma, or a drunkard, a meat-eater or a killer of animals

## \|\| 3.68 \|\|

*rudhirāndhe patantyete patantyete evamāhurmanīṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*upaviṣṭantvekapaṅktyāṃ viṣaṃ sambhojayanti ye \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. All these fall into Rudhirandha. Those who administer poison to their guests, sitting in a row, fall into the fierce hell Vidbhuj. There is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 3.69 \|\|

*patanti niraye ghore viḍbhuje nātra saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*madhugrāho vaitaraṇīmākrośī mūtrasaṃjñake \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. He who drinks wine falls into Vaitaranl. He who calls others by bad names falls into Mutra.

## \|\| 3.70 \|\|

*asipatravane 'saucī krodhanaśca etedapi \|*  
*agnijvālāṃ mṛgavyādho bhojyate yatra vāyasaiḥ \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. He who has not lived a pious life falls into Asita-patravana. So also he who is hottem- pered. A deer-hunter falls into Agnijvala where he is eaten by the crows.

## \|\| 3.71 \|\|

*ijyāyāṃ vratalopācca sandaṃśe narake patet \|*  
*skandante yadi vā svapne yatino brahmacāriṇaḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

71\. The person who breaks his vow during the course of sacrifice falls into Sandamsa. So also Brahmacarins who discharge their semen in dream.

## \|\| 3.72 \|\|

*putrairadhyāpitā ye ca putrairājñāpitāśca ye \|*  
*te sarve narakaṃ yānti nirayaṃ cāpyabhojanam \|\| 72 \|\|*

72\. The fathers who are taught by their sons or who receive orders from them fall into Abhojana.

## \|\| 3.73 \|\|

*varṇāśramaviruddhāni krodhaharṣasamanvitāḥ \|*  
*karmāṇi ye tu kurvanti sarve nirayavāsinaḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

73\. Those who perform acts violating the rules of their Varna and Asrama, on account of anger or pleasure go to hell.

## \|\| 3.74 \|\|

*upariṣṭātsthito ghora uṣṇātmā rauravo mahān \|*  
*sudāruṇaḥ suśītātmā tasyā dhastāmasaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

74\. There is the hot Raurava above and the cold Tamasa below.

## \|\| 3.75 \|\|

*evamādikrameṇaiva sarve 'dho 'dhaḥ paristhitāḥ \|*  
*duḥ khotkarṣaśca sarveṣu karmasvapi nimittataḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

75\. In this way, the hells are situated one below the other. The climax of misery is due to bad acts.

## \|\| 3.76 \|\|

*sukhotkarṣaśca sarvatra dharmasyehanimitataḥ \|*  
*paśyantinarakāndevā hyadhovaktrānsudāruṇān \|\| 76 \|\|*

76\. And the climax of joy accrues from good acts. The gods look down and see the fierce hells.

## \|\| 3.77 \|\|

*nārakāścāpi te devānsarvānpaśyanti ūrdhvagān \|*  
*etānyānyāni śataśo narakāṇi viyadgate \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. The sinners of hell also look up and see the gods above. There are thus hundreds of hells, О bird.

## \|\| 3.78-79 \|\|

*dinedine tu narake pacyate dahyatenyataḥ \|*  
*śīryate bhidyate 'nyatra cūryate klidyatenyataḥ \|\| 78 \|\|*

*kvathyate dīpyate 'nyatra tathā vātahato 'nyataḥ \|*  
*ekaṃ dinaṃ varṣaśatapramāṇaṃ narake bhavet \|\| 79 \|\|*

78-79. Daily in the hell, the sinners are cooked, burnt, torn, broken, pounded, moistened,boiled, heated and blown by winds. A day in hell is equal to one hundred years of the mortal.

## \|\| 3.80-82 \|\|

*tataḥ sarveṣu nistīrṇaḥ pāpī tiryaktvamaśnate \|*  
*kṛmikīṭapataṅgeṣu sthāvaraikaśapheṣu ca \|\| 80 \|\|*

*gatvā vanagajāḍhyeṣu goṣvaṣu tathaiva ca \|*  
*kharo 'śvo 'śvataro gauraḥ śarabhaścamarī tathā \|\| 81 \|\|*

*ete caikaśaphāḥ ṣaṭ ca śṛṇu pañcanakhānataḥ \|*  
*anyāsu bahupāpāsu duḥ khadāsu ca yo niṣu \|\| 82 \|\|*

80-82. Having crossed these, the sinner is reborn as a worm, a germ, a fly, a one-hoofed creature, a wild elephant, a cow, an ass, a horse, a. mule, a buffalo, sarabha, a camari, a six-hoofed animal or one having five nails. In these and other yonis he takes birth.

## \|\| 3.83 \|\|

*mānuṣyaṃ prāpyate kubjo kutsito vāmano 'pi vā \|*  
*caṇḍālapukkasādyāsu narayoniṣu jāyate \|\| 83 \|\|*

83\. If born as a human being he becomes hunchbacked or a dwarf or a candala in wretched yonis.

## \|\| 3.84 \|\|

*muhurgarbhe vasantyeva mriyante ca muhurmuhuḥ \|*  
*avaśiṣṭena pāpena puṇyena ca samanvitaḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

84\. The sinner is born again and again and dies again and again till he has exhausted his sin and acquired virtue.

## \|\| 3.85 \|\|

*tataścārohiṇīṃ yoniṃ śūdravaiśyanṛpādikam \|*  
*vipradevendratāṃ cāpi kadācidadhirohati \|\| 85 \|\|*

85\. Then some time he steps into the yoni of &udra or Vaigya or Ksatriya or Brahmana or a deity.

## \|\| 3.86 \|\|

*evaṃ tu pāpakarmāṇo nirayeṣu patantyadhaḥ \|*  
*yathā puṇyakṛto yānti tanme nigadataḥ śṛṇu \|\| 86 \|\|*

86\. Thus I have told you how a sinner falls into hell. Now I shall tell you how and where the virtuous go after death.

## \|\| 3.87 \|\|

*te yamena vinirdiṣṭāṃ yoniṃ puṇyagatiṃ narāḥ \|*  
*pragītagandharvagaṇā nṛtyotsavasamākulāḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

87\. The virtuous obtain good yonis as directed by Yama. Immediately after the soul leaves the body, the Gandharvas come singing and dancing, adorned with garlands and anklets.

## \|\| 3.88 \|\|

*hāranūpuramādhuryaiḥ śobhitānyamalāni tu \|*  
*prayāntyāśu vimānāni divyagandhasragujjvalāḥ \|\| 88 \|\|*

88\. And then appear splendid aerial cars decorated with sweet smelling garlands (which take the virtuous souls to heaven).

## \|\| 3.89-90 \|\|

*tasmācca pracyutā rājñāmanyeṣāṃ ca mahātmanām \|*  
*jāyante nīrujāṃ gehe sa dvṛttipīrapālakāḥ \|\| 89 \|\|*

*bhogānsamprāpnuvantyugrāṃstato yāntyūrdhvamanyathā \|*  
*avarohiṇīṃ samprāpya pūrvavadyanti mānavāḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

89-90. When their merits are exhausted, the virtuous souls fall from heaven and are born in the houses of kings or nobles of illustrious character, where they enjoy various pleasures. Thus men go up and down the ladder as stated before.

## \|\| 3.91 \|\|

*jātasya mṛtyuloke vai prāṇino maraṇaṃ dhruvam \|*  
*pāpiṣṭhānāmadomārgājjīvo niṣkramate dhruvam \|\| 91 \|\|*

91\. Death is certain for the mortals who are born on this earth. The soul of the sinner leaves the body from the downward path, undoubtedly.

## \|\| 3.92 \|\|

*pṛthivyāṃ līyate pṛthvī āpaścaiva tathāpsu ca \|*  
*tejastejasi līyate samīre ca samīraṇaḥ \|\| 92 \|\|*

92\. Earth mingles with earth, water with water, fire with fire, air with air.

## \|\| 3.93 \|\|

*ākāśe ca tathākāśaṃ sarvavyāpi niśākare \|*  
*tatra kāmastathā krodhaḥ kāye pañcendriyāṇi ca \|\| 93 \|\|*

93\. Sky mingles with sky, pervading all around. Itt the body there esist love, anger and five organs,

## \|\| 3.94 \|\|

*ete tārkṣya samākhyātā dehe tiṣṭhanti taskarāḥ \|*  
*kāmaḥ krodho hyahaṅkāro manastatraiva nāyakaḥ \|\| 94 \|\|*

94\. О Garuda, there abide thieves in the body in the form of love, anger and pride. Mind is the leader of all these.

## \|\| 3.95 \|\|

*saṃhāraścaiva kālo 'sau puṇyapāpena saṃyutaḥ \|*  
*pañcendriyasamāyuktaḥ sakalairvibudhaiḥ saha \|\| 95 \|\|*

95\. Death is temporal. The jlva along with sins, virtues and five subtle organs enters into new body just as a householder enters into a new house when the old one is burnt.

## \|\| 3.96-98 \|\|

*praviśetsa nave dehe pṛhe daradhe guhī yathā \|*  
*śarīre ye samāsīnāḥ sarve vai sapta dhātavaḥ \|\| 96 \|\|*

*ṣāṭkauśiko hyayaṃ kāyaḥ sarve vātāśca dehinām \|*  
*mūtraṃ purīṣaṃ tadyogādye cānye vyādhayastathā \|\| 97 \|\|*

*pittaṃ śleṣmā tathā majjā māṃsaṃ vai meda eva ca \|*  
*asthi śukraṃ tathā snāyurdehena saha dahyati \|\| 98 \|\|*

96-98. In the body exist seven elements and seven Kosas. All these exist in the bodyurine, waste, allied matter, bile, cough, marrow, flesh, fat, bones, semen, sinews which are destroyed, along with the body (when the soul departs).

## \|\| 3.99 \|\|

*eṣa te kathitastārkṣya vināsaḥ sarvadehinam \|*  
*kathayāmi punasteṣāṃ śarīraṃ ca yathā bhavet \|\| 99 \|\|*

99\. Thus I have told you, О bird, about the end of all bodies. Now I shall tell you, О bird, how their body looks like.

## \|\| 3.100-102 \|\|

*ekastambaṃsnāyubaddhaṃ sthūṇātrayavibhūṣaṇam \|*  
*indriyaiśca samāyuktaṃ navadvāraṃ śarīrakam \|\| 100 \|\|*

*viṣayaiśca samākrāntaṃ kāmakrodhasamākulam \|*  
*rāgadveṣasamākīrṇaṃ tṛṣṇādurgamataskaram \|\| 101 \|\|*

*lobhajālaparicchinnaṃ mohavastreṇa veṣṭitam \|*  
*subaddhaṃ māyayā caitallobhenādhiṣṭhitaṃ puram \|\| 102 \|\|*

100-102. It is a shrub of sinews adorned with three trunks, combined with organs and having nine openings. Full of sensual pleasures, love, anger, desire and envy, possessing a high way robber in the form of greed.

## \|\| 3.103 \|\|

*etadguṇasamākīrṇaṃ śarīraṃ sarvadehinām \|*  
*ātmānaṃ ye na jānanti te narāḥ paśavaḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 103 \|\|*

103\. Caught in the net of avarice and covered by the cloth of affection. It is bound by illusion and inhabited by greed. These qualities inhere in the bodies of all creatures. Those who do not realize their self are nothing more than beasts.

## \|\| 3.104 \|\|

*evameva samākhyātaṃ śarīraṃ te caturvidham \|*  
*caturaśītilakṣāṇi nirmitā yonayaḥ purā \|\| 104 \|\|*

104\. Thus I have told you about the fourfold body. Formerly, eightyfour yonis were created \[for the jlva to pass through\].

## \|\| 3.105 \|\|

*udbhijjāḥ svedajāścaiva aṇḍajāśca jarāyujāḥ \|*  
*etatte sarvamākhyātaṃ nirayasya prapañcataḥ \|\| 105 \|\|*

105\. While narrating about the hell I told you about all these-that are born from the earth, from the sweat, from 'the eggs and from the I shall tell you more as and when neccessary embryo. or do you possess a desire to ask more?

## Chapter 4

## \|\| 4.1 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*jñānato 'jñānato vāpiyannaraiḥ kaluṣaṃ kṛtam \|*  
*tasya pāpasya śuddhyarthaṃ vidheyā niṣkṛtirnaraiḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Krsna said: — Whatever sin a man commits consciously or unconsciously stands in need of purification by means of atonement.

## \|\| 4.2 \|\|

*bhasmādisnānadaśakamādau kuryādvicakṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*yathāśakti ṣaḍabdādipratyāmnāyāccaredapi \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The wise one should first perform ten types of baths beginning with bhasma. These should be observed, as far as possible for six years repeatedly.

## \|\| 4.3 \|\|

*tadardhaṃ vā tadardhaṃ vā tadardhārdhamathāpi vā \|*  
*yathāśaktyā tataḥ kuryāddaśa dānāni vai śṛṇu \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Or half of that or half of that or half of the half of that. Then as far as he can he should donate ten gifts. Now hear about them.

## \|\| 4.4 \|\|

*gobhūtilahiraṇyājyavāsodhanyaguḍāstathā \|*  
*rajataṃ lavaṇaṃ caiva dānāni daśa vai viduḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Cow, earth, gingelly seeds, gold, butter, cloth, grains, sugar, silver and salt — these are known as ten gifts.

## \|\| 4.5-6 \|\|

*prāyaścitte tvāgatā ye tebhyo dadyānnaro daśa \|*  
*tato yamadvārapathe pūyaśoṇitasaṃkule \|\| 5 \|\|*

*nadīṃ vaitaraṇīṃ tartuṃ dadyādvaitaraṇīṃ ca gām \|*  
*kṛṣṇastanī sakṛṣṇāṅgī sā vai vaitaraṇī smṛtā \|\| 6 \|\|*

5-6. He should make these gifts to those who have come during atonement. Then to cross the river Vaitarani full of pus and blood, at Yama's door he should donate the Vaitarani cow. A black cow wuh black udders is called Vaitarani.

## \|\| 4.7-8 \|\|

*tilā lohaṃ hiraṇyaṃ ca karpāsaṃ lavaṇaṃ tathā \|*  
*saptadhānyaṃ kṣitirgāva ekaikaṃ pāvanaṃ smṛtam \|\| 7 \|\|*

*etānyaṣṭau mahādānānyuttamāya dvijātaye \|*  
*ātureṇa tu deyāni padarūpāṇi me śṛṇu \|\| 8 \|\|*

7-8. Gingelly seeds, iron, gold, cotton, salt, seven grains, earth and cow-these are all pure. These eight precious gifts should be given to a learned brahmana by the dying person. I shall now tell you about the form of pada.

## \|\| 4.9 \|\|

*chatro pānahavastrāṇi mudrikā ca kamaṇḍaluḥ \|*  
*āsanaṃ bhājanaṃ padaṃ cāṣṭavidhaṃ smṛtam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Umbrella, shoes, clothes, ring, gourd, seat, vessel and food-these are the eight kinds of pada.

## \|\| 4.10 \|\|

*tilāpātraṃ sarpiḥ pātraṃ śayyā sopaskarā tathā \|*  
*etatsarvaṃ pradātavyaṃ yadiṣṭaṃ cātmano 'pi tat \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. A vessel full of gingelly seeds or butter arid a bed with all its epuipment should be gifted or all those articles which are liked by the donor.

## \|\| 4.11 \|\|

*aśvo rathaśca mahīṣī vyañjanaṃ vastrameva ca \|*  
*brāhmaṇebhyaḥ pradātavyaṃ brahmapūrvamapi svayam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Horse, chariot, she-buffalo, fan and clothall these should be given to the brahmanas,

## \|\| 4.12-14 \|\|

*dānā nyanyānyapi khaga tarpayetsvīyaśaktitaḥ \|*  
*prāyāścittaṃ kṛtaṃ yena daśa dānānyapi kṣitau \|\| 12 \|\|*

*dānaṃ gorvaitaraṇyāśca dānānyaṣṭau tathāpi vā \|*  
*tilapātraṃ sarpiḥ pātraṃ śayyādānaṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 13 \|\|*

*padadānaṃ ca vidhivannāsau nirayagarbhagaḥ \|*  
*svātantryeṇāpi lavaṇadānamicchanti sūrayaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

12-14. О Lord, these gift and others should be givenas far as possible. He who has done atonement, given ten gifts on this earth, or the gift of Vaitarani cow or eight gifts or the gifts of a vessel full of gingelly seeds or clarified butter or the gift of bed or of pada, as prescribed in the Shastras does not go hell. The wise have prescribed the gift of salt to be given freely.

## \|\| 4.15 \|\|

*viṣṇudehasamutpanno yato 'yaṃ lavaṇo rasaḥ \|*  
*āturasya yadā prāṇā na yānti vasudhātale \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. The salt is produced from the body of Visnu. When the soul of the dying person does not leave the body but lingers on, the salt should be gifted as it opens the door of the heaven.

## \|\| 4.16 \|\|

*lavaṇaṃ ca tadā deyaṃ dvārasyodvāṭanaṃ divaḥ \|*  
*yānikāni ca dānāni svayaṃ dattāni mānavaiḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Whatever gifts a man has given himself they all stand in his favour (at the hour of death).

## \|\| 4.17 \|\|

*tānitāni ca sarvāṇi upatiṣṭhanti cāgrataḥ \|*  
*prāyaścittaṃ kṛtaṃ yena sāṅgaṃ khaga sa vai pumān \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. О bird, the man who has completed atonement in all its parts is placed in heaven, after all his sins are washed away.

## \|\| 4.18 \|\|

*pāpāni bhasmasātkṛtvā svargaloke mahīyate \|*  
*amṛtaṃ tu gavāṃ kṣīraṃ yataḥ patagasattam \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. О noble bird, since the cow's milk is nectar, whosoever gifts a cow attains immortality.

## \|\| 4.19 \|\|

*tasmāddadāti yo dhenumamṛtatvaṃ sa gacchati \|*  
*dānānyaṣṭau tu dattvā vai gandharvanilaye vaset \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Whosoever gives the- eight gifts lives in the abode ofthe Gandharvas.

## \|\| 4.20 \|\|

*ālayastatra raudre hi dahyate yena mānavaḥ \|*  
*chatradānena succhāyā jāyate pathi tuṣṭidā \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. He gets shelter even in the tortuous hell named. Raudra where the sinners are burnt. If he gifts an umbrella, he encounters the comforting shade in the way.

## \|\| 4.21 \|\|

*asipatravanaṃ dhāramatikrāmati vai sukham \|*  
*aśvārūḍhaśca vrajati dadate yadyupānahau \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. So also he passes comfortably through the fierce Asipatravana. If he gifts shoes he moves there too mounting a, horse.

## \|\| 4.22 \|\|

*bhojanāsanadānena sukhaṃ mārge bhunakti vai \|*  
*pradeśe nirjale dātā sukhī syādvai kamaṇḍaloḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. If he gifts food and seat he eats sitting comfortably in the way. If he gifts a waterjar he feels comfortable even, in thfl waterless region.

## \|\| 4.23 \|\|

*yamadūtā mahāraudrāḥ karālāḥ kṛṣṇapiṅgalāḥ \|*  
*na pīḍayanti dākṣiṇyādvastrābharaṇadānataḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. He who gifts clothes and ornaments liberally is not tortured by the fierce messengers of Yama who are black and yellow in colour.

## \|\| 4.24 \|\|

*tilapātraṃ tu viprāya dattaṃ patraratha dhruvam \|*  
*nāśayettrividhaṃ ṣāpaṃ vāṅmanaḥ kāyasambhavam \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. О bird, if a vessel full of gingelly seeds is donated to a Brahmana it destroys all the three types of sins born out of his voice, body and mind.

## \|\| 4.25-26 \|\|

*ghṛtapātrapradāne rudraloke vasennaraḥ \|*  
*sarvopaskarasaṃyuktāṃ śayyāṃ dattvā dvijātaye \|\| 25 \|\|*

*nānāpsarobhirākīrṇaṃ vimānamadhirohati \|*  
*ṣaṣṭivarṣasahasrāṇi krīḍitvā śakramandire \|\| 26 \|\|*

25-26. If he gifts a vessel full of clarified butter he stays, in Rudraloka. If he gifts abed along with all its equipment he rides an aerial car in the company of fairies and enjoys in the abode of lndra for sixty-thousand years.

## \|\| 4.27-28 \|\|

*indralokātparibhraṣṭa iha loke nṛpo bhavet \|*  
*sarvopaskaraṇopetaṃ yuvānaṃ doṣavarjitam \|\| 27 \|\|*

*yo 'śvaṃ dadāti viprāya svargaloke ca tiṣṭhati \|*  
*yāvanti romāṇi haye bhavanti hi khageśvara \|\| 28 \|\|*

27-28. After falling down from Indraloka he becomes a monarch in this world. He who gifts a young faultless horse along with all equipment to a Brahmana lives in heaven, О bird, for years, equal to the number of hair on the body of a horse.

## \|\| 4.29-30 \|\|

*tāvato rājitāṃllokānāpnuvanti hi puṣkalān \|*  
*caturbhisturagairyuktaṃ sarvopakaraṇairyutam \|\| 29 \|\|*

*rathaṃ dvijātaye dattvā rājasūyaphalaṃ labht \|\| 30 \|\|*

29-30. If he gifts a chariot drawn by four horses along with the contiguous equipment to a learned Brahmana he reaps, the benefit of performing a Rajasuya.

## \|\| 4.31 \|\|

*dugdhādhikāṃ ca mahiṣīṃ navameghavarṇāṃ santuṣṭatarṇakavalīṃ jaghanābhirāmām \|*  
*dattvā suvarṇatilakāṃ dvijapuṅgavāya lokodayaṃ sa jayatīti kimatra citram \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. If he donates to a Brahmana a milk yielding buffalo with her plump hind parts, and a golden ornament on her preached accom panied by her satiated calf, what wonder is there that he attains heaven.

## \|\| 4.32 \|\|

*tālavṛntasya dānena vāyunā vījyate pathi \|*  
*kāntiyuk subhagaḥ śrīmān bhavatyambaradānataḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. If he donates a fan (Talavrta), he is fanned by air in the way. If he donates cloth he becomes full of lustre, wealth and prosperity.

## \|\| 4.33 \|\|

*rasānnopaskarayutaṃ gṛhaṃ viprāya yor'payet \|*  
*na hīyate tasya vaṃśaḥ svargaṃ prāpnotyanuttamam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. If he donates ahorse along with salt, food and other ingredients to a Bramana, his family does not suffer a break and he goes to heaven after death.

## \|\| 4.34 \|\|

*bhavatyatra khagaśreṣṭha phalagaukhalāghavam \|*  
*śraddhāśraddhāvibhedena dānagauravalāghavāt \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. О bird, there is a difference in result according to-faith and gifts.

## \|\| 4.35 \|\|

*tato yenāmbudānāni kṛtānyatra rasāstathā \|*  
*tadā khaga tathāhlādamāpadi pratipadyate \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. He who donates water and salt gets enjoyment in. distress.

## \|\| 4.36 \|\|

*annāni yena dattāni śraddhāpūtena cetasā \|*  
*so 'pi tṛptimavāpnoti vināpyannena vai tadā \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. He who donates food with a heart purified by faith is satiated even without food.

## \|\| 4.37 \|\|

*āsanne maraṇe kuryātsaṃnyāsaṃ cedvidhānataḥ \|*  
*āvarteta punarnāsau brahmabhūyāya kalpate \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. If he accepts saihnyasa as prescribed in the sacred texts he is not reborn but is merged into Brahma itself.

## \|\| 4.38 \|\|

*āsannamaraṇo matyaścettīrthaṃ pratinayite \|*  
*tīrthaprāptau bhavenmuktirmriyate yadi mārgagaḥ \|*  
*padepade kratusamaṃ bhavettasya na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. If he dies at a sacred place he attains moksa (release from re-birth) after dying there. If he dies in the way, each and every step he has taken in reaching this place procures for him the fruit of performing a sacrifice. There is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 4.39 \|\|

*gṛhṇīyāccedanaśanaṃ vrataṃ vidhivadāgate \|*  
*mṛtyau na so 'pi saṃsāre bhūyaḥ paryaṭati dvija \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. If he undertakes a fast unto death he docs not return to this world, О bird.

## \|\| 4.40 \|\|

*kiṃ dānamiti turyasya praśnasyottaramīritam \|*  
*dāhamṛtyorantare kimitipraśnottaraṃ śṛṇu \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. I have given the answer to your queries about gifts. Now, I shall tell you about the obsequies and the funeral rites of the deceased.

## \|\| 4.41 \|\|

*gataprāṇaṃ tato jñātvā snātvā putrādirāśu tam \|*  
*śavaṃ jalena śuddhena kṣālayedavicārayan \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. When it is ascertained that life has gone out of the body, the son should bathe and wash the corpse with pure water without delay.

## \|\| 4.42 \|\|

*paridhāpyāhate vastre candanaiḥ prokṣayettanum \|*  
*tato mṛtasya sthāne vai ekoddiṣṭaṃ samācaret \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Having clothed it afresh they should smear the body with the sandaj-paste. The son or the successor should perform the ekoddista rite afterwards.

## \|\| 4.43 \|\|

*prayogapūrvaṃ dāhasya yogyatādiryathā bhavet \|*  
*āṃsanaṃ prākṣaṇa ca syānna syādetaccatuṣṭayam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Then the arrangement for cremating the corpse should be made as far as the means allow.

## \|\| 4.44 \|\|

*āvāhanārcana caiva pāntrālambhāvagāhane \|*  
*bhaveddānānnasaṅkalpaḥ piṇḍadānaṃ sadā bhavet \|\| 44 \|\|*
