*dharmeṣu gṛhyamāṇeṣu vṛṣotsargo viśeṣitaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Gifts, pilgrimage, penance, sacrifice, samnyasa and pitryajna — all these come under religion, specially the Vrsosarga.

## \|\| 6.12 \|\|

*eṣṭavyā bahavaḥ putrā yadyeko 'pi gayāṃ vrajet \|*  
*yajeta vāśvamedhena nīlaṃ vā vṛṣamutsṛjet \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. A man should desire many sons, because even if one of them goes to Gaya, he may perform a horse sacrifice or release a bull,

## \|\| 6.13 \|\|

*brahmahatyādipāpāni jñānājñānakṛtāni ca \|*  
*nīlodvāhena śudhyettu samudraplavanena vā \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. All heinous sins like the Brahmana slaughter, done knowingly, or unknowingly, are purified by releasing a bull or by

## \|\| 6.14 \|\|

*ekādaśāhe rājendra yasya notsūjyate vṛṣaḥ \|*  
*pretatvaṃ niścalaṃ tasya kṛtaiḥ śrāddhaistu kiṃ bhavet \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. О king, if the bull is not released on the eleventh day, the ghosthood of the deceased person becomes eternal. Then what is the use of performing a sraddha?

## \|\| 6.15 \|\|

*yathākathañcit kartavyastīrthe vā pattane 'tha vā \|*  
*vṛṣayajñaiḥ pramucyate nānyathā sādhanaiḥ khaga \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Somehow or other, in the holy place or in the town, a Vrsa-yajna should be done, О bird.

## \|\| 6.16-17 \|\|

*vṛṣabhaṃ pañcakalyāṇaṃ yuvānaṃ kṛṣṇakaṃbalam \|*  
*goyūthamadhye nitarāṃ vicarantaṃ vidhānataḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

*catasṛbhirvatsakābhirdvābhyāñcaivaikayā khaga \|*  
*vivāhya maṅgaladravyairmantravattaṃ samutsṛjet \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. A young Panca-kalyana bull, moving among the cows should be wedded to the four she-calves or two or even one, reciting the wedding mantras accompanied by the auspicious rites.

## \|\| 6.18 \|\|

*iha ratīti ṣaḍṛgbhirhemaṃ kuryādvibhāvasoḥ \|*  
*kārtikyāṃ māghavaiśākhyāṃ saṃkrame pātaparvasu \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. An offering should be made to God Fire reciting the six rks Iha ratih etc. It is specially recommended on the full moon day of Karttika, Magha, Vai^akha or on Sankranti days or on Sampata days, in the Pitr-tlrtha or on Aksaya day.

## \|\| 6.19-21 \|\|

*tīrthe pitryekṣayāhe ca viśeṣeṇa praśasyate \|*  
*lohito yastu varṇena mukhe pucche ca pāṇḍuraḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

*pītaḥ khuraviṣāṇeṣu sa nīlo vṛṣa ucyate \|*  
*śvetavarṇo bhavedvipro lohitaḥ kṣattra ucyate \|\| 20 \|\|*

*pītavarṇo bhavedvaiśyaḥ śūdraḥ kṛṣṇaḥ smṛto budhaiḥ \|*  
*yathāvarṇaṃ samuddiṣṭo varṇeṣu brāhmaṇādiṣu \|\| 21 \|\|*

19-21. A bull red in colour, white in the tail, yellow in hoofs and horns is called Nila, that of white colour is Vipra, that of red colour is Ksatra, that of yellow is Vaisya, and that of black is sudra, just as the colours of four castes have been prescribed by the wise ones.

## \|\| 6.22-26 \|\|

*atha vā raktavarṇastu sarveṣāmeva śasyate \|*  
*pitā pitāmahaścaiva tathaiva prapitāmahaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*āśāsate sutaṃ jātaṃ vṛṣotsargaṃ kariṣyati \|*  
*dharmastvaṃ vṛṣarūpeṇa jagadānandadāyakaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

*aṣṭamūrteradhiṣṭhānamataḥ śāntiṃ prayaccha me \|*  
*gaṅgāyamunayoḥ peyamantarvedi tṛṇaṃ cara \|\| 24 \|\|*

*dharmarājasya purato vācyaṃ me sukṛtaṃ vṛṣa \|*  
*dakṣiṇāṃse triśūlāṅkaṃ vāmorau cakracihnitam \|\| 25 \|\|*

*iti saṃprārthya vṛṣabhaṃ gandhapuṣpākṣatādibhiḥ \|*  
*vṛṣaṃ tatsatarīyuktaṃ pūjayitvā samutsṛjet \|\| 26 \|\|*

22-26. A bull of red colour is preferred. Father, grandfather, and great-grandfather hope that the son will perform a Vrsotsarga. "You are Dharma in the form of a bull, the delighter of the whole world. You are asta-murti, hence, give me peace. Drink and eat grass in the antarvedi of Ganga and Yamuna. О bull, you should speak about my good deeds, before Dharmaraja." Thus praising and marking him with the sign of Trisula in the right shoulder and with the sign of Cakra in the left thighs and worshipping the bull with incense, flowers, uncooked rice, etc., he should release the same along with the she-calf.

## \|\| 6.27 \|\|

*tasmādrājan vidhānena vṛṣotsargaṃ samācara \|*  
*bahusādhanayuktasya nānyathā sadgatistava \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Hence, О king, perform Vrsotsarga as prescribed the Dharma-sastras. You possess all the virtues for under in taking the rite. There is no other way for you.

## \|\| 6.28-29 \|\|

*āsīttretāyuge pūrvaṃ videhanagare nṛpa \|*  
*brāhmaṇo dharmavatseti svakarmanirataḥ sudhīḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

*viṣṇubhakto 'titejasvī yathālābhena tuṣṭikṛt \|*  
*pitṛparvaṇi saṃprāpte kuśāryo kānanaṃ yayau \|\| 29 \|\|*

28-29. Long, long ago, in the Tretayuga, there lived a Brahmana Dharma-vatsa by name, in the city of Vaideha. He was learned, dutiful, lustrous and devoted to Visnu. He remained satisfied with whatever he could get. When the pitrpaksa came he went to the forest for collecting the Kusa grass.

## \|\| 6.30 \|\|

*aṭannitastatastatra cinvan kuśapalāśakam \|*  
*sahasopetya puruṣāścātvāraścārudarśanāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. When he was moving here and there, picking the sacred grass and leaves, four good- looking persons came all of a sudden.

## \|\| 6.31-33 \|\|

*vibhrāntamanasaṃ gṛhya pratyagjagmurvihāyasā \|*  
*bahuvṛkṣasamākīrṇaṃ giridurgabhayānakam \|\| 31 \|\|*

*vanādvanāntaraṃ ninyurnadīnadasamākulam \|*  
*sa tatra nagaraṃ rājan dadarśa bahuvistaram \|\| 32 \|\|*

*gopuradvāraracitaṃ saudhaprāsādamaṇḍitam \|*  
*catvarāpaṇapaṇyādinaranārīsamākulam \|\| 33 \|\|*

31-33. They caught hold of him while he was perplexed. They carried him forcibly into the sky. Crossing fierce mountains and forests, rivers and rivulets, they reached a city of huge dimension, with gopura doors, adorned with palaces, cross-roads, markets and shops crowded with men and women, resounding with the noise of trumpets, lutes and drums.

## \|\| 6.34-36 \|\|

*tūryadvandvābhinirghoṣavīṇāpaṭahanāditam \|*  
*kāṃścitkṣudhārditāndīnānmalinānvigataujasaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

*tato 'tituṣṭānmalinānvastrakhaṇḍasamāvṛtān \|*  
*agrato hṛṣṭapuṣṭāṃśca svarṇavastropaśobhitān \|\| 35 \|\|*

*tato 'pi surasaṃkāśānsa dṛṣṭvā vismito 'bhavat \|*  
*kiṃ svapna uta māyā vai madīyo mānaso bhramaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

34-36. There he saw persons-some poor, some rich, some hungry, some good, some bad some ill clad, some dressed in golden clothes like the deities. He thought within himself. Is this a dream or an illusion or a fancy.

## \|\| 6.37-38 \|\|

*sandihānaṃ dvijaṃ ninyuḥ puruṣā rājasannidhim \|*  
*sataddadarśa viprastu svarṇaprāsādamandire \|\| 37 \|\|*

*siṃhāsanaṃmahādivyaṃ chatracāmaravījitam \|*  
*tatropa viṣṭaṃ rājānaṃ kirīṭakanakojjvalam \|\| 38 \|\|*

37-38. They took the surprised Brahmana to the king. There he saw in a golden palace a divine throne fanned with Camara and Chatra and the king sitting there with a shining golden crest.

## \|\| 6.39-40 \|\|

*mahatyā ca śriyā yuktaṃ stūyamānaṃ suvandibhiḥ \|*  
*rājāpi dṛṣṭvā taṃ vipraṃ pratyutthāya kṛtāñjaliḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

*pūjayāmāsa vidhivanmadhuparkāsa nādibhiḥ \|*  
*santuṣṭamanasaṃ devamastauṣītparayā mudā \|\| 40 \|\|*

39-40. The king possessed great lustre and was praised by the singing bards. On seeing that Brahmana come, the king stood up with folded hands, honoured him in the traditionally prescribed way with Madhuparka, seat, etc. Then with joy and delight he praised the Brahmana who was fully satisfied.

## \|\| 6.41 \|\|

*adya me saphalaṃ janma pāvitañca kulaṃ prabho \|*  
*viṣṇubhaktasya dharmasya yatte dṛggocaraṃ gataḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Today, my birth has been successful and my family purified, О lord, as I see today a religious devotee of Visnu.

## \|\| 6.42 \|\|

*natvā stutvā bahuvidhamuvācānuvasannṛpaḥ \|*  
*yataḥ samāgato devaḥ punastatraiva nīyatām \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Having bowed to the Brahmana and praised him in many ways, the king said: "you will be taken back to the place whence you have come here."

## \|\| 6.43-44 \|\|

*iti śrutvā vaco rājñaḥ papraccha dvijapuṅgavaḥ \|*  
*brāhmaṇa uvāca \|*  
*ko 'yaṃ deśa- kuto lokā uttamā madhyamādhamāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

*kena puṇyena tu bhavānpārameṣṭyavibhūṣitaḥ \|*  
*kimarthamahamānītaḥ punastatraiva nīyate \|\| 44 \|\|*

43-44. Brahmana said: — On hearing the king's words the Brahmana said: "What is this land? Who are the people? the noble, the middle, and the base ones? By what good deeds have you obtained the lordship of this region? What for was I brought here and why am I being carried away?"

## \|\| 6.45-46 \|\|

*apūrvamiva paśyāmi sarvaṃ svapnagato yathā \|*  
*rājovāca \|*  
*svadharmanirato yastu haribhaktirataḥ sadā \|\| 45 \|\|*

*virakta indriyārthebhyaḥ sa me pūjyo na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*tīrthayātrāparo nityaṃ vṛṣotsargaviśeṣavit \|\| 46 \|\|*

45-46. "I see many things unseen before. To me everything seems to be a dream." "As you have been always devoted to religion and have been a devotee of Visnu disinterested in any other thing I respect you. You have always been on the pilgrimage and have performed Vrsotsarga to the deserving.

## \|\| 6.47 \|\|

*satyadānaparo yastu sa namasyo divaukasām \|*  
*darśanārthamihānītaḥ pūjārhaśca parantapa \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. As you always speak truth and give gifts, you are bowed to by the deities. О Parantapa, you have been brought for a glimpse of this land, as you are worthy of this honour.

## \|\| 6.48 \|\|

*anugṛhāṇa māṃ deva kṣamasva mama sāhasam \|*  
*ityuktvā darśayāmāsa mantriṇāṃ saṃjñayā bhruvaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Be pleased withme, О Lord, and excuse my rash act." Having said thus he presented his ministers by name by hint of brows.

## \|\| 6.49 \|\|

*vadiṣyati samagraṃ te svayaṃ vaktuṃ na sāmpratam \|*  
*sāmantaḥ sarvavedajño jñātvā hārdaṃ nṛpasya ca \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. "He will tell you everything. It is not proper for me to speak more." Thus he introduced Samanta who knew the Vedas, and understood their meaning, and asked him to speak.

## \|\| 6.50 \|\|

*vipaściduvāca \|*  
*pūrvajanmani vaiśyo 'yaṃ viśvambhara iti śrutaḥ \|*  
*virādhanagare vipra dvijadevavibhūṣite \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. The Samanta spoke — "This man was a Vaisya, Visvambhara by name, in his previous birth, in the city named Viradha, adored by Brahmanas and deities.

## \|\| 6.51 \|\|

*vaiśyavṛttyā sadā jīvankuṭumbaparipālakaḥ \|*  
*gavāṃ śuśrūṣako nityaṃ brāhmaṇānāñca pūjakaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Не lived by the profession of his caste and maintained his family. He served the cows and worshipped the Brahmanas.

## \|\| 6.52 \|\|

*pātradānaparo nityamātitheyāgnisevakaḥ \|*  
*gārhasthyaṃ vidhivaccakre bhāryayā satyamedhayā \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. He offered gifts to the deserving, welcomed the guests and poured oblations into the fire. He performed the duties of his profession together with this truthful wife.

## \|\| 6.53-54 \|\|

*smārtena lokānajayacchrautena ta havirbhujaḥ \|*  
*kadācidbandhubhiḥ sākaṃ kṛtvā tīrthāni bhūriśaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

*yāvadāyāti sadanaṃ dṛṣṭavālloṃmaśaṃ pathi \|*  
*daṇḍavatpraṇipatyāśu kṛtāñjalipuṭaṃ sthitam \|\| 54 \|\|*

53-54. By his righteous conduct he won his people; by his gifts of oblations he won the deities. Once, when after completing his pilgrimage together with his relatives, he was returning home, he saw Sage Lomasa in the way. He bowed to him immediately fell prostrate and then stood up with joined. hands.

## \|\| 6.55-57 \|\|

*papraccha vinayopetaṃ karuṇāvārivāridhiḥ \|*  
*ṛṣiruvāca \|*  
*kuta āgamyate sādho brāhmaṇairbandhubhiryutaḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

*dṛṣṭvā tvāṃ dharmanilayaṃ praklinnaṃ mānasaṃ mama \|*  
*viśvambhara uvāca \|*  
*śīryamāṇaṃ śarīraṃ hi jñātvā mṛtyuṃ puraḥ sthitam \|\| 56 \|\|*

*bharyayā dharmacāriṇyā tīrthayātrāṃ vinirgataḥ \|*  
*kṛtvā tīrthāni vidhivadviśrāṇya vipulaṃ vasu \|\| 57 \|\|*

55-57. The pious sage who was the ocean of pity said to him: The Sage said: — Gentleman, from where do you come together with your relatives and Brahmanas. On seeing you, a religious person that you are, my heart has melted. Visvambhara said: — After seeing the decaying body and the death standing before, I went to pilgrimage together with my wife.

## \|\| 6.58 \|\|

*yāvadbrajāmyahaṃ veśma bhavān dṛṣṭipathaṃ gataḥ \|*  
*lomaśa uvāca \|*  
*tīrthāni santi bhūrīṇi varṣai'smin bhārate śubhe \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. I am returning home when I happen to see you Just now.

## \|\| 6.59-63 \|\|

*yattvayā hyupacīrṇāni tāni sarvāṇi me vada \|*  
*vaiśya uvāca \|*  
*gaṅgā ca sūrya tanayā mahāpuṇyā sarasvatī \|\| 59 \|\|*

*daśāśvamedhairayajadyatra brahmā sureśvaraḥ \|*  
*tīrtharājastataḥ kāśī mahādevo dayānidhiḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

*mṛtānāṃ yatra jantūnāṃ karṇe japati tārakam \|*  
*pulahasyāśramaṃ puṇyaṃ phalgutīrthañca gaṇḍakī \|\| 61 \|\|*

*cakratīrthaṃ naimiṣañca śivatīrthamanantakam \|*  
*gopratārakanāgeśamayodhyābindusaṃjñitam \|\| 62 \|\|*

*yatrāsta muktidaḥ sākṣādrāmo rājīvalocanaḥ \|*  
*āgneyaṃ vāyukauberaṃ kaumāraṃ bhūruhāṃ punaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

Lomasa said:-There are many holy places in this sacred region. Tell me those which you have visited now.

59-63. Vaisya said: — I have visited Ganga, the daughter of the sun, holy Sarasvati where Brahma performed ten horse-sacrifices; KasI, the best of holy places where the kind Mahadeva recites Taraka in the ears of dead creatures; the holy hermitage of Pulaha; Phalgutlrtha; Gandaki. Cakratlrtha; Naimisa; Shivatlrtha; Goprataraka; Nagesa; Ayodhya where the lotus-eyed Rama lived to confer mukti (salvation).

## \|\| 6.64-67 \|\|

*saukaraṃ mathurā yatra nityaṃ sannihato hariḥ \|*  
*puṣkaraṃ satyatīrthañca jvālatīrthaṃ dineśvaram \|\| 64 \|\|*

*indratīrthaṃ kurukṣetraṃ yatra prācī sarasvatī \|*  
*tāpī payoṣṇī nirvindhyā malayaḥ kṛṣṇaveṇikā \|\| 65 \|\|*

*godāvarī daṇḍakañca tāmracūḍaṃ sadodakam \|*  
*dyāvābhūmīśvaraṃ dṛṣṭvā śrīśailaḥ parvateśvaraḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

*asaṃkhyaliṅgatīrthāni yatra santi sadā mune \|*  
*veṅkaṭādrau mahātejāḥ śrīraṅgākhyaḥ svayaṃ hariḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

64-67. Mathura where Hari always stayed; Puskara; Satyatirtha; Jvala-tlrtha; Dinesvara. Indra-tlrtha; Kuruksetra where flows the ancient Sarasvati; Tapi; Payosnl; Nirvindhya; Malaya; Krsna-Venika. Godavari; Dandaka; Tamracuda; Sadodaka; Dyavabhumlsvara; Shrisaila the best of mountains where many Lingatlrthas exist; Venkata, a mountain where god Hari Srlranga, lives. And also the goddess Venkati, the slay.. of Mahisa lives, Candratlrtha, Bhadre1.a, Kaverl; Kulilacala.

## \|\| 6.69-72 \|\|

*avaṭodā tāmraparṇo trikṛṭaḥ kollako giriḥ \|*  
*vāsiṣṭhaṃ brahmatīrthañca jñānatīrthaṃ mahodadhiḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

*hṛṣīkeśaṃ virājañca viśālaṃ nīlaparvataḥ \|*  
*bhīmakūṭaḥ śvetagirī rudratīrthamumāvanam \|\| 70 \|\|*

*avāpa girijā devī tapasā yatra śaṅkaram \|*  
*vāruṇaṃ sūryatīrthañca haṃsatīrthaṃ mahodayam \|\| 71 \|\|*

*nimajjya yatra kākolā rājahaṃsatvamāyayuḥ \|*  
*asuro yatra devatvamavāpa snānamātrataḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

69-72. Avatoda; Tamraparm, Trikuta; Kollaka mount; Vasistha Brahmatlrtha and Jnanatlrtha ocean, mountain; Bhlma Kuta; sveta-giri; Rudratirtha; Umavana. Where Goddess Girija obtained Sankara by performing penance; Varuna; Surya-tlrtha; Hamsa tlrtha; Mahodaya where ravens become royal geese after having a dip and where the demons became deities after bathing.

## \|\| 6.73-74 \|\|

*viśvarūpaṃ vanditīrthaṃ ratneśaḥ kuhakācalaḥ \|*  
*naranārāyaṇaṃ dṛṣṭvā mucyate pāpakoṭibhiḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

*sarasvatīdṛṣadvatyau narmadā śarmadā nṛṇām \|*  
*nīlakaṇṭhaṃ mahākālaṃ puṇyaṃ cāmarakaṇṭakam \|\| 74 \|\|*

73-74. Visvarupa; Bandl-tirtha; Ratnesa; Kuhaka- cala where one is released from crores of sins after seeing Nara-Narayana. Sarasvatl; Drsadvatl; Narmada that grants happiness to kings; Nllakantha; Mahakala; holy Amara- kantaka.

## \|\| 6.75-76 \|\|

*candrabhāgā vetravatī vīrabhadraṃ gaṇeśvaram \|*  
*gokarṇaṃ bilvatīrthañca karmakuṇḍaṃ satārakam \|\| 75 \|\|*

*snānamātreṇa yatrāśu mucyate karmabandhanāt \|*  
*anyānyapi ca tīrthāni kṛtāni kṛpayā tava \|\| 76 \|\|*

75-76. Candrabhaga; Vetravatl; Virabhadra; Ganesvara; Gokarna, Vilvatirtha; Sataraka; Karmakunda where one is immedialely released from the bondage of Karmas by taking a dip; and so many other holy places I have visited by your grace.

## \|\| 6.77 \|\|

*utpadyate śubhā buddhiḥ sādhūnāṃ yadanugrahaḥ \|*  
*ekataḥ sarvatīrthāni karuṇāḥ sādhavo 'nyataḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. Whenever the saints take pity there dawn upon the visitor so many good thoughts. Hence, all sacred places are on the one side and the compassionate saints on the other.

## \|\| 6.78 \|\|

*anugrahāya bhūtānāṃ caranti caritavratāḥ \|*  
*tvaṃ guruḥ sarvarṇānāṃ vidyayā vayasādhikaḥ \|\| 78 \|\|*

78\. These men of character are moved to pity all creatures. By knowledge and by age you are the instructor of all Varnas.

## \|\| 6.79 \|\|

*ataḥ pṛcchāmyahaṃ kiñcidādhibhūtaṃ cirantanam \|*  
*kiṃ kuryāṃ kaṃ nu pṛcche 'haṃ mano me 'ticalaṃ mune \|\| 79 \|\|*

79\. Hence, I shall ask you the secret about the spiritual eternal. What shall I do and whom shall I approach? Tell me О sage, my mind is wavering.

## \|\| 6.80 \|\|

*niḥ spṛhaṃ brahmaviṣaye viṣayeṣvatilālasam \|*  
*manāgapi na sahate virahaṃ timiraṃ bruvat \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. It has no desire for attaining Brahma. It is very much fond of worldly objects and it cannot stand their separation even for a moment, nor can it consider them to be illusory.

## \|\| 6.81 \|\|

*mohitaṃ vividhairbhāvaiḥ karmaṇāṃ kṣetramuttamam \|*  
*śāntiṃ yathā samāyāti sampannamiva bhūsura \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. Infatuated by various bhavas it think the field of Karmas to be the best. О Brahmana, now tell me how it may obtain peace.

## \|\| 6.82 \|\|

*vivekapravaṇaṃ śuddhaṃ yathā syātkṛpayā vada \|*  
*ṛṣiruvāca \|*  
*manastu prabalaṃ nityaṃ savikāraṃ svabhāvataḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

82\. How it may incline towards good thought and how it may become pure. Now enlighten me on this point, please.

## \|\| 6.83 \|\|

*vaśaṃ nayanti kariṇaṃ pramattamapi hastipāḥ \|*  
*tathāpi sādhusaṅgatyā sādhanairapyatandritaḥ \|\| 83 \|\|*

83\. The mind is always very powerful and naturally inclined to error.

## \|\| 6.84-85 \|\|

*tīvreṇa bhaktiyogena vicāreṇa vaśaṃ nayet \|*  
*itihāsaṃ pravakṣyāmi tava pratyayakārakam \|\| 84 \|\|*

*nārado 'kathayanmahyaṃ svavṛttagatajanmanaḥ \|*  
*nārada uvāca \|*  
*kasyaciddvijamukhyasya dāsīputtraḥ purā mune \|\| 85 \|\|*

84-85. As the elephant keepers can bring under control even an uncontrollable elephant, similarly by association with the saints, by constant application of proper means, by adopting the path of devotion and virtue, the mind can be controlled. I shall now tell you an old history to make you believe it.

## \|\| 6.86-87 \|\|

*śikṣito bālabhāve 'pi pāṭhito nitarāmaham \|*  
*tatrāpi saṅgatirjātā mahatāṃ puṇyakarmaṇām \|\| 86 \|\|*

*prāvṛṭkāle mama gṛhe sthitānāṃ bhāgyayogataḥ \|*  
*śuśrūṣaṇānuvṛttyā ca praśrayeṇa damena ca \|\| 87 \|\|*

86-87. Now, hear what Narada had told me about the history of his previous life. In my previous birth, I was the maid's son of a Brahmana. In my childhood, I was taught continuously and I remained in the company of pious people who stayed by good luck, in my house during the rainy season. Once, interested in their talk and wholesome precepts I was greatly delighted. I approached them with extreme politeness and due reverence and addressed them thus

## \|\| 6.88 \|\|

*santoṣaṃ paramaṃ prāpya kṛpayā tvidamabruvan \|*  
*manīṣā nirmalā yena jātā mama śubhārthinī \|\| 88 \|\|*

88\. I said: О noble sages, be pleased to purify my thoughts so that my intellect may get pure and seek the good only, seeing everything full of Visnu

## \|\| 6.89-92 \|\|

*yayā viṣṇumayaṃ sarvamtmanyeva dadṛśivān \|*  
*munaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*śṛṇu vatsa pravakṣyā mo hitāya tava bālaka \|\| 89 \|\|*

*yena vai dhriyamāṇena ihāmutra sukhaṃ bhavet \|*  
*devatiryaṅmanuṣyāśca saṃsāre vividhā janāḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

*nibaddhāḥ karmapaśaiste bhuñjan bhogān pṛthagvidhān \|*  
*devatvaṃ yāti sattvena rajasā ca manuṣyatām \|\| 91 \|\|*

*tiryaktvaṃ tamasā janturvāsanānugato 'budhaḥ \|*  
*māturlabdhvā punarjanma mriyate ca punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 92 \|\|*

89-92. О child, hear. We shall tell you something for your benefit, by understanding which you will achieve happiness here and hereafter. Different types of creatures bf the world- deities, animals, men are tied with the noose of Karmas and experience different types of enjoyment. By sattva they attain deityhood; by rajas they attain manhood; by tamas they attain animalhood. A creature is led by desires and'is ignorant. He is born again and again out of the womb of his mother and dies again and again in different births.

## \|\| 6.93-94 \|\|

*evaṃ gatvā hyasaṃkhyātā yonīstāḥ karmabhūrapi \|*  
*mānuṣyaṃ durlabhaṃ labdhvā kadāciddaivayogataḥ \|\| 93 \|\|*

*anugraheṇa mahatāṃ hariṃ jñātvā vimucyate \|*  
*rogagrāhaṃ mohajālamapāraṃ bhavasāgaram \|\| 94 \|\|*

93-94. They pass through several births as the result of their activities. Sometimes, they are born as men by virtue of their previous actions. It happens that they come in contact with, the pious people and by their good will and blessing they come to realize the Supreme Self and achieve liberation.

## \|\| 6.95-96 \|\|

*na paśyāmi titīrṣoranyad rāmasmaraṇaṃ vinā \|*  
*navanīyaṃ yathā dadhno jyotiḥ kāṣṭhādapi kvacit \|\| 95 \|\|*

*manthanaiḥ sādhanairevaṃ paraṃ jñātvā sukhī bhavet \|*  
*ātmā nityo 'vyayaḥ satyaḥ sarvagaḥ sarvabhṛnmahān \|\| 96 \|\|*

95-96. This vast ocean of Existence abounds in crocodiles, overspread with the network of illusions. Any person who wishes to cross this ocean has no means other than the recitation of the name Rama.

## \|\| 6.97-98 \|\|

*aprameyaḥ svayañjyotiragrāhyo manasāpi yaḥ \|*  
*saccidānandarūpo 'sau sarvaprāṇihṛdi sthitaḥ \|\| 97 \|\|*

*vinaśyatsvapi bhāveṣu na vinaśyati karhicit \|*  
*ākāśaḥ sarvabhūteṣu sthitastejojale tathā \|\| 98 \|\|*

97-98. As we get butter by churning the curd, fire by rubbing the two pieces of wood, so by constant and concentrated meditation we can realize the Self and obtain peace. The Self is eternal, indeclinable, truth, omnipresent, allsupporting, greal, non-comprehensible, self- refulgent, non-apprehensible even to the mind. In the form of Existence, Consciousness and Bliss, the self is stationed in the heart of all creatures. While all other things are mortal, the Self alone is immortal. The Self is present in all beings as the ether in fire and water.

## \|\| 6.99 \|\|

*ātmā sarvatra nirlepaḥ pārthiveṣu yathānilaḥ \|*  
*bhaktānukampī bhagavān sādhūnāṃ rakṣaṇāya ca \|\| 99 \|\|*

99\. The Supreme Self, like the fire, is not attached to any one. It is compassionate to the devotees and for their protection it assumes human form but the ignorant regardit as man.

## \|\| 6.100 \|\|

*āvirbhavati lokeṣuguṇīvājñaiḥ pratīyate \|*  
*evaṃvivekatvayā yo buddhyā saṃśīlayeddhṛdi \|\| 100 \|\|*

100\. If a person, possessed of differential knowledge meditates on him in the heart, the Unborn Self is pleased by his devotion and appears to him in his true form.

## \|\| 6.101 \|\|

*bhaktiyogena santuṣṭa ātmānaṃ darśayedajaḥ \|*  
*tataḥ kṛtārtho bhavati sadā sarvatra niḥ spṛhaḥ \|\| 101 \|\|*

101\. It makes the seeker ever gratified when all sorts of desires forsake him for ever.

## \|\| 6.102 \|\|

*ato 'haṅkāramutsṛjya sānubandhe kalevare \|*  
*caredasaṃgo lokeṣu svapnaprāyeṣu nirmamaḥ \|\| 102 \|\|*

102\. Hence, leaving your ego and attachment for this mortal frame you should move all alone in this dream land without imbibing any sense of desire.

## \|\| 6.103 \|\|

*kva svapne niyataṃ dhairyamindrajāle kva satyatā \|*  
*kva nityatā śaranmeghe kva vā satyaṃ kalevare \|\| 103 \|\|*

103\. Where is stability in dream, truth in magic, eternity in the autumnal cloud and reality in the body.

## \|\| 6.104 \|\|

*avidyākarmajanitaṃ dṛśyamānaṃ carā caram \|*  
*jñātvācāravaśī yogī tataḥ siddhimavāpsyasi \|\| 104 \|\|*

104\. Knowing that the world is born of ignorance, you should restrain your senses and control your mind, thereby you will obtain peace.

## \|\| 6.105 \|\|

*ityuktvā te gatāḥ sarve sādhavo dīnavatsalāḥ \|*  
*so 'haṃ taduktamārgeṇa tathaivācaramanvaham \|\| 105 \|\|*

105\. Having said thus the affectionate saints went away and I followed the path shown by them.

## \|\| 6.106 \|\|

*tato 'cireṇātmanīdaṃ dṛṣṭavānahamadbhutam \|*  
*jyotirmayaṃ sadānandaṃ śaracchītāṃśunirmalam \|\| 106 \|\|*

106\. Then very soon, I saw within myself a wonderful ever-joyful Atman pure like the autumnal moon.

## \|\| 6.107 \|\|

*niṣicya sukhasandohairmāṃ kṛtvādhikasaspṛham \|*  
*antarhitaṃ mahatejo yathā saudāminī divi \|\| 107 \|\|*

107\. Sprinkling me with happiness and making me more inclined, the dazzling light hid itself inside like lightning in the sky.

## \|\| 6.108 \|\|

*bhaktyā tadeva manasi bhāvayannahamadbhutam \|*  
*kāle kalevaraṃ tyaktvā gatavān harimavyayam \|\| 108 \|\|*

108\. Realizing the same in my mind through devotion, I left my mortal coil in due course

## \|\| 6.109 \|\|

*tasyecchayā punarbrahman brahmaṇo me 'bhavajjaniḥ \|*  
*anugrahādbhagavatastriṣu lokeṣu niḥ spṛhaḥ \|\| 109 \|\|*

109\. Then by His desire, О Brahmana, I was born again through the grace of God. I had no attachment in the world.

## \|\| 6.110 \|\|

*āpīḍayan muhurvoṇāṃ gāyamānaścarāmyaham \|*  
*ityuktvā me svānubhavaṃ yayau yādṛcchiko muniḥ \|\| 110 \|\|*

110\. Playing on my lute and singing I moved from place to place. Thus telling me his experience, the self-willed sage went away.

## \|\| 6.111-112 \|\|

*mamāpi paramāścaryaṃ santoṣaśca mahānabhūt \|*  
*ataste sādhusaṅgatyā bhaktyā ca paramātmanaḥ \|\| 111 \|\|*

*viśuddhaṃ nirmalaṃ śāntaṃ mano nirvṛtimeṣyati \|*  
*anekajanmajanitaṃ pātakaṃ sādhusaṃgame \|\| 112 \|\|*

111-112. I was surprised and satisfied. Thus, indeed, in the company of virtuous men and by God's grace, a person becomes pure and calm in mind and attains perfect peace. О Knower of Virtue, even the sin inherited from previous births disappears immediately in the company of good men like the water of the autumnal ciouds.

## \|\| 6.113-114 \|\|

*kṣipraṃ naśyati dharmajña jalānāṃ śarado yathā \|*  
*vaiśya uvāca \|*  
*pītvā te vākyapīyūṣaṃ svāntaṃ me śāntimāgamat \|\| 113 \|\|*

*sarvatīrthaphalaṃ me 'dhya sañjātaṃ tava darśanāt \|*  
*iti śrutvā vacastasya provāca ṛpisattamaḥ \|\| 114 \|\|*

113-114. Viprascitti said: — Drinking the nectar of your words, my soul has obtained peace. I have received the fruit of all pilgrimages at the very I sight of yours. Having heard the words of the king the sage said unto him.

## \|\| 6.115 \|\|

*lomaśa uvāca \|*  
*hitāya tava rājendra trivargaphalamicchataḥ \|*  
*yattvayā sukṛtaṃ bhūrivṛṣotsargaṃ vinā kṛtam \|\| 115 \|\|*

115\. Lomasa said: — О mighty monarch, who desire the result of Trivarga, this is for your benefit. Whatever good you have done is, in my opinion, rendered fruitless because it is done without Vrsotsarga.

## \|\| 6.116 \|\|

*manye 'kiñcatkaraṃ sarvaṃ nīhārasalilaṃ yathā \|*  
*vṛṣotsargasamaṃ kiñcit sādhanaṃ na mahītale \|\| 116 \|\|*

116\. Hence, it is like a dew-drop. There is no other means of accomplishment in this world and went to the eternal abode of Hari. except the performance of Vrsotsarga.

## \|\| 6.117 \|\|

*anāyāsena gacchanti gatiṃ te puṇyakarmaṇām \|*  
*vṛṣotsargaḥ kṛto yena aśvamedhasya yājakaḥ \|\| 117 \|\|*

117\. Know that the two can easily obtain the status of pious persons. One who performs Vrsotsarga and the other who performs the horse-sacrifice.

## \|\| 6.118-119 \|\|

*ubhau samau mayā dṛṣṭau divyau tau śakrasannidhau \|*  
*atastvaṃ puṣkaraṃ gatvā vṛṣotsargaṃ vidhāya ca \|\| 118 \|\|*

*tato yāhi gṛhaṃ sādho yena sarvaṃ kṛtaṃ bhavet \|*  
*vipaściduvāca \|*  
*tataḥ sa punarāgatya kārtikyāṃ puṣkare vare \|\| 119 \|\|*

118-119. I have seen the two standing side by side near the Great God Indra. Hence, go to Puskara and perform a Vrsotsarga. Vipascitti said: — Then go home, О gentleman, and do the needful. For what is to be done should be done immediately.

## \|\| 6.120 \|\|

*varāharūpī bhagavān yatrāste yajñapūrakaḥ \|*  
*cakāra vidhivat sarvaṃ yudkamṛṣisattamaiḥ \|\| 120 \|\|*

120\. Then in the monilt of Karttika come to the Puskara lake where god in the form of boar himself performs a sacrifice. Thus told he performed Vrsotsarga as prescribed by the best of the sages

## \|\| 6.121-122 \|\|

*gatāni bahutīrthāni tato lomaśasaṃgatiḥ \|*  
*tato 'dhikataraṃ jātaṃ puṇyaṃ nīlavivāhajam \|\| 121 \|\|*

*sabhuktvā viṣayān divyān vimānavaramāśritaḥ \|*  
*tena rājakule janma vīrasenasya dharmataḥ \|\| 122 \|\|*

121-122. He visited holy places in the company of Lomasa and his merit was increased by performing Vrsotsarga. After death he went to heaven, enjoyed heavenly pleasures, travelling in magnificent cars (and after the exhaustion of merit) was born in the royal family of Virasena.

## \|\| 6.123-128 \|\|

*vīrapañcānanākhyātañcaturvargaikasādhakam \|*  
*prakurvato vṛṣotsargaṃ tatra ye paricārakāḥ \|\| 123 \|\|*

*divyarūpābhavan spṛṣṭā gopucchodakaśīkaraiḥ \|*  
*surūpāḥ puṣṭavapuṣaḥ paśyanto dūrasaṃsthitāḥ \|\| 124 \|\|*

*tato dūratarā ye ca dṛśyante malinā janāḥ \|*  
*durbhagā malinā rūkṣāḥ kṛśā vigatavāsasaḥ \|\| 125 \|\|*

*vṛṣayajñamapaśyanto ye cāsūyāṃ prakurvate \|*  
*sarvaṃ niveditaṃ rājñaścaritaṃ pūrvajanmanaḥ \|\| 126 \|\|*

*dharmyaṃ vicitramākhyānaṃ śrutaṃ me yat parāśarāt \|*  
*atastvaṃ svagṛhaṃ gaccha kṛpāṃ kṛtvā mamopari \|\| 127 \|\|*

*śrutvā vipaścidvākyaṃ sa vismayaṃ paramaṃ gataḥ \|*  
*gṛhaṃ jagāma vipro 'sau prāpito rājasevakaiḥ \|\| 128 \|\|*

123-128. Vasistha said: — He was known as Vlrapan- canana. When he performed Vrsotsarga that obtains for the performer the fruits of Trivarga he was assisted by a number of attendants. These were sprinkled over by the drops of water from the bull's tail and as a result got divine forms. These were the persons who looked strong, stout and beautiful. Then there were the persons who out of jealousy did not cherish the performance of Vrsotsarga. They were born lean, remained naked and dirty. This, in brief, is the record of the past life of the king. This strange story I have heard from Parasara. Having heard this story you may go home immediately. On hearing the words of Vipascit the Brahmana was very much surprised. He went home escorted by the King's servants.

## \|\| 6.129-130 \|\|

*vasiṣṭha uvāca \|*  
*tasmādrājan vṛṣotsargaṃ variṣṭhaṃ sarvakarmaṇām \|*  
*samācara vidhānena yadi bhīto yamādapi \|\| 129 \|\|*

*vṛṣotsargasamaṃ kiñcit sādhanaṃ nadivaḥ param \|*  
*mayā dharmarahasyaṃ te kathitaṃ rājasattama \|\| 130 \|\|*

129-130. Vasistha said: — Therefore, О king, perform Vrsotsarga, the best of all pious acts, if you are afraid of Yama. There is no other means better than Vrsotsarga, О King, I have told you the secret of Dharma to-day.

## \|\| 6.131 \|\|

*patiputravatī nārī bharturagre mṛtā yadi \|*  
*vṛṣotsargaṃ na kurvīta gāṃ dadyācca payasviḥ nīm \|\| 131 \|\|*

131\. If a woman whose husband and son are alive dies in the presence of her husband, Vrsotsarga should not be performed and as an alternative a milch, cow should be given to a Brahmana.

## \|\| 6.132-133 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*śrutvā vākyaṃ vasiṣṭhasya rājā madhupurīṃ gataḥ \|*  
*cakāra vidhivat sarvaṃ vṛṣotsargamahaṃ khaga \|\| 132 \|\|*

*gṛhaṃ gatvā sa ātmānaṃ kṛtakṛtyamamanyata \|*  
*kālena nidhanaṃ prāpto nīto vaivasvatānugaiḥ \|\| 133 \|\|*

132-133. Sri Krsna said: — On hearing the words of Vasistha, the king went to Madhupurl and performed Vrsotsarga and was gratified at heart. When in due course of time he passed away he was carried by Yama's messengers.

## \|\| 6.134-135 \|\|

*sa kālanagaraṃ hitvā gato dūrataraṃ pathi \|*  
*śrāddhadevapuraṃ kutretyevaṃ dūtānapṛcchata \|\| 134 \|\|*

*pāpino yatra pātyante yāmyai pāpaviśuddhaye \|*  
*yatra devaḥ sa dharmādharmavicetanaḥ \|\| 135 \|\|*

134-135. He passed by the city of Yama in the way. He asked the escorting messengers about the city of sraddhadeva where the sinners were let down by Yama's servants for the purgation of their soul, and where the lord who knows dharma and adharma resides.

## \|\| 6.136 \|\|

*gataṃ pāpapuraṃ tattu na draṣṭavyaṃ bhavādṛśaiḥ \|*  
*agre dṛṣṭvā dharmarājamūcuste paramādarāt \|\| 136 \|\|*

136\. Thereupon the messengers told him that they had passed by the city of Sinners which was not to be seen by persons like him. They then escorted him to the city of Dharmaraja and standing before Dharma-raja informed Him about the history of the monarch.

## \|\| 6.137-140 \|\|

*divyarūpastadā devo devagandharvasaṃyutaḥ \|*  
*ātmānaṃ darśayā māsa tasya rājño mahātmanaḥ \|\| 137 \|\|*

*praṇamya daṇḍavadrājā kṛtāñjaliḥ puraḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*tuṣṭāva bahudhā devaṃ harṣapuritamānasaḥ \|\| 138 \|\|*

*dharmarājo 'pi rājānaṃ praśasyedamuvāca ha \|*  
*nīyatāṃ devalokāya yatra bhogāḥ supuṣkalāḥ \|\| 139 \|\|*

*tadvīravāhanaḥ śrutvā papracchasamavartinam \|*  
*na jāne kena puṇyena svargaṃ nayasi māṃ vibho \|\| 140 \|\|*

137-140. The lord who stood along with the deities and gandharvas showed His divine form. The king bowed prostrate and then stood with hands joined in reverence. Delighted in mind, he praised the lord in many ways. Then Dharmaraja praised the king and asked the messengers to take him to Devaloka where many delights exist. Viravahana was surprised. He asked Dharmaraja to tell him what merit he had performed in life that he was brought to heaven by the messengers.

## \|\| 6.141 \|\|

*dharmarāja uvāca \|*  
*tvayā kṛtāni puṇyāni dānaṃ yajñāḥ savistarāḥ \|*  
*mathurāyāṃ vṛṣotsargo vasiṣṭhavacanāt kila \|\| 141 \|\|*

141\. Dharmaraja said: — You have done several pious deeds, given gifts and performed sacrifices and accomplished Vrsotsarga in Mathura at the instance of V asistha.

## \|\| 6.142 \|\|

*dharmaḥ svalpo 'pi nṛpate yadi samyagupāsitaḥ \|*  
*dvijadevaprasādena sa yāti bahuvistaram \|\| 142 \|\|*

142\. О King, even a little dharma if done properly multiplies, due to the favour of brahmanas and deities.

## \|\| 6.143 \|\|

*ityuktvā yamunābhrātā kṣaṇādantardhimāyayau \|*  
*vīrabāhurdivaṃ gatvā devaiḥ saha mumoda ha \|\| 143 \|\|*

143\. Having said thus, Yama disappeared at once. VIrabahu went to heaven and enjoyed there in the company of deities.

## \|\| 6.144 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*mayā te kathitaṃ pakṣin vṛṣayajñaḥ suvistaraḥ \|*  
*prāṇināṃ karmanirhāraṃ śrutvā pāpaiḥ pramucyate \|\| 144 \|\|*

144\. О bird, I have told you in detail all about Vrsotsarga. On hearing this, the people get released from sins which they incur as the result of their bad activities.

## Chapter 7

## \|\| 7.1 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*śrutaṃ me mahādākhyānaṃ vṛṣotsargaphalaṃ hare \|*  
*punaranyāṃ kathāṃ brūhi yatra te mahimādbhutaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Garuda said: — I have heard the story relating to the rite of Vrsotsarga. I wish to be enlightened farther upon this topic, for your knowledge is very great.

## \|\| 7.2 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*ahaṃ te kathayāmyadya saṃvādaṃ paramādbhutam \|*  
*santaptakasya ca pretaistadrūpajñāpanāya vai \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Sri Krsna said: — Now I shall tell you a wonderful dialogue between Santapana and the ghosts on this very point.

## \|\| 7.3 \|\|

*vipraḥ santaptakaḥ kaścit tapasādagdhakilbiṣaḥ \|*  
*saṃsārāsāratāṃ jñātvāraṇyaṣveva cacāra ha \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. There was a Brahmana-Santaptaka by name whose sins had been destroyed by penance. Knowing the futility of the world he left home and went to the forest.

## \|\| 7.4 \|\|

*vaikhānasamunivrātaiḥ prāṇipātakṛtekṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*sa kadācit tīrthayātrāmuddiśya smāṭatidvijaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Whenever Vaikhanasas, sages and Bratas saw him they bowed to him with respect. Once he went on a pilgrimage.

## \|\| 7.5 \|\|

*pratyākṛṣṭendriyatvācca bahirvṛttinirodhakaḥ \|*  
*saṃskāramātragamano mārgabhraṣṭo babhūva ha \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Though he controlled the outer senses and acted in the prescribed way, he was still dragged by the organs and he slipped \*in his path.

## \|\| 7.6-7 \|\|

*calannevaṃ snānakāle madhyāhne 'thābhilāṣukaḥ \|*  
*jalasyonmīlya nayane diśaḥ sarvā nyabhīlayat \|\| 6 \|\|*

*sa dadarśa tadā gulmairvorudvṛkṣaśataiścitam \|*  
*tvaksāraiḥ śākhiśākhābhiḥ saṃkulaṃ gahanaṃvanam \|\| 7 \|\|*

6-7. Once in the mom while he was taking bath, he opened his eyes and looked around. He saw a forest full of shrubs, creepers, trees, barks, branches, etc.

## \|\| 7.8-9 \|\|

*tatra tālāstamālāśca priyālāḥ panasāstathā \|*  
*śrīparṇo śālaśākhoṭasyandanāstindukāstathā \|\| 8 \|\|*

*sarjārjunāmrātakāśca śleṣmā takabhibhītakau \|*  
*picumardaściñciṇī ca karkandhūkarṇikārakāḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

8-9. He saw talas, tamalas, priyalas, panasas, sriparni, salas, sakhotas syandanas, tindukas, sarjas arjunas, amras, slesmatakas, bibhitakas, picumardas, Cinimas, Karkandhus and Karanikaras.

## \|\| 7.10 \|\|

*ete cānye ca bahavo vṛkṣāsteṣu na dṛśyate \|*  
*pakṣiṇāmapi vai panthā manuṣyasya kutaḥ punaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. All these and other trees were there among which the way could not be seen even by the bird?, not to say of men.

## \|\| 7.11-12 \|\|

*tasmin vane mahāghore siṃhavyāghrasamākule \|*  
*tarakṣugavayairṛkṣairmahiṣaiśca niṣevite \|\| 11 \|\|*

*kuñja rairurubhirnāgairmarkaṭaiśca tathāmṛgaiḥ \|*  
*śvāpadaiśca tathā cānyaiḥ piśācai rākṣasairvṛte \|\| 12 \|\|*

11-12. There in that fierce forest, full of lions, tigers, hyenas, wild oxen, bears, buffaloes, elephants, deer, cobras, monkeys and other animals and also demons and goblins,

## \|\| 7.13 \|\|

*santaptako dvijaḥ kiñcidbhayasantrastamānasaḥ \|*  
*kāndiśīkaḥ samabhavaḍh yadbhaviṣyo yayau punaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Santapana was terrified in his heart and could not decide where to go. Then thinking Come what may, he went further.

## \|\| 7.14 \|\|

*jhaṅkāreṣu ca jhillīnāṃ ghūkānāṃ ghūtkṛteṣvapi \|*  
*dattakarṇaḥ kunīlāṅgaścacāla padapañcakam \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Hearing the singing of crickets and the hooting of owls, he moved forward about five steps.

## \|\| 7.15 \|\|

*sa tatra vaṭavṛkṣāgre snāyuvaddhaṃ śavaṃ tathā \|*  
*dadarśa tadbhujaścaiva pañca pretān sudāruṇām \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. There he saw a corpse tied to a banyan tree and five fierce ghosts eating the same.

## \|\| 7.16-17 \|\|

*śirāsthicarmaśeṣāṅgān pṛṣṭhalagnodarān khaga \|*  
*tyaktānnāsikayā netrakūpapātabhayādiva \|\| 16 \|\|*

*sūcīkrakakacakavrātaghātapātitakīkasān \|*  
*vasāktanavamastiṣkasvādanityamahotsavān \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. They were rejoicing over their feast by relishing the head-bones, stomach attached to the back, fallen bones of the body, marrow, brain, etc.

## \|\| 7.18 \|\|

*raṇatkoṭimahādaṃṣṭrānasthigranthyavaghaṭṭitān \|*  
*tāndṛṣṭvā trastahṛdayo gatimākuñcya saṃsthitaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Seeing the ghosts who were loudly cracking the bones with their fierce jaws, he was awe-struck in his heart and stopped at once.

## \|\| 7.19 \|\|

*te vilokyāgataṃ vipramaṭavīṃ janavarjitām \|*  
*ahaṃ pūrvamahaṃ pūrvaṃ yāmītyāktvā pradudruvuḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. When they saw, that Brahmana in that lonely forest, they ran towards him saying 'I first, I first

## \|\| 7.20 \|\|

*teṣu dvaudvāvagṛhṇītāmasya hastāvathāpare \|*  
*dvaudvau pādāvagṛhṇītāṃ mūrdhānaṃ pañcamo 'grahīt \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Two of them caught hold of his two arms, two caught hold of his legs and the fifth one caught hold of his head.

## \|\| 7.21 \|\|

*svajātyucitavākyena sphuṭavarṇavatābruvan \|*  
*ahaṃ jakṣāmyahaṃ bhakṣāmīti karṣaṇatatparāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Speaking loudly in their own language, I shall eat first, I shall cat first', they got busy in dragging him.

## \|\| 7.22 \|\|

*sahasaiva sahaivāmuṃ gṛhātvā vyagamanviyam \|*  
*kiyatsthitaṃ baṭau māṃsaṃ kriyannetinyabhālayan \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Then, all of a sudden, they went up in the sky. From there they looked down how much flesh was left in the corpse.

## \|\| 7.23 \|\|

*te 'paśyannijadaṃṣṭrāyaḥ pāṭitāntramimaṃ śavam \|*  
*avatīrya tato vyomno gṛhītvā caraṇaiḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. They saw, the corpse bitten by their jaws. Then they got down and caught hold of the corpse by legs.

## \|\| 7.24-26 \|\|

*svakhaṇḍitaśarīrantu punarvyomaiva cakramuḥ \|*  
*sa nīyamānamātmānaṃ vilokya viyati dvijaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

*jagāma manasā māṃ sa śaraṇaṃ bhayavihvalaḥ \|*  
*namaścakre cakradharaṃ cetasā cinmayaṃ samam \|\| 25 \|\|*

*vakraṃ nakraṃ cakrapātena dūre kṛtvā hṛtvā tasya duḥ khaṃ mukundaḥ \|*  
*mātaṅgaṃ yo 'mūmucannakravaktrātpāśaṃso 'sau karmaṇāṃ me lunātu \|\| 26 \|\|*

24-26. Thus taking hold of the body cut by themselves, they went up again in the skyThen seeing himself being, borne in the heaven, the Brahmana praised the lord in his mind. "I bow to lord Visnu, the holder of discus who is supreme consciousness, who kept away the crocodile by throwing his discus and released the elephant from the noose of crocodile, May he release me from the noose of my actions.

## \|\| 7.27 \|\|

*ruddhāñśuddhān bhūpatīnmāgadhena bhīmenainaṃ ghātayitvā murāriḥ \|*  
*nirbaddhānyo bhargayajñāya muktaścakro me 'sau karmapāśaṃ lunātu \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. When the kings were captured by Magadha Bhima, the lord got them released so that they might visit Bharga's sacrifice. May he release me from the noose of my activities."

## \|\| 7.28 \|\|

*manasaivaiha māmastautstūyamāno 'hamutthitaḥ \|*  
*agacchaṃ sahasā tatra yatra pretaiḥ sa nīyate \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. He praised me in his mind and being praised I got up all of a sudden and I went to the place where he was being carried by the ghosts

## \|\| 7.29 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā tairnoyamānantu kautukaṃ me 'bhavatkhaga \|*  
*papraccha na kiyantaṃ vai kālaṃ tānpṛṣṭhato 'nvagām \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Seeing him thus carried away by the ghosts I was surprised and without speaking anything I followed them a while.

## \|\| 7.30 \|\|

*mama sannidhimātreṇa dvijātiṃ tañca sarpahan \|*  
*tatkālaṃ śivikāsuptabhūpālasukhamāviśat \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Simply by the dint of my presence, О bird, that Brahmana felt the pleasure of riding a palanquin.

## \|\| 7.31 \|\|

*maṇibhadrāstato meruṃ gacchandṛṣṭo mayā pathi \|*  
*nikocyākṣi svapārśvaṃ sa nīto vai yakṣarāṇmayā \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Then in the way, I saw Manibhadra going to Meru and winking at him I took the king of Yaksas by my side.

## \|\| 7.32 \|\|

*tamavocaṃ mahāyakṣaṃ tvaṃ hi pratibhaṭo bhava \|*  
*pretānnāśaya tadbhūyaḥ śavañca hara tadgatam \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. I said to the lord of Yaksas to be active and destroy the ghosts and take away the corpse.

## \|\| 7.33 \|\|

*ityuktaḥ sa mahāghoraṃ kṛtvā roṣaṃ suduḥ saham \|*  
*jagrāha pretarūpaṃ tatpretānāmapi duḥ khadam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Being instructed thus he took the form of a ghost terrific even to those ghosts.

## \|\| 7.34 \|\|

*sa vivṛtya svakau bāhū sṛkkiṇī parilelihan \|*  
*bhedayannuruvātena pretāṃstānsaṃmukho yayau \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. He stretched his arms besmeared with blood and appeared before the ghosts challenging them.

## \|\| 7.35 \|\|

*bāhubhyāṃ dvau dvau ca padbhyāṃ mūrdhnaikaṃ ca samāharat \|*  
*pretānathāpi sahasā jaghāna dṛḍhamuṣṭinā \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Не struck two with arms, two with legs and one with head and beat the ghosts with severe blows.

## \|\| 7.36 \|\|

*te vivarṇamukhāḥ sarve taṃ dvijañca śavaṃ tathā \|*  
*ekaikaṃ hastapādaiśca gṛhītvā yuddhamārabhan \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. They held that corpse bound by hands and legs and began to fight.

## \|\| 7.37 \|\|

*te nakhaistalaghātaiśca pādaghātaistathaiva ca \|*  
*daṃṣṭrāghātaiśca sarve tamekaṃ pretaṃ vyadārayan \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. They attacked the Yaksa lord with nails, feet and jaws.

## \|\| 7.38 \|\|

*teṣāṃ prahārānviphalānkṛtvā saṃprati tānatha \|*  
*jīvaṃ na tu śavaṃ teṣāṃ jahre prāṇamivāntakaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. But avoiding their attacks, the Yaksa lord snatched the corpse, as death takes away the breath.

## \|\| 7.39 \|\|

*hṛtamātre śave te tu pāriyātre girau dvijam \|*  
*muktvādhamātre pramuditā ekaṃ pretaṃ sudāruṇāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. When the corpse was snatched by Yaksa they ran towards him.

## \|\| 7.40 \|\|

*sa vāyugamanaḥ pretaḥ prāptastaiḥ kṣaṇamātrataḥ \|*  
*adṛśyatāṃ yayau te 'tha hatāśā vipramāgaman \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. As soon as they reached the Yaksa moving in air, the Yaksa vanished immediately. In utter dejection they went to the Brahmana.

## \|\| 7.41 \|\|

*prārabdhamātre viprasya pāṭane tatra parvate \|*  
*mama sthānasya viprasya mahimneva ca tatkṣaṇe \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. As they were going to kill that Brahmana on the mountain they remembered their previous birth. It happened by the glory of my position and by the nobility of the Brahmana.

## \|\| 7.42 \|\|

*sadyaḥ smṛtiḥ samutpannā teṣāṃ pūrvasya janmanaḥ \|*  
*vipraṃ pradakṣiṇīkṛtyadvijarṣabhamathābruvan \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Then they encircled the Brahmana and spoke to him reverentially.

## \|\| 7.43 \|\|

*adya naḥ kṣantumarhe 'sītyuktvā te suradāmbhikāḥ \|*  
*gireriva parāvartaṃ samudrasyeva śoṣaṇam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Please excuse us to day. They spoke like the echo of the mountain or the turmoil of the stormy sea.

## \|\| 7.44 \|\|

*teṣāṃ tadvacanaṃ śrutvāpṛcchatkeyūyamityatha \|*  
*kiṃ māyā kimu vā svapna utāho cittavibhramaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Hearing their words he asked: 'Who are you? Is it simply an illusion, a dream, or a fancy?

## \|\| 7.45-49 \|\|

*pretā ūcuḥ \|*  
*avehi tattvamevaitatpretā vai karmajā vayam \|*  
*brāhmaṇa uvāca \|*  
*kiṃnāmānaḥ kimācārāḥ kathañcemāṃ daśāṃ gatāḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

*avinītāḥ kathaṃ pūrvaṃ vinītāḥ sāmprataṃ katham \|*  
*pretā ūcuḥ \|*  
*śṛṇu viprendra vakṣyāmaḥ praśrānāmanupūrvaśaḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

*uttarāṇi mahāyogiṃstvaddarśanagatāṃhasaḥ \|*  
*ahaṃ paryuṣito nāmnā eṣa sūcīmukhaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

*tṛtīyaḥ śīghragasturyorodhako lekhakaḥ paraḥ \|*  
*brāhmaṇa uvāca \|*  
*pretānāṃ karmajātānāṃ kuto nāma nirarthakam \|\| 48 \|\|*

*niruktimeṣāṃ nāmnāṃ vai pretā vadata mā ciram \|*  
*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*evamuktāstu vipreṇa pṛthaguttaramabruvan \|\| 49 \|\|*

45-49. Hear, О Brahmana, we shall tell you what you have asked us, О Mahayogin, We are absolved of our sins by seeing you. My name is Paryusita. He is Sucimukha. The third one is Shlghra and the fourth one is Rodha and the fifth one is Lekhaka. Why are these meaningless names borne out by you? Can they be derived from actions performed by you? О ghosts, now tell us the meanings of these names. Being thus addressed by the Brahmana, they replied separately.

## \|\| 7.50 \|\|

*paryuṣita uvāca \|*  
*kadācicchrāddhakāle vai mayā vipro nimantritaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Once, in a month, when sraddha is performed for the manes, I invited a Brahmana to my house. He arrived after I had eaten the part of food out of hunger.

## \|\| 7.51-53 \|\|

*sa ca kṛtvā vilambena vṛddho madgṛhamāgataḥ \|*  
*akṛtaśrāddhakarmāhaṃ taṃ pākaṃ bhuktavān kṣudhā \|\| 51 \|\|*

*adadāmannamākṛṣya vipre paryuṣitaṃ kiyat \|*  
*tasmāt pāpānmṛtaḥ pāpo yoniṃ vai kutsitāṃ gataḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

*yataḥ paryuṣitaṃ dattaṃ tataḥ paryuṣitaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*sūcīmukha uvāca \|*  
*kadācidbrāhmaṇī kācittīrthaṃ bhadravaṭaṃ yayau \|\| 53 \|\|*

51-53. Then I gave stale food to that Brahmana when he came. On account of that sin, when I died I became a ghost and got the name Paryusita since I had given him the stale food. Once an aged woman of the Brahmana caste went to the holy place Bhadravata.

## \|\| 7.54-56 \|\|

*pañcavarṣasutā vṛddhā putramātraikajīvitā \|*  
*ahaṃ kṣattriyadāyādastasyā rodhamakāriṣam \|\| 54 \|\|*

*vane tu vijane tatra pāpādhvagagatiṃ gataḥ \|*  
*tasyāḥ savastraṃ pātheyaṃ tatsūnorvasanāni ca \|\| 55 \|\|*

*gṛhītāni mayā vipra śirasyāpīḍya muṣṭinā \|*  
*tṛṣārtastatkṣaṇaṃ bālaḥ pātrasaṃsthaṃ jalaṃpiban \|\| 56 \|\|*

54-56. The old woman lived with her son aged, five years. I being a Ksatriya pretender stopped her in wilderness, became a wayside robber and I took her viaticum with clothes along with the dress of her son. I wrapped them around my head and wanted to leave.

## \|\| 7.57 \|\|

*tāvanmātrodake deśe mayā huṅkṛtya vāritaḥ \|*  
*mayātha sakalaṃ pītaṃ jalaṃ pātrāttṛṣāvatā \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. I saw the little boy drinking water from a jar. In that wilderness, only that much water was there. I frightened the boy from drinking water and being thirsty myself began to drink from the jar.

## \|\| 7.58 \|\|

*bālo 'pi bhayasantrastaḥ pipāsurvyasurāpatat \|*  
*putraśokānmṛtā mātā kūpe prāsya nijaṃ vapuḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. The boy died of thirst and the mother who was struck with grief died too, bythrowing herself into a dry well.

## \|\| 7.59 \|\|

*etasmātpātakādvipra pretatvaṃ prāptavānaham \|*  
*sūcyagraprāyavivaramukhaḥ parvatadehavān \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. О Brahmana, by that sin I became a ghost with mouth as small as the hole of a needle and body as huge as a mountain.

## \|\| 7.60 \|\|

*yadyapi prāpnuyāṃ bhakṣyaṃ bhakṣituntu na śakyate \|*  
*mayā kṣudhānalenāpi jvalatāsyaṃ nikocitam \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. Although I get food I cannot eat. Although I bum with hunger my mouth is contracted.

## \|\| 7.61 \|\|

*ata āsye tu vivaraṃ sūcyagreṇa samaṃmama \|*  
*etasmātkāraṇādvipre nāmnā sūcīmukho 'smyaham \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. Since in my mouth I have a hole equal to that of a needle I am known as Sucimukha.

## \|\| 7.62 \|\|

*śīghraga uvāca \|*  
*purāhaṃ vaiśyajātīyaḥ sākaṃ sakhyā ca kenacit \|*  
*vāṇijyaṃ kartumagamaṃ deśamanyaṃ mahādhanaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. Formerly I was a rich Vaisya and went to a distant country for business.

## \|\| 7.63 \|\|

*mitraṃ ca me bahudhanaṃ tasya lobho mahāṃstataḥ \|*  
*jāto 'pyadṛṣṭavaimukhyānme naṣṭaṃ mūlamapyuta \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. I was accompanied by a friend who was a partner in business. He was rich but greedy. Then due to bad luck we fared badly in business to the extent that even our capital was lost.

## \|\| 7.64 \|\|

*tatastasmāttu niṣkrāntāvāvāṃ nāvātha nimnagām \|*  
*mārgagāṃ tartumārabdhau lohitāyati bhāskare \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. Then we started from there, travelling in a boat. Just as the sun reddened, we began to cross the river.

## \|\| 7.65 \|\|

*sakhā sa ca madutsaṅge suṣvāpādhvaklamākulaḥ \|*  
*abhūttadāti pāpasya krūrā matiratīva me \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. My friend was tired due to labour, slept in my lap. Then a cruel thought entered into my mind.

## \|\| 7.66 \|\|

*tamutsaṅgagataṃ sūre naṣṭe pūre 'kṣipaṃ tadā \|*  
*katkṛtyaṃ kurvato nāvi lokaistu jñātameva na \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. I threw my friend sleeping in my lap into the river. Nobody in the boat knew anything about my act.

## \|\| 7.67 \|\|

*tasya yadvastu tatsarvaṃ maṇimuktādikāñcanam \|*  
*ādāya śīgragastasmāddeśātsvagṛhamāgataḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. I got hold of his belongings, jewels, rubies, gold, etc. and returned home.

## \|\| 7.68 \|\|

*tatsarvaṃ svagṛhe muktvā tasya patnyai nyavedayam \|*  
*dasyubhirme hato bhrātā dhanamācchidya vai pathi \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. I kept that all in my house and told his wirfe: "My brother has been taken away by robbers in the way and robbed of his wealth.

## \|\| 7.69-73 \|\|

*prajāvati pradruto 'haṃ mā rodītyevamabravam \|*  
*śokārtā sāpi tatkālaṃ mamatvaṃ gṛhabandhuṣu \|\| 69 \|\|*

*tyaktvā cāti priyānprāṇāñjuhāvāgnau yathāvidhi \|*  
*tato niṣkaṇṭakaṃ taddhi vīkṣya hṛṣṭo gato gṛham \|\| 70 \|\|*

*abhuñjaṃ sarvamāgatya yāvajjīvaṃ tu taddhanam \|*  
*mitraṃ pūre hi niḥ kṣipya yadahaṃ śīghramāgataḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

*etasmātkāraṇātpretaḥ śīghrago 'haṃ tu nāmataḥ \|*  
*rodhaka uvāca \|*  
*ahantu śūdrajātīyaḥ purābhūvaṃ munīśvara \|\| 72 \|\|*

*rājaprasādāptamahāśatagrāmādhikāravān \|*  
*vṛddhau me pitarāvāstāṃ laghurekaḥ sahodaraḥ \|\| 73 \|\|*

69-73. I ran away and escaped, Do not weep"She was overwhelemed with grief and burnt herself into the fire. Then seeing my path was without obstruction I returned home gladly. I enjoyed my friend's wealth to my heart's content. Since throwing my friend into the river I returned home quickly, I am called Shlghra. Rodhaka said: — О Brahmana, formerly I was a sudra. By the king's favour I owned a hundred villages, I had old parents and a young brother.

## \|\| 7.74 \|\|

*śīghraṃ sa ca mayā bhrātā lubdhenaikaḥ pṛthakkṛtaḥ \|*  
*āptavānparamaṃ duḥkhaṃ sonnavastravivarjitaḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

74\. Very soon my brother was estranged from me by a greedy person. I stopped giving him food and clothes. He suffered too much at my hands.

## \|\| 7.75-79 \|\|

*adattāṃ pitarau cchannaṃ kiñcitkiñcittu tasya ca \|*  
*tasmai pitṛbhyāṃ yaddattamāptebhyastanmayā śrutam \|\| 75 \|\|*

*tatsarvaṃ tattvato jñātvā pitro rodhamakārayam \|*  
*śūnyamandira ekasminbaddhvā tu nigaḍairdṛḍhaiḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

*tatastau jahatuḥ prāṇānduḥ khitau viṣapānataḥ \|*  
*sosau bālo 'pi babhrāma pitṛbhyāṃ rahito dvija \|\| 77 \|\|*

*puraḥ pattanakharvācān kheṭānapi mṛtaḥ kṣudhā \|*  
*etasmātpātakādvipra mṛtaḥ pretatvamāgataḥ \|\| 78 \|\|*

*ruddhau tu pitarau yasmānnāmnāhaṃ rodhakastataḥ \|*  
*lekhaka uvāca \|*  
*ahaṃ vipra purābhūvamavantyāṃ dvijalasattamaḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

75-79. My parents gave him something secretly whatever they gave him I learnt from my flose confidants. Then I bound my paretns withiron chains in a deserted temple. Being miserable they ended their life by drinking poison. They boy who was left all alone wandered here and there and expired ultimately. By this sin, О Brahmana I have become ghost. Since I chained my parents I was called Rodhaka.

## \|\| 7.80 \|\|

*bhadrasya rājño devānāṃ pūjane 'dhikṛto hyaham \|*  
*bahvyastu pratimāstatra babhūvurbahunāmikāḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. Lekhaka said: — Formerly I was a brahmana in Avanti. I was authorised to worship the deities of Bhadra king. There were many images with different names.

## \|\| 7.81 \|\|

*hemnastadaṅgeṣu bahu ratnajātaṃ babhūva ha \|*  
*tāsāṃ me kurvataḥ pūjāṃ pāpā matirajāyata \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. On their bodies they wore gold and jewels. While worshipping them an evil thought entered me.

## \|\| 7.82 \|\|

*akhilaṃ tīkṣṇalohena tāsāmaṅgaṃ viśīrya ca \|*  
*ullekhanañca ratnānāṃ netrādibhyaḥ kṛtaṃ mayā \|\| 82 \|\|*

82\. Piercing their bodies with an iron-rod and jewels. While jewels from their eyes.

## \|\| 7.83 \|\|

*tathākṛtānyathāṅgāni pratimānāṃ nirīkṣya ca \|*  
*netrāṇi ca viratnāni nṛpaścukrodha vahnivat \|\| 83 \|\|*

83\. When the king saw the images in that state and their eyes without jewels, he became inflamed like fire.

## \|\| 7.84 \|\|

*pratijajñe nṛpaḥ paścādeṣa brāhmaṇapuṅgavaḥ \|*  
*ābhyo ratnaṃ suvarṇañca hṛtaṃ yena bhaviṣyati \|\| 84 \|\|*

84\. Then he vowed О Brahmana and said: "Whosoever has stolen gold and jewels from these images, if known will be killed".

## \|\| 7.85-86 \|\|

*jñātaśca sa hi me vadhyo bhaviṣyati na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*ahaṃ tatsakalaṃ jñātvā rātrāvasidharo gṛham \|\| 85 \|\|*

*rājñaḥ praviśya rājānaṃ paśumāramamārayam \|*  
*gṛhītvātha maṇīn svarṇaṃ niśīthe 'haṃ gato 'nyataḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

85-86. Knowing all that, one night, with a sword in hand, I entered the king's palace and struck him dead. I then took jewels and gold and went away at midnight.

## \|\| 7.87-88 \|\|

*vyāghreṇa mahātāraṇye nakhaṭaṅkairviṭaṅkitaḥ \|*  
*lekhanātpratimāyā yanmayā lohena kartitam \|\| 87 \|\|*

*etasmātpātakātpreto lekhako nāmato 'smyaham \|*  
*āsīnnarakabhogānte naḥ pretatvamidaṃ dvija \|\| 88 \|\|*

87-88. Then in the deep forest, a tiger put on me his nails. Since I had incised the images with iron-rod, I was known as Lekhaka.

## \|\| 7.89 \|\|

*brāhmaṇa uvāca \|*  
*saṃjñāstādṛśya ākhyātā yathaitā bhavatā daśāḥ \|*  
*vadantvācāramātraṃ me pretā āhāramapyuta \|\| 89 \|\|*

89\. The Brahmana said: — You have told us facts about your names; now let us know about your way of life as ghosts as well as your food.

## \|\| 7.90-91 \|\|

*pretā ūcuḥ \|*  
*vedamārgānusaraṇaṃ lajjā dharmo damaḥ kṣamā \|*  
*dhṛtirjñānaṃ naiva yatra vayaṃ tatra vasāmahe \|\| 90 \|\|*

*tasya pīḍāṃ vapaṃ kurmo naiva śrāddhaṃ na tarpaṇam \|*  
*yasya gehe tadaṅgāttu māṃsañca rudhiraṃ kramāt \|\| 91 \|\|*

90-91. We stay where people do not follow the Vedas, where there is no feeling of shame for falsehood, no faith in religion, no sense of discipline, no inclination for forgiveness, no patience and no knowledge.

## \|\| 7.92-96 \|\|

*jakṣāmaśca pibāmaśca ukta ācāra eṣa naḥ \|*  
*śṛṇu cāhāramasmākaṃ sarvalokavigarhitam \|\| 92 \|\|*

*dṛṣṭastvayā ca kiñcidvai brūmojñātaṃ tvayānagha \|*  
*vamanaṃ viḍū dūṣikā ca śleṣmā mūtrāśruṇī tathā \|\| 93 \|\|*

*etadbhakṣyañca pānañca mā pṛcchātaḥ paraṃ dvija \|*  
*lajjā no jāyate svāminnāhāraṃ vadatāṃ svakam \|\| 94 \|\|*

*ajñānāstāmasā mandā kāndiśīkā vayaṃ vibho \|*  
*akasmājjanmanāṃ vipra smṛtiḥ prāptā tu paurvikī \|\| 95 \|\|*

*vinītatvāvinītatve jānīmo naiva naḥ prabho \|*  
*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*evaṃ vadatsu preteṣu tathā śrutavati dvije \|\| 96 \|\|*

92-96. We trouble the person who does not perform sraddha or Tarpana. We eat his flesh and suck his blood. Now hear about-our food which is most despicable in the world. Something of this you have already seen. We shall now tell you something unknown to you. Vomiting, waste, cough, urine, and tearsthese we eat and drink. Do not ask us further, О Brahmana, we are ashamed to tell you, out our food. We are ignorant,:in dark, fools, puzzled. Suddenly have we remembered -about our previous births. We are neither humble nor wild and we know nothing. When the ghosts were Speaking thus and the Brahmana was hearing.

## \|\| 7.97-98 \|\|

*adarśayamahaṃ rūpaṃ tadā tārkṣyedameva vai \|*  
*sa tu dṛṣṭvā dvijaśreṣṭho hṛdgataṃ puruṣaṃ puraḥ \|\| 97 \|\|*

*stotraistuṣṭāva pakṣīśa daṇḍavatpraṇanāma mām \|*  
*te 'pi tepustataḥ pretā āścaryotphullacakṣuṣaḥ \|\| 98 \|\|*

97-98. I showed my form, O, Tarkhya. When that Brahmana saw before him the Purusa of his heart, he praised me with hymns and fell prostrate before me. Those ghosts too trembled with eyes wide open in surprise.

## \|\| 7.99-100 \|\|

*praṇayena skhaladvācaḥ khaga nocuḥ kimapyuta \|*  
*rajasā goracittānāṃ tamasā mūḍhacetasām \|*  
*kṛpayā yaḥ samuddhāraṃ kuruṣe vai namo 'sta te \|\| 99 \|\|*

*evaṃ dvijātau bruvati prabhū taprabhaiśca mukhyāṃbaracāriyuktaiḥ \|*  
*tadā madicchāprabhavairvimānaiḥ ṣaḍbhiḥ samantādruruce giriḥ saḥ \|\| 100 \|\|*

99-100. Their voice muttered inrith affection, still they could not speak. "Bow to you who release the cruel from rajas and the stupid from tamos." This being, uttered by the Brahmana that mountain was adorned with six shining aerial cars moving at my will and attended by celestial beings.

## \|\| 7.101 \|\|

*itthaṃ vimānena madīyalokaṃ gato dvijaraso 'pyatha pañcamistaiḥ \|*  
*pretā yayuḥ svargamagaṇyapuṇyaṃ satsaṅgasaṃsargavaśātsuparṇam \|\| 101 \|\|*

101\. By that Vimana the Brahmana went to my abode along with the five ghosts. The ghosts went to heaven by virtue of their association with the Brahmana.

## \|\| 7.102 \|\|

*pretāḥ saṃgavaśena nākamavansantaptako brāhmaṇo viṣvaksana iti prasiddhavibhavo nāmnā gaṇe me 'bhavat \|*  
*etatte sakalaṃ mayā nigāditaṃ yaścaitadutkīrtayedyaścedaṃ śṛṇuyānna so 'pi puruṣaḥ pretatvamāpnoti hi \|\| 102 \|\|*

102\. Living in heaven along with the ghosts that Brahmana Santaptaka became my famous gana called Visvaksena. Thus I have told you everything, О bird. Whoever tells or hears this narrative, О bird, does not become a ghost.

## Chapter 8

## \|\| 8.1 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*svāminkasyādhikāro 'tra sarva evaurdhvadehike \|*  
*kriyāḥ katividhāḥ proktā vadaitatsarvameva me \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. О Lord, now tell me who are authorised to perform obsequies and what are the methods of performance prescribed in the &astras.

## \|\| 8.2 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*putraḥ pautraḥ prapautro vā tadbhrātā bhrātṛsantatiḥ \|*  
*sapiṇḍasantatirvāpi kriyārhāḥ khaga jñātayaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Son, grandson, great-grand-son, his brother and brother's progeny, so also sapinda- progeny-all these are authorized to perform the obsequies. In their absence, samanodaka- progeny of all these can perform the rite.

## \|\| 8.3-4 \|\|

*teṣāmabhāve sarveṣāṃ samānodakasantatiḥ \|*  
*kuladvaye 'pi cocchinne strībhiḥ kāryāḥ kriyāḥ khaga \|\| 3 \|\|*

*icchayocchinnabandhaśca kārayedavanīpatiḥ \|*  
*pūrvāḥ kriyā madhyamāśca tathā caivottarāḥ kriyāḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

3-4. If both the families are maleless, the women should perform the obsequies. Or the king may perform all the rites- the preliminary, the middling and the final. О bird, the Ekoddista sraddha should be performed every year.

## \|\| 8.5-6 \|\|

*pratisaṃvatsaraṃ pakṣinnekoddiṣṭavidhānataḥ \|*  
*śrāddhaṃ tatra prakartavyaṃ phalaṃ tasya śṛṇuṣva me \|\| 5 \|\|*

*brahmendrarudranāsatyasūryāgnivasumārutān \|*  
*viśvedevānpitṛ gaṇānvayāṃsi manujānpaśūn \|\| 6 \|\|*

5-6. Now hear about the fruit accruing from the performance of this sacrifice.

## \|\| 8.7 \|\|

*sarīsṛpānmātṛgaṇānyaccānyadbhūtasaṃjñitam \|*  
*śrāddhaṃ śraddhānvitaḥ kurvanprīṇayatyakhilaṃ jagat \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. When Brahma, Indra; Rudra, Nasatyas, Surya, Agni, Vasu, Maruts, Visvedevas, manes, birds, men, animals, serpents, the Mother deities and other divine beings are given Sraddha with faith, there is rejoicing in the whole world.

## \|\| 8.8 \|\|

*te tṛptāstarpayantyenaṃ putradāradhanaistathā \|*  
*adhikāraḥ kriyābhedaḥ samāsātte nirūpitaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. When they are satisfied they satisfy the performer with, sons, wife and wealth. Thus I have told you, in brief, about the rites and the persons authorized to perform them.

## \|\| 8.9 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*ukteṣveko 'pi cenna syādadhikārī surottama \|*  
*kartavyaṃ kiṃ tadā viṣṇo puruṣeṇa vijānatā \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. О Lord, now tell me when no authorized person is alive, who should conduct the obsequious rites.

## \|\| 8.10 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*adhikāro yadā nāsti yadi nāsti ca niścayaḥ \|*  
*jīvite sati jīvāya dadyācchrāddhaṃsvayaṃ naraḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. If no authorized person is alive to conduct the obsequious rites after death, one should perform the rites in his life time.

## \|\| 8.11 \|\|

*kṛtopavāsaḥ susnātaḥ kṛṣṇāsaṅgaḥ samāhitaḥ \|*  
*kartāramatha bhoktāraṃ viṣṇuṃ sarveśvaraṃ yajet \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. After fasting and bathing he should along with his wife worship Visnu, the lord of all, the Creator as well as the Enjoyer.

## \|\| 8.12 \|\|

*sadakṣiṇāśca satilāstistraśca jaladhenavaḥ \|*  
*nivedayetpitṛbhyaśca svadheti susamāhitaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. With Svadha he should offer Jaladhenus to the manes along with Daksina and gingelly seeds.

## \|\| 8.13 \|\|

*agnaye kavyavāhanāya svadhā nama iti smaran \|*  
*somāyatvā pitṛmate svadhā nama iti smaran \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Reciting the mantra Agnaye Kavyavahandya svadha, namah, Somdya tvd pitrmate svadha namah.

## \|\| 8.14 \|\|

*dakṣiṇena tu dadyācca tṛtīyāṃ dakṣiṇāyutām \|*  
*yamāyāṅgirase cātha svadhā nama iti smaran \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. With right hand he should donate the third one with daksina reciting Yamaydngirase cdtha svadha namah.

## \|\| 8.15-16 \|\|

*tayormadhye tu niḥ kṣipya viprānsaṃmantrya bho jayet \|*  
*prathamāmuttare nyasya dvitīyāṃ dakṣiṇe nyaset \|\| 15 \|\|*

*madhye tṛtīyāṃ vinyasya paścādāvāhanādikam \|*  
*āvāhanādinā pūrvaṃ viśvedevānprapūjyaca \|\| 16 \|\|*

15-16. In between he should invite the brahmanas and feast them. The first should be stationed in north and the second one in the south, the third one in the middle. Before he invokes the other deities, he should worship the Visvedevas.

## \|\| 8.17 \|\|

*vasubhyastvāmahaṃ vipra rudrebhyastvāmahaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*sūryebhyastvāmahaṃ vipra bhojayāmīti tānvadet \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Не should then tell the Brahmana. "O Brahmana, I am feeding you for the propitiation of Vasus, Rudras and Suryas." He should thus tell all the Brahmanas.

## \|\| 8.18 \|\|

*āvāhanādikaṃ śeṣaṃ kuryācca pitṛśeṣavat \|*  
*sāmyāṃ dhenuṃ tato dadyādvasūddeśaṃ dvijāya tu \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. He should then perform avdhana(invocation), etc, as it is performed in the case of manes. He should give Saumya cow to the Brahmana for the propitiation of Vasus.

## \|\| 8.19 \|\|

*āgneyyāṃ cātha raudrāya yāmyāṃ sūryadvijāya tu \|*  
*viśvebhyaścātha devebhyastilapātraṃ nivedayet \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. He should give Agneya cow for the propitiation of Rudra and Yamya cow for the propitiation of Surya. He should give a vessel full of gingelly seeds for the propitiation of Visvedevas.

## \|\| 8.20 \|\|

*svastītyeva tathākṣayyaṃ jalaṃ dattvātha tāndvijān \|*  
*visarjayetsmaranviṣṇuṃ devamaṣṭākṣaraṃ vibhum \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Then uttering svasti and giving sacred water (as that of the Ganges) he should dismiss the Brahmanas (i.e. ask them to go away as they liked), while he himself should set his heart on the eight-syllabled Visnu.

## \|\| 8.21 \|\|

*tataḥ kāmaṃ kuleśānīṃ śivaṃ nārāyaṇaṃ smaret \|*  
*caturdaśyāṃ tato gacchedyathāprāptāṃ saridvarām \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Then he should think of Kama, Kulesani, diva and Narayana. He should go to the nearest river on Caturdasi.

## \|\| 8.22 \|\|

*vastrāṇi lohakhaṇḍāni jitaṃ ta iti saṃjapan \|*  
*dakṣiṇābhimukho vahniṃ jvālayettatra ca svayam \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Muttering the mantra Vastrdm Lohakhanddni jitam to he should light fire facing the south

## \|\| 8.23 \|\|

*pañcāśatā kuśairbrāhmīṃ kṛtvā pratikṛtiṃ dahet \|*  
*hutvā śmāśānikaṃ homaṃ pūrṇāhutyantameva hi \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Then making a Brahni image with fifty Kusas he should burn the same in fire. He should perform the sacrifice usually done in the crematorium and offer the final oblation (Purnahuti).

## \|\| 8.24 \|\|

*niragrimatha vā bhūmiṃ yamaṃ rudrañca saṃsmaret \|*  
*hutvā prādhānike sthāne paścādāvāhayecca tam \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Or he should not kindle fire but simply propitiate Earth, Yama and Rudra. He should invoke each one of them separately and give offering at the appropriate place.

## \|\| 8.25 \|\|

*śrapayeccāparaṃ vahnau mudgamiśraṃ caruṃ tataḥ \|*  
*tilataṇḍulamiśrañca dvitīyaṃ sapavitrakam \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Не should sacrifice caru mixed with mudga into the fire. He should offer sacred thread,.rice and gingelly seeds to a Brahmana.

## \|\| 8.26 \|\|

*oṃ pṛthivyai namastubhyamiti caikaṃ nivedayet \|*  
*oṃ yamāya namaśceti dvitīyaṃ tadanantaram \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. He should say; Om homage to Earth', Orh homage to Yama.

## \|\| 8.27 \|\|

*oṃ namaścātha rudrāya śmāśānapataye namaḥ \|*  
*tato dīpte samiddhe 'gnau bhūmau prakṛtidāruṇe \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Om homage to Rudra, lord of cemetery Then in the fire lit on the earth

## \|\| 8.28-29 \|\|

*saptabhyo yamasaṃjñebhyo dadyātsapta jalāñjalīn \|*  
*yamāya dharmarājāya mṛtyave cāntakāya ca \|\| 28 \|\|*

*vaivasvatāya kālāya sarvaprāṇaharāya ca \|*  
*svadhākāranamaskārapraṇavaiḥ saha saptadhā \|\| 29 \|\|*

28-29. He should give handful of water to Yama, one to each of the seven names of Yama, viz, Yama, Dharma-raja, Mrtyu, Antaka, Vaivas- vata, Kala, Sarva-pranahara, muttering svadha, namah and Orh seven times, i.e. with each of the seven names of Yama.

## \|\| 8.30-31 \|\|

*amukāmukagautraitattubhyamastu tilodakam \|*  
*pradadyāddaśa piṇḍāṃstu arghapuṣpasamanvitān \|\| 30 \|\|*

*dhūpo dīpo balirgandhaḥ sarveṣāmastu cākṣayaḥ \|*  
*daśa piṇḍāṃstu dāndattvā viṣṇoḥ saumyaṃ mukhaṃ smaret \|\| 31 \|\|*

30-31. О you of such and such gotra, this water with gingelly seeds is for you. Ten pindas (rice- balls) should be given along with argha, flowers, incense, lamp, bali and gandha. He should then say: Let everyone be prosperous. After giving the ten pindas he should remember the gentle face of Visnu.

## \|\| 8.32-33 \|\|

*kuryācca māsikaṃ māsi sapiṇḍīkaraṇaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*āśaucānte tataḥ kuryādātmano vā marasya tu \|\| 32 \|\|*

*kuryādasthiratāṃ jñātvā śaktyārogyadhanāyuṣam \|*  
*etatte sarvamākhyātaṃ jīvacchrāddhaṃ mayā khaga \|\| 33 \|\|*

32-33. He should perform this rite spread over for a month. He should do sapindtkarana for himself or others at the end of asauca within a month but not later than that knowing fully well the transitoriness of vitality, health, wealth and age. Thus I have told you all about Jivatsraddha, О bird.

## Chapter 9

## \|\| 9.1 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*uktamādyāṃ kriyāṃ yāvannṛpo 'pītitvayānagha \|*  
*kasyacitkenacidrājñā kimādyā sā kṛtā purā \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. О sinless one, you have just said that a king may also perform the aforesaid rites. Did any king in the past perform such rites for any one?

## \|\| 9.2 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*suparṇa śṛṇu vakṣyāmi yathā rājñā kriyā kṛtā \|*  
*āsīt kṛtayuge rājā vāṅgo vai babhruvāhanaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Hear, О bird, I shall tell you how a king performed such rites (for an issueless deceased). In the Krtayuga, there was a king Babhruvahana by name, in the country of Angas.

## \|\| 9.3 \|\|

*pṛthivyāścaturantāyā goptā pakṣīndra dharmataḥ \|*  
*caturbhāgāṃ bhuvaṃ kṛtsnāṃ sa bhuṅke vasudhādhipaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. О King of birds. He protected the earth on all sides. Following the laws of the land, he enjoyed the earth, having four divisions.

## \|\| 9.4-6 \|\|

*na pāpakṛtkaścidāsīttasminrājyaṃ praśāsati \|*  
*nāsīccaurabhayaṃ tārkṣya na kṣudrabhayameva hi \|\| 4 \|\|*

*nāsīdvyādhibhayañcāpi tasmiñjanapadeśvare \|*  
*svadharme remire cāsīttejasā bhāskaropamaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

*akṣubdhatver'calasamaḥ sahiṣṇutve dharāsamaḥ \|*  
*sa kadācinmahābāhuḥ prabhūtabalavāhanaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

4-6. During his reign, there was no sinner. Fear of thieves or evil-doers did not exist. During his reign there was no fear of any epidemic. He followed the path of religion for the welfare of his people. In lustre he resembled the sun, the earth in stability and the mountain in forbearance. Once, he of long arms, with his ample army and armaments went to a great forest.

## \|\| 9.7 \|\|

*vanaṃ jagāma gahanaṃ hayānāñca śatairvṛtaḥ \|*  
*siṃhanādaiśca yodhānāṃ śaṅkhadundubhiniḥ svanaiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. He was surrounded by hundreds of horses, roars of warriors, sounding of conches and beating of drums.

## \|\| 9.8 \|\|

*āsītkilakilāśabdastasmin gacchatī pārthive \|*  
*tatratatra ca viprendraiḥ stūyamānaḥ samantataḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. As the king proceeded onjourney he was praiesed by the brahmanas and other learned people on the way.

## \|\| 9.9 \|\|

*niryayau parayā prītyā vanaṃ mṛgajighāṃsayā \|*  
*sa gacchandadṛśe dhīmānnandanapratimaṃ vanam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. He went to the forest with great pleasure for hunting. He was delighted to see the forest which resembled the Nandana garden of Indra.

## \|\| 9.10 \|\|

*bilvārkakhadirākīrṇaṃ kapitthadhvajasaṃyutam \|*  
*viṣamaiḥ parvataiścaiva sarvataśca samanvitam \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. It was full of Bilva, Khadira, and Kapitha- dhvaja. It was surrounded by the mountains on all sides.

## \|\| 9.11 \|\|

*nirjalaṃ nirmanuṣyañca bahuyojanamāyatam \|*  
*mṛgasiṃhairmahāghorainyaiścāpi vanecaraiḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. It was devoid of water, devoid of human beings. It spread over may joyanas and abounded in deer, lion, and other fierce wild animals.

## \|\| 9.12 \|\|

*tadvanaṃ manuja vyāghraḥ sabhṛtyabalavāhanaḥ \|*  
*līlayā loḍayāmāsa sūdayanvividhānmṛgān \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Accompanied by his attendants and surrounded by his army he walked into the interior of the forest, killing different animals.

## \|\| 9.13 \|\|

*mṛgasya kasyacitkukṣiṃ tato vivyādha bhūmipaḥ \|*  
*rājā mṛgaprasaṅgena tamanu prāviśadvanam \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Leisurely, he struck a deer in the stomach with his arrow. The deer ran helter-skelter with pain, followed by the monarch.

## \|\| 9.14 \|\|

*ekākī vai hṛtabalaḥ kṣutpripāsāsamanvitaḥ \|*  
*sa vanasyāntamāsādya mahaccāraṇyamāsadat \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. While his army was left behind, he wandered all alone, hungry and thirsty and entered another forest.

## \|\| 9.15 \|\|

*tṛṣayā parayāviṣṭo 'nviṣyajjalamitastataḥ \|*  
*sa dūrātpūracakrāhvaṃ haṃsasārasanāditaiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Then suffering from thirst and seeking for water here and there, he got the indication of a lake from the noise of geese and cranes.

## \|\| 9.16-17 \|\|

*sūcitaṃ sara āgatya sāśva eva vyagāhata \|*  
*padmānāñca parāgeṇa utpalānāṃ rajena ca \|\| 16 \|\|*

*sugandhamamalaṃ śītaṃ pītvāmbho nirjagāma ha \|*  
*mārgaśramapariśrāntastaḍāgataṭamaṇḍapam \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. At last he found a lake where he immersed for bath together with his horse. Mixed with the filament of the lotuses, the water was fragrant, clear and cool. As he was tired of travelling a long way he rested beneath a fig tree on the bank of the lake.

## \|\| 9.18 \|\|

*nyagrodhaṃ vīkṣya tasyāśu jaṭāsvaśvaṃ babandha ha \|*  
*sa tatrāstaramāstīrya kheṭakānupadhāya ca \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. He fastened his horse to the branches of the tree, spread the coverlet and used the clubs as pillows.

## \|\| 9.19-20 \|\|

*sūṣvāpa vāyunā tatra sevyāmātastadā kṣaṇam \|*  
*kṣaṇaṃ supte nṛpe tatra preto vai pretavāhanaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

*kaścidatrājagāmātha yuktaḥ pretaśatena ca \|*  
*asthicarmaśirāśeṣaśarīraḥ parivibhraman \|\| 20 \|\|*

19-20. He slept there for a moment being fanned by air. Even as he slept for a moment a certain kingly ghost came there along with hundreds of attendant ghosts. It was a remnant of bones, skin and sinews.

## \|\| 9.21 \|\|

*bhakṣyaṃpeyaṃ mārgamāṇo na badhnāti dhṛtiṃ kvacit \|*  
*tamapūrvaṃ nṛpo dṛṣṭvākarodastraṃ śarāsane \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. He was moving here and there, in search of food and drink. He did not rest even for a while. On seeing such a terrible ghost, the king put an arrow on his bow.

## \|\| 9.22-24 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā so 'pi ciraṃ bhūpaṃ tasthau sthāṇupivāgrataḥ \|*  
*tamavasthitamālokya rājā prāptakutūhalaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*papraccha tañca ko 'sīti kuto vā vikṛtiṃ gataḥ \|*  
*preta uvāca \|*  
*pretabhāvo mayā tyakto gatiṃ prāpto 'ramyahaṃ parām \|\| 23 \|\|*

*tvatsaṃyogānmahābāho nāsti dhanyataro mayā \|*  
*babhruvāhana uvāca \|*  
*kimetatdipine ghore sarvatrātibhayānake \|\| 24 \|\|*

22-24. The ghost looked at the king for a long time and stood before him like a branchless post. The king got curious at the sight of the ghost and put the question to him thus: Who are you? How did you get his deformity? The ghost replied: — I have just left my ghosthood and attained a diffemt man. The ghost looked at the king for a long time and stood before him like a branchless post.

## \|\| 9.22-24a \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā so 'pi ciraṃ bhūpaṃ tasthau sthāṇupivāgrataḥ \|*  
*tamavasthitamālokya rājā prāptakutūhalaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*papraccha tañca ko 'sīti kuto vā vikṛtiṃ gataḥ \|*  
*preta uvāca \|*  
*pretabhāvo mayā tyakto gatiṃ prāpto 'ramyahaṃ parām \|\| 23 \|\|*

*tvatsaṃyogānmahābāho nāsti dhanyataro mayā \|*  
*babhruvāhana uvāca \|*  
*kimetatdipine ghore sarvatrātibhayānake \|\| 24 \|\|*

22-24. The king got curious at the sight of the ghost and put the question to him thus: Who are you? How did you get this deformity? The ghost replied: I have just left my ghosthood and attained a different form, just at the sight of you, О long-armed one. None is more fortunate than myself.

## \|\| 9.25-26 \|\|

*dodhūyamāne vātena vātyārūpeṇa koṇapa \|*  
*pataṅgā maśakāḥ kṣudrāḥ kavandhāśca śirāṃsi ca \|\| 25 \|\|*

*matsyāḥ kūrmāḥ kṛkalāsā vṛścikā bhramarāhayaḥ \|*  
*adhomukhordhvapādāste krandamānāḥ sudāruṇam \|\| 26 \|\|*

25-26. In this fierce forest, shaken with wind and cyclone, full of flies and mosquitoes, trunks and head, fish, tortoise, lizards, scorpions, bees, serpents are seen with their mouth bent downwards and their feet raised upwards.

## \|\| 9.27 \|\|

*pravānti vāyavo rūkṣā jvalanto vidyudagnayaḥ \|*  
*itastato bhramantīva vāyunā tṛṇasantatiḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Dry winds are blowing; lightning is flashing; grass is scattered here and there.

## \|\| 9.28 \|\|

*dṛśyante vividhā jīvā nāgāśca śalabhavrajāḥ \|*  
*śrūyante bahudhā rāvā na dṛśyante kvacitkvacit \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. I see many creatures — Nagas and Salabhas. I hear many sounds at some places and at some places I have no sounds at all. At the sight of all this deformity my heart trembles overwhelmingly.

## \|\| 9.29-32 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvedaṃ vikṛtaṃ sarvaṃ vepate hṛdayaṃmama \|*  
*prate uvāca \|*  
*yeṣāṃ naivāgnisaṃskāro na śrāddhaṃ nodakakriyāḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

*ṣaṭ piṇḍā daśa gātrāṇi sapiṇḍīkaraṇaṃ na hi \|*  
*viśvāsaghātino ye ca surāpāḥ svarṇahāriṇaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

*mṛtā durmaraṇādye ca ye cāsūyāparā janāḥ \|*  
*prāyaścittavihīnā ye agamyāgamane ratāḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

*karmabhirbhrāmyamāṇāste prāṇinaḥ svakṛtairiha \|*  
*durlabhāhārapānīyā dṛśyante pīḍitā bhṛśam \|\| 32 \|\|*

29-32. Those for whom the rites of cremation, funeral offering, such as the offering of riceball, gingelly-water are not performed nor the rites of dasa-pindas, dasagatras nor sapindlkarana, who daring their life were ungrateful, drunkards goldstealers. who died by accident, who were envious, without atonement, attached to women of ill repute, take the form of ghost and suffer for their bad actions through want of food and water.

## \|\| 9.33 \|\|

*eteṣāṃ kṛpayā rājaṃstvaṃ kuruṣvaurdhvadehikam \|*  
*yeṣāṃ na mātā na pitā na putro na ca bāndhavāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. О king, please perform their funeral rites since they do not have mother, father, sons and relations.

## \|\| 9.34-35 \|\|

*teṣā rājā svayaṃ kuryātkarmāṇi tu yato nṛpaḥ \|*  
*ātmanaśca śubhaṃ karma kartavyaṃ pāralaukikam \|\| 34 \|\|*

*vimuktaḥ sarvaduḥ khebhyo yenāñjo durgātiṃ taret \|*  
*bhrātaraḥ kasya ke putrāstriyo 'pi svārthakovidāḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

34-35. О king, you perform this good deed for their welfare, so that theymay be released from misery and may cross the ocean of distress. Of what avail are the brothers and sons? One should not rely on women for they are expert in selfishness.

## \|\| 9.36 \|\|

*na kāryasteṣu viśrambha svakṛtaṃ bhujyateyataḥ \|*  
*gṛheṣvarthā nivarnttante śmaśāne caiva bāndhavāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. A person reaps what he sows. All objects or belongings remain at home. The relatives turn back from the cemetery.

## \|\| 9.37 \|\|

*śarīraṃ kāṣṭhāmādatte pāpaṃ puṇyaṃ saha vrajet \|*  
*tasmādāśu tvayā samyagātmanaḥ śreya icchatā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Body is consumed by fire; good and evil deeds alone accompany the deceased.

## \|\| 9.38 \|\|

*asthireṇa śarīreṇa kartavyañcaurdhvadohikam \|*  
*rājovāca \|*  
*kṛśarūpaḥ karālākṣastvaṃ preta iva lakṣyase \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Therefore, for your welfare, you perform immediately the funeral rites of the kinless deceased, as you are mortal yourself.

## \|\| 9.39 \|\|

*kathayasvaḥ mama prītyā pretarāja yathātatham \|*  
*tathā pṛṣṭaḥ sa vai rājñā uvāca sakalaṃ svakam \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. With your emaciated form and fierce book, you seem to be a ghost. О king of ghosts, now, tell me all about yourself in order to relieve my anxiety. Thus asked, the king of ghosts began to tell all about himself.

## \|\| 9.40 \|\|

*preta uvāca \|*  
*kathayāmi nṛpaśreṣṭha sarvamevāditastava \|*  
*pretatve kāraṇaṃ śrutvā dayāṃ kartumihārhasi \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. О best of kings, I shall tell you all about myself from the very start. After hearing the causes that obtained ghosthood for me, you will have sympathy for me.

## \|\| 9.41 \|\|

*vaidiśaṃ nāma nagaraṃ sarvasampatsukhāvaham \|*  
*nānājanapadākīrṇaṃ nānāratnasamākulam \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. There is a city Vidisa by name, full of pleasure and prosperity. It has many janapadas and abounds in abundant treasure.

## \|\| 9.42 \|\|

*nānāpuṣpavanākīrṇaṃ nānāpuṇyajanāvṛtam \|*  
*tatrāhaṃ nyavasaṃ bhūpa devārcanarataḥ sadā \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. It has forests of flowers where dwell the holy sages. I too lived there, О king, engaged in the worship of gods.

## \|\| 9.43 \|\|

*vaiśyajātiḥ sudevo 'haṃ nāmnā viditamastu te \|*  
*havyena tarpitā devāḥ kavyena pitaro mayā \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. I belong to the Vaisya caste, and my name is Sudeva. I propitiated the deities with oblations and the manes with sraddhas.

## \|\| 9.44 \|\|

*vividhairdānayogaiśca viprāḥ santarpitā mayā \|*  
*āhāraśca vihāraśca mayā vai suniveśitaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. I presented gifts to the Brahmanas and donated food.

## \|\| 9.45 \|\|

*dīnānāthaviśiṣṭebhyo mayā dattamanekadhā \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ niṣphalaṃ jātaṃ mama daivādupāgatam \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. I gave money and precious articles to the poor and the needy. But by bad luck all that went in vain.

## \|\| 9.46 \|\|

*na me 'sti santatistāta na suhṛnna ca bāndhavāḥ \|*
