41\. The pindas are associated in their serial order with their presiding deities, viz., Brahma, Visnu, and the messengers of Yama.

## \|\| 15.42 \|\|

*datte tṛtīye piṇḍe 'smindehadoṣaiḥ pramucyate \|*  
*ādhārabhūtajīvaścajvalanairjvālayeccitām \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. When the third pinda is offered, the dead body is relieved of impurity. The son or the nearest relative should set fire to the pyre.

## \|\| 15.43 \|\|

*saṃmṛjya copalipyātha ullikhyoddhṛtya vedikām \|*  
*abhyukṣyopasamādhāya vahniṃ tatraḥ vidhānataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. He should cleanse, besmear, dig up, extract the place of altar and after sprinkling the same with water, he should arrange sticks in the prescribed way and light the fire.

## \|\| 15.44-45 \|\|

*puṣpākṣataiśca sampūjya devaṃ kravyādasaṃjñakam \|*  
*tvaṃ bhūtakṛjjagadyone tvaṃ lokaparipālakaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

*upasaṃhārakastasmādenaṃ svargaṃ mṛtaṃ naya \|*  
*iti kravyādamabhyarcya śarīrāhutimācaret \|\| 45 \|\|*

44-45. After worshipping the deity Kravyada with "owers and rice he should recite the following formula: "O Kravyada, the originator of beings, the source of the universe, thou art the originator, saviour and the destroyer of the people. Take this dead person to heaven." Thus worshipping the deity Kravyada, he should sacrifice the dead body.

## \|\| 15.46 \|\|

*ardhadagdhe tathā dehe dadyādājyāhutiṃ tataḥ \|*  
*lomabhyaḥ svotivākyena kuryāddhomaṃ yathāvidhi \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. When the body is half-burnt he should pour ghee into the pyre reciting the formula 'Lomabhyah svaha'

## \|\| 15.47 \|\|

*citāmāropya taṃ pretaṃ hunedājyāhutiṃ tataḥ \|*  
*yamāya cāntakāyeti mṛtyave brahmaṇe tathā \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. Putting dead body on the pyre he should pour ghee over the pyre with the formula: Yamaya Antakaya Svaha.

## \|\| 15.48 \|\|

*jātavedomuke deyā ekā pretamukhe tathā \|*  
*ūrdhvaṃ tu jvālayedvahniṃ pūrvabhāge citāṃ punaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. A single offering should be made to each of the deities — Mrtyu, Brahma, Jata vedas (fire) as well as in the mouth of the preta. Then he should light the fire from the eastern direction.

## \|\| 15.49 \|\|

*asmāttvamadhijātosi tvadayaṃ jāyatāṃ punaḥ \|*  
*asau svargāya lokāya svāhā jvalati pāvakaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. He should recite the mantra (addressed to the dead); thou art born of this fire;' (addressed to the fire): May he be born of you again. May thou take him to the world of heaven, please.

## \|\| 15.50 \|\|

*evamājyāhutiṃ dattvā tilamiśrāṃ samantrakām \|*  
*tato dāhaḥ prakartavyaḥ putreṇa kila niścitam \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Reciting this mantra he should pour ghee mixed with gingelly seeds. Thus, he should cremate the body as dictated by the funeral code (antyesti paddhati).

## \|\| 15.51 \|\|

*roditavyaṃ tato gāḍhamevaṃ tasya sukhaṃ bhavet \|*  
*dāhasyānantaraṃ tatra kṛtvā sañcayanakriyām \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. He should weep aloud at this juncture. For this would assuredly give pleasure to the deceased. The rite of cremation is followed by the rite of.collecting bones.

## \|\| 15.52 \|\|

*pretapiṇḍaṃ pradadyācca dāhārtiśamanaṃ khaga \|*  
*tāvadbhūtāḥ pratīkṣante taṃ pretaṃ bāndhavārthinam \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. The preta-pinda forestalls the pain due to cremation. The Bhutas (who share the pinda with the deceased) allow the preta to wait til! he receives the pinda from his relatives.

## \|\| 15.53 \|\|

*dāhasyānantaraṃ kāryaṃ putraiḥ snānaṃ sacailakam \|*  
*tilodakaṃ tato dadyānnāmagotreṇa tiṣṭhatu \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. After the corpse is cremated, the sons or the descendants should bathe in dress. They should offer libations' of water mixed with gingelly seeds by his personal name or by the appellation of his lineage.

## \|\| 15.54 \|\|

*tato janapadaiḥ sarvairdātavyā karatālikā \|*  
*viṣṇurviṣṇuriti brūyādguṇaiḥ pretamudīrayet \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. The people of the town who have assembled for morning should applaud the dead by reciting the formula Visnu, Visnu. They should accompany the relatives back to the house (as a token of sympathy for the relatives).

## \|\| 15.55-56 \|\|

*janāḥ sarve samāstasya gṛhamāgatya sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*dvārasya dakṣiṇe bhāge gomayaṃ gaurasarṣapān \|\| 55 \|\|*

*nidhāya varuṇaṃ devamantardhāya svaveśmani \|*  
*bhakṣayennimbapatrāṇi ghṛtaṃ prāśya gṛhaṃ vrajet \|\| 56 \|\|*

55-56. At the southern part of the house he should put cow-dung and the white mustard. He should set up an icon of Varuna in the house, eat the leaves of the Nimba and partake of the ghee.

## \|\| 15.57 \|\|

*keciddugdhena siñcanti citāsthānaṃ khageśvara \|*  
*aśrupātaṃ na kurvīta dadyādasmai jalāñjalīn \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. О lord of birds, some sprinkle milk over the pyre. He should not shed tears but offer libations of water to the dead.

## \|\| 15.58 \|\|

*śleṣmāśru bāndhavairmuktaṃ preto bhuṅkte yato 'vaśaḥ \|*  
*ato na roditavyaṃ hi kriyāḥ kāryāḥ svaśaktitaḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. They should not weep. If they weep and spit phlegm the sou! of the dead person is compelled to eat that.

## \|\| 15.59 \|\|

*dugdhaṃ ca mṛnmaye pātre toyaṃ dadyāddinatrayam \|*  
*sūrye cāstaṃ gate tārkṣya valabhyāṃ catvare 'pi vā \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. The son should perform the obsequial rites as his means can afford. О Garuda, he should pour milk or water by the earthern jar, in favour of the deceased, over the sloping roof or the courtyard.

## \|\| 15.60 \|\|

*baddhaḥ saṃmūḍhahṛdayo dehamicchankṛtānugaḥ \|*  
*śmaśānaṃ catvaraṃ gehaṃ vīkṣanyāmyaiḥ sa nīyate \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. The soul of the deceased held fast by his previous actions and terribly bewildered, desires for another body. While he is being taken to the abode of Yama by his messengers he casts a mournful look at the cemetery and the courtyard of his house.

## \|\| 15.61-62 \|\|

*garte piṇḍā daśāhaṃ ca dātavyāśca dinedine \|*  
*jalāñjalīḥ pradātavyāḥ pretamuddiśya nityaśaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

*tāvadvṛddhiśca kartavyā yāvatpiṇḍaṃ daśāhikam \|*  
*puttreṇa hi kriyā kāryā bhāryayā tadabhāvataḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

61-62. For ten days, without intermittence, the son should offer pinda in the pit and pour handful of water in favour of the deceased. If he has no son, his wife should perform the obsequial rites.

## \|\| 15.63 \|\|

*tadabhāve ca śiṣyeṇa tadabhāve sahodaraḥ \|*  
*śmaśāne cānyatīrthe vā jalaṃ piṇḍaṃ ca dāpayet \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. If he has no wife, his disciple should perform the same. If no disciple, his brother should do that. The libations of pinda and water should be given either at the cremation ground or at the place of pilgrimage.

## \|\| 15.64 \|\|

*odanāni ca saktūṃśca śākamūlaphalādinā \|*  
*prathame 'hani yaddadyāttaddadyāduttare 'hani \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. Whatever the desceudent of fers-boiled rice, barley-meal, vegetables, fruits, etc. on the day of death, he should partake of the same on successive days.

## \|\| 15.65 \|\|

*dināni daśa piṇḍāṃśca kurvantyatra sutādayaḥ \|*  
*pratyahaṃ te vibhajyante caturbhāgāḥ khageśvara \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. The son or the descendent offers pinda for ten days consecutively. The pinda is divided every day into four parts, О lord of birds.

## \|\| 15.66 \|\|

*bhāgadvayaṃ tu dehārthaṃ prītidaṃ bhūtapañcake \|*  
*tṛtīyaṃ yamadūtānāṃ caturthaṃ copajīvyati \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. Two parts of the pinda go to the building of new body. The third part goes to the messengers of Yama. The fourth he partakes of himself.

## \|\| 15.67 \|\|

*ahorātraistu navabhiḥ preto niṣpattimāpnuyāt \|*  
*jantorniṣpannadehasya daśame valavatkṣudhā \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. Within three days and nights the soul assumes a new body. On the tenth day the embodied soul longs for food.

## \|\| 15.68 \|\|

*na vidhirnaiva mantraśca na svadhāvāhanāśiṣaḥ \|*  
*nāma gotraṃ samuccārya yaddattaṃ taddaśāhikam \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. No procedure, no mantra and no rites are prescribed. He should offer gifts to the deceased just by his personal name or by the appellation of his lineage,

## \|\| 15.69-71 \|\|

*dagdhe dehe punardehamevamutpadyate khaga \|*  
*prathame 'hani yaḥ piṇḍastena mūrdhā prajāyate \|\| 69 \|\|*

*grīvā skandhau dvitīye ca tṛtīye hṛdayaṃ bhavet \|*  
*caturthena bhavetpṛṣṭhaṃ pañcame nābhireva ca \|\| 70 \|\|*

*ṣaṭ saptame kaṭī guhyamṛrū cāpyaṣṭame tathā \|*  
*tālū pādau ca navame daśame 'hni kṣudhā bhavet \|\| 71 \|\|*

69-71. О bird, when the dead body is cremated, the soul that has gone out of the body takes a new body. With the pinda of the first day his head comes into being; with the pinda of the second day his neck and shoulders; with that on the third day his heart; with that on the fourth day his back; with that on the fifth day his navel; with those on the sixth and seventh days his waist and private parts; with that on the eighth his thighs; with that on the ninth his palate and feet;, with that on the tenth a feeling of hunger comes into being.

## \|\| 15.72 \|\|

*dehaṃ prāptaḥ kṣudhāviṣṭo gṛhe dvāre ca tiṣṭhati \|*  
*daśame 'hani yaḥ piṇḍastaṃ dadyādāmiṣeṇa tu \|\| 72 \|\|*

72\. Having assumed a new body tormented by hunger he stands at the threshold of his house. The tenth day pinda should be accompanied with the offering of flesh.

## \|\| 15.73 \|\|

*yato dehe samutpanne preto 'tīva kṣudhānvitaḥ \|*  
*atastvāmiṣabāhyena kṣudhā tasya na naśyati \|\| 73 \|\|*

73\. The soul of the deceased when he had assumed a new body feels extremely hungry. His hunger is not appeased if an offering without flesh is made to him.

## \|\| 15.74-75 \|\|

*ekādaśe dvādaśāhe preto bhuṅkte dinadvayam \|*  
*yoṣitaḥ puruṣāsyāpi pretaśabdaṃ samuccaret \|\| 74 \|\|*

*dīpamannaṃ jalaṃ vastraṃ yatkiñcidvastu dīyate \|*  
*pretaśabdena taddeyaṃ mṛsyānandadāyakam \|\| 75 \|\|*

74-75. On the eleventh day and the twelfth, the soul of the dead eats to his fill. While offering gifts of lamp, food, water etc. to the male or the female deceased one should mutter the name preta. Whatever is offered to the deceased, should be done by the name preta, a for, doing so gives delight to the deceased.

## \|\| 15.76 \|\|

*trayodaśe 'hni sa preto nīyate ca mahāpathe \|*  
*piṇḍajaṃ dehamāśritya divā naktaṃ bubhukṣitaḥ \|\| 76 \|\|*

76\. On the thirteenth day, the soul of the dead is taken to the High Way. Now, he assumes a body born of the pinda and feels hungry by day and night.

## \|\| 15.77 \|\|

*śītoṣṇaśaṅkukravyādavahnimārgastu pāpinām \|*  
*kṣudhā tṛṣṇātmikā caiva savva saumyaṃ kṛtātmanām \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. The sinners travel by the path of extreme cold and heat — the path which is beset with nails, infested with demons and spread over with fire. Hunger and thirst await the sinner but the good escape torments.

## \|\| 15.78 \|\|

*mārge caitāni duḥ khāni asipatravanānvite \|*  
*kṣutpipāsārdito nityaṃ yamadṛtaiḥ prapīḍitaḥ \|\| 78 \|\|*

78\. In the path beset with trees, with their leaves as sharp as swords, such tortures are usual. He suffers from hunger and thirst, tortured by the messengers of Yama.

## \|\| 15.79 \|\|

*ahanyahani vai preto yojanānāṃ śatadvayam \|*  
*catvāriṃśattathā sapta ahorātreṇa gacchati \|\| 79 \|\|*

79\. The departed soul traverses two hundred and forty-seven Yojanas in twentyfour hours.

## \|\| 15.80 \|\|

*gṛhīto yamapāśaiśca hāheti rudite tu saḥ \|*  
*svagṛhaṃ tu parityajya yāmyaṃ puramanuvrajet \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. He is bound by the noose of Yama. He weeps as he leaves the house for the city of Yama.

## \|\| 15.81 \|\|

*krameṇa yāti sa pretaḥ puraṃ yāmyaṃ śubhāśubham \|*  
*atītya tānitānyeva mārge puravarāṇi ca \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. The departed soul goes to the city of Yama which the pious souls find comfortable and where the sinners feel distressed.

## \|\| 15.82-83 \|\|

*yāmyaṃ sauripuraṃ nagendrabhavanaṃ gandharvaśailāgamau \|*  
*kroñcaṃ krūrapuraṃ vicitrabhavanaṃ bahvāpadaṃ duḥ khadam \|\| 82 \|\|*

*nānākrandapuraṃ sutaprabhavanaṃ raudraṃ payovarṣaṇaṃ śītāḍhyaṃ bahudharmabhītabhavanaṃ yāmyaṃ puraṃ cāgrataḥ \|\| 83 \|\|*

82-83. In his upward journey he passes over the best of cities viz., Yamya, Sauripura, Nagendra- bhavana, Gandharva, Sailagama Kraunca, Krurapura, Vicitrabhavana, Bahvapada, Duhk- hada, Nanakrandapura, Sutaptabhavana, Raudra, Payovarsana, Shltadhya and Bahubhlti. Yama's city heads the list.

## \|\| 15.84 \|\|

*trayodaśe 'hni sa preto gṛhīto yamakiṅkaraiḥ \|*  
*tasminmārge brajatyeko gṛhīta iva markaṭaḥ \|\| 84 \|\|*

84\. On the thirteenth day seized by the servants of Yama, and all alone, the departed soul traverses the path like a monkey led by the juggler.

## \|\| 15.85-86 \|\|

*tathava sa vrajanmārge putraputreti ca bruvan \|*  
*hāheti krandate nityaṃ kīdṛśaṃ tu mayā kṛtam \|\| 85 \|\|*

*mānuṣyaṃ labhyate kasmāditi brūte prasarpati \|*  
*mahatā puṇyayogena mānuṣyaṃ janma labhyate \|\| 86 \|\|*

85-86. As he goes along the path, he cries aloud repeating:"0 my son, О my son, I am undone, alas, I am undone. I did not act well." He laments, crying over and over again: Human body is rare to get. I got it by my accumulated merit.

## \|\| 15.87-88 \|\|

*na tatprāpya pradattaṃ hi yācakebhyaḥ svakaṃ dhanam \|*  
*parādhīnaṃ tadabhavaditi brūte (rauti) sagadgadaḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

*kiṅkaraiḥ pīḍyate 'tyarthaṃ smarate pūrvadaihikam \|\| 88 \|\|*

87-88. I earned sufficient wealth but gave nothing to the poor. Alas! that wealth has gone to others (in spite of hoarding it). Thus he speaks in the choked voice. He is severely tortured by the servants of Yama while he remembers his past.

## \|\| 15.89 \|\|

*sukhasya duḥ khasya na kopi dātā paro dadātīti kubuddhireṣā \|*  
*purā kṛtaṃ karma sadaiva bhujyate dehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 89 \|\|*

89\. None gives him pleasure or pain. This is wrong to presume that pleasures or pains are caused by others. The soul suffers for all that he performs Tin his life. О soul, now suffer the consequences of your past activities.

## \|\| 15.90 \|\|

*mayā na dattaṃ na hutaṃ hutāśane tapo na taptaṃ himaśailagahvare \|*  
*na sevitaṃ gāṅgamaho mahājalaṃ dehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 90 \|\|*

90\. \[He laments:\] I neither gave gifts to the needy nor poured oblations into the fire. I neither practised penance in the caves of the Himalayas nor bathed in the Ganges. О soul, now suffer the cousequences of your activities.

## \|\| 15.91 \|\|

*na nityadānaṃ na gāvāhnikaṃ kṛtaṃ na vedadānaṃ na ca śāstrapustakam \|*  
*purā nadṛṣṭaṃ na ca sevito 'dhvā dehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 91 \|\|*

91\. I neither gave regular gifts nor made any pastures nor offered sacred books: the Vedas and the Dharmasastras to the learned Brahmins in charity nor heard the Puranas. Now, О soul, suffer for your activities.

## \|\| 15.92 \|\|

*jalāśayo naiva kṛto hi nirjale manuṣyahetoḥ paśupakṣihetave \|*  
*gotṛptihetorna kṛtaṃ hi gocaraṃ dehinkvacinnastara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 92 \|\|*

92\. I did not construct a tank for satiating thirsty people, beasts or birds. I did not make a pasture for the cattle to graze. Now, О soul, suffer for your activities.

## \|\| 15.93 \|\|

*mayā na bhuktaṃ patisaṅgasaukhyaṃ vahnipraveśo na kṛto mṛte sati \|*  
*tasminmṛte tadvratapālanaṃ vā dehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 93 \|\|*

93\. I neither enjoyed the pleasures of my husband's company, nor did I enter the pyre of my husband. Nor did I observe the vow of charity after his death. О soul, now suffer for your activities.

## \|\| 15.94 \|\|

*māsopavāsairna viśoṣitaṃ vapuścāndrāyaṇairvā niyamaiśca saṃhataiḥ \|*  
*nārīśarīraṃ bahuduḥ khabhājanaṃ labdhaṃ mayā pūrvakṛtairvikarmabhiḥ \|\| 94 \|\|*

94\. I did not reduce my body by keeping monthly fasts or Candrayana vow or observances of like nature. I derived womanhood — the cause of multiple sorrows, from unholy activities of my former life.

## \|\| 15.95 \|\|

*uktāni vācyāni mayā narāṇāmataḥ śṛṇuṣvāvahito 'pi pakṣin \|*  
*strīṇāṃ śarīraṃ pratilabhya dehī bravīti karmāṇi kṛtāni pūrvam \|\| 95 \|\|*

95\. О bird, you think attentively on what I have aaid about the lamentations of men and women for their acts of omission and commission done in their previous life

## Chapter 16

## \|\| 16.1-2 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*evaṃ vilapatastasya pretasyaivaṃ khageśvara \|*  
*krandamānasya nitarāṃ pīḍitasya ca kiṅkaraiḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

*saptadaśa dinānyeko vāyumārge vikṛṣyate \|*  
*aṣṭādaśe tvahorātre pūrvaṃ yāmyapuraṃ vrajet \|\| 2 \|\|*

1-2. О lord of birds, when the departed soul laments thus, he cries being struck by Yama's servants who drag him for seventeen days in the firmament. On the eighteenth day he reaches the city of Yama.

## \|\| 16.3 \|\|

*tasminpuravare ramye pretānāṃ ca gaṇo mahān \|*  
*puṣpabhadrā nadī tatranyagrodhaḥ priyadarśanaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. In that beautiful city there abides a group of departed Souls. The river Puspabhadrd flows there and a big fig tree grows up there.

## \|\| 16.4-6 \|\|

*pure sa tatra viśrāmaṃ prāpyate yamakiṅkaraiḥ \|*  
*jāyāputrādikaṃ saukhyaṃ smarettatra suduḥ khitaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

*rudate karuṇairvākyai stṛṣārtaḥ śramapīḍitaḥ \|*  
*svadhanaṃ svakalatrāṇi gṛhaṃ putrāḥ sukhāni ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

*bhṛtyamitrāṇi cānyacca sarvaṃ śocati vai tadā \|*  
*kṣudhārtasya pure tasminkiṅkaraistasya cocyate \|\| 6 \|\|*

4-6. There in that city, the.servants of Yama bid him take rest- In utter distress he remembers pleasures he enjoyed in the company of his wife and sons. Thirsty and distressed he weeps bitterly and laments over the erstwhile comforts of his wealth, wife, house, sons, servants, friends and comforts of life. He is extremely distressed with hunger. As he laments the servants of Yama speak to him.

## \|\| 16.7 \|\|

*kiṅkarā ūcuḥ \|*  
*kva dhanaṃ kva sutā jāyā kva gṛhaṃ kva tvamīdṛśaḥ \|*  
*svakarmopārjitaṃ bhuṅkṣva ciraṃ gaccha mahāpathe \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Where is your wealth? Where arc your sons? Where is your wife? Where are you in this wretched state? Now suffer for your past evil actions and go by this ancient path.

## \|\| 16.8 \|\|

*jānāsi śaṃbalavaśaṃ balamadhvagānāṃ no śaṃbalaḥ prayatate paralokapāntha \|*  
*gantavyamasti tava niścitameva tena mārgeṇa yatra bhavataḥ krayavikrayau na \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. О traveller in this foreign world, do you not know-the power of Yama who is the lord of us all-his messengers. Assuredly, you have to travel over that path where there is no provision for barter in virtue or vice.

## \|\| 16.9 \|\|

*yamadūtoditaṃ vākyaṃ pakṣinnaivaṃ tvayā śrutam \|*  
*evamuktastataḥ sarvairhanyamānaḥ sa mudgaraiḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. О bird, hear attentively what the messengers of Yama speak to the departed soul. They strike him with the rod of iron.

## \|\| 16.10 \|\|

*atra dattaṃ sutaiḥ pātre (traiḥ) snehādvā kṛpayātha vā \|*  
*māsikaṃ piṇḍamaśrāti tataḥ sauripuraṃ vrajet \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Here, ontheearth, whatever is given by his sons every month, rice-ball, etc., due to affection or kindness, he eats the same and then he goes to Sauripura.

## \|\| 16.11 \|\|

*tatra nāmnā tu rājā vai jaṅgamaḥ kālarūpadhṛk \|*  
*taṃ dṛṣṭvā bhayabhītastu viśrāme kurute matim \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. King Jangama rules in that city who can assume any shape at will. He is frightened by his looks. He feels the urge of taking rest.

## \|\| 16.12-13 \|\|

*udakaṃ cānnasaṃyuktaṃ bhuṅkte tasminpure gataḥ \|*  
*traipakṣike tu yaddattaṃ tatpuraṃ sa vyatikramet \|\| 12 \|\|*

*nagendranagare ramye preto yāti divāniśam \|*  
*gacchanvanāni raudrāṇi dṛṣṭvā krandati tatra saḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

12-13. Whatever is offered to him in the three fortnights together with the oblations of water he eats and drinks. He passes over that city and reaches the beautifui town Nagendra by name. He has to travel day and night for two months for reaching that city. He passes over dreadful forests in the way and cries aloud.

## \|\| 16.14-15 \|\|

*bhīṣaṇaiḥ kliśyamānastu rudate ca punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*māsadvayāvasāne tu tatpuraṃ so 'tigacchati \|\| 14 \|\|*

*bhuktvā cānnaṃ jalaṃ pītvā yaddattaṃ vāndhavairiha \|*  
*kliśyamānastataḥ pāśairnoyate yamakiṅkaraiḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

14-15. He is beaten by the cruel and merciless messengers of Yama and weeps over and over again. Having eaten the rice-balls and drunk the libation of water offered by the relatives in the second month, he moves further. He is tied with the noose and dragged by the messengers of Yama.

## \|\| 16.16 \|\|

*tṛtīye māsi samprāpte gandharvanagaraṃ śubham \|*  
*tṛtīyaṃ māsikaṃ bhuktvā tatra gacchatyasau puraḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. He reaches Gandharvanagara (or Gandha- madana) in the third month. Here, he eats the quarterly rice-ball offered by the relations. Then he moves further.

## \|\| 16.17 \|\|

*śailāgamaṃ caturthe sa māse prāpnoti vai puram \|*  
*pāṣāṇāstatra varṣanti pretasyopari saṃsthitāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. He reaches the city Sailagama in the 'fourth month. The stones rain upon him continuously, О lord.

## \|\| 16.18 \|\|

*caturthamāsike śrāddhaṃ bhukte tatra sukhī bhavet \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. There he eats the fourth monthly oblation and feels satisfied.

## \|\| 16.19 \|\|

*tato yāti puraṃ pretaḥ krūraṃ māse tu pañcame \|*  
*iha dattaṃ sutairbhuṅkte preto vai tatpure sthitaḥ \|*  
*ṣaṣṭhe māsi tataḥ preto yāti krauñcābidhaṃ puram \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. In the fifth month, he goes to Krurapura. There he eats the rice ball offered by his relatives. In the sixth month he reaches the Krauncapura.

## \|\| 16.20 \|\|

*tatra dattena piṇḍena śrāddhenāpyāyitaḥ pure \|*  
*muhūrtārdhaṃ tu viśramya kampamānaḥ suduḥ khitaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. There he feeds upon the rice-ball offered by his relatives in the sixth month. He takes rest for a while but all the time he remains frightened and distressed.

## \|\| 16.21 \|\|

*tatpuraṃ sa vyatikramya tarjito yamakiṅkaraiḥ \|*  
*prayāti citranagaraṃ vicitro yatra pārthivaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. He passes over that town being struck and dragged by the messengers of Yama. He reaches Citranagara where rules King Vicitra.

## \|\| 16.22-23 \|\|

*yamasyaivānujaḥ sauriryatra rājyaṃ praśāsti hi \|*  
*māsaistu pañcabhiḥ sārdhairūpaṣāṇmāsikaṃ bhavet \|\| 22 \|\|*

*ūnaṣāṇmāsikaṃ tatra bhuṅkte yāmyasamāhataḥ \|*  
*mārge punaḥ punastasya bubhukṣā pīḍayatyalam \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. He is the younger brother of Yama. There he eats the sixth monthly rice-ball but is not satiated fully. Then he moves further; he suffers again and again for want of food.

## \|\| 16.24 \|\|

*santiṣṭhate mṛte ko 'pi madīyaḥ sutabāndhavaḥ \|*  
*saukhyaṃ yo me janayati patataḥ śokasāgare \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. "Do my sons, brothers, parents or relatives exist who may take me outf of the ocean of distress wherein I have fallen."

## \|\| 16.25-28 \|\|

*evaṃ mārge vilapati vāryamāṇaśca kiṅkaraiḥ \|*  
*āyānti saṃmukhāstatra kaivartāstu sahasraśaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

*vayaṃ te tartukāmāya mahāvaitaraṇīṃ nadīm \|*  
*śatayojanavistīrṇāṃ pūyaśoṇitasaṃkulām \|\| 26 \|\|*

*nānājhaṣasamākīrṇāṃ nānāpakṣigaṇairvṛtām \|*  
*vayaṃ tvāṃ tārayiṣyāmaḥ sukheneti vadanti te \|\| 27 \|\|*

*antaraṃ dehi bho pāntha bahulā cedrucistava \|*  
*tena tatra pradattā gaustayā nāvā prasarpati \|*  
*manujānāṃ hitaṃ dānamante vaitaraṇī smṛtā \|\| 28 \|\|*

25-28. He laments in the way and is warned by the messengers of Yama. He then, reaches the Vaitarani that flows over hundred Yojanas. It is full of pus and blood, abounds in fish and vultures. Here the fishermen approach him saying, "O traveller, give us liberal fee; we shall row you across the river." If he has gifted the Vaitarani cow he is rowed across the river' The gift of a cow at the time of death is called Vaitarani which gives relief to the departed soul.

## \|\| 16.29 \|\|

*parāpāpaṃ dahetsarvaṃ viṣṇulokaṃ ca sā nayet \|*  
*na dattā cetkhagaśreṣṭha tāṃ sametya samajjati \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. The gift of Vaitarani cow destroys his sins and takes him to the region of Visnu. О best of birds, if the Vaitarani cow is not gifted, the departed soul is drowned in that stream.

## \|\| 16.30 \|\|

*svasthāvasthe śarīre 'tra vaitaraṇyā vrataṃ caret \|*  
*deyā ca viduṣe dhenustāṃ nadīṃ tartumicchatā \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. When a person is in good health he should gift a cow to a learned person.

## \|\| 16.31-32 \|\|

*avadanmajjamānastu nindatyātmānamātmanā \|*  
*pātheyārthaṃ mayā kiñcinna pradattaṃ dvijāyaca \|\| 31 \|\|*

*na dattaṃ na hutaṃ japtaṃ na snātaṃ na kṛtaṃ stutam \|*  
*yādṛśaṃ karma caritaṃ mūḍha bhuṅkṣveti tādṛśam \|\| 32 \|\|*

31-32. While drowning he reproaches himself: T gave no food to a Brahmin traveller nor poured oblations in the fire nor performed Japa nor undertook bath nor prayed to the gods. Now, let me suffer for the acts I did in my life.

## \|\| 16.33 \|\|

*tadaiva hṛdi saṃmūḍhastāḍito bhāṣate bhaṭaiḥ \|*  
*vaitaraṇyāḥ parataṭe bhuṅkte dattaṃ ghaṭādikam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. The messengers of Yama strike him again. He repeats those words but in silence this time.

## \|\| 16.34 \|\|

*ūnaṣāṇmāsikaśrāddhaṃ bhuktvā gacchati cāgrataḥ \|*  
*tārkṣya tatra viśeṣeṇa bhojayīta dvijāñchubhān \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. He eats the sixth monthly offering-made by the relatives and proceeds further. О Garuda, the gift of food to the pious Brahmins gives relief to the donor in distress.

## \|\| 16.35 \|\|

*catvāriṃśattathā sapta yojanāni śatadvayam \|*  
*prayāti pratyahaṃ tārkṣya ahorātreṇa karśitaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. О bird, the departed soul covers two hundred and forty seven Yojanas every day. Thereafter he is completely exhausted.

## \|\| 16.36 \|\|

*saptame māsi samprāpte puraṃ bahvāpadaṃ vrajet \|*  
*tatra bhuktva pradattaṃ yacchrāddhaṃ saptamamāsikam \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. In the seventh month he reaches the city. Bahvapada. He eats the rice-ball offered by his relatives.

## \|\| 16.37 \|\|

*aṣṭame māsi samprāpte nānākrandapuraṃ vrajet \|*  
*nānākrandagaṇāndṛṣṭvā krandamānānsudāruṇam \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. In the eighth month he reaches Nanakranda- pura. There he sees people crying bitterly aloud.

## \|\| 16.38 \|\|

*svayaṃ ca śūnyahṛdayaḥ samākrandati duḥ khitaḥ \|*  
*tanmāsikaṃ ca yacchrāddhaṃ bhuktvā tatra sukhī bhavet \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Himself in utter distress, he cries in pain. He eats the eighth-monthly rice-ball and feels comfortable.

## \|\| 16.39 \|\|

*vihāya tatpuraṃ preto yāti taptapuraṃ prati \|*  
*sutaptanagaraṃ prāpya navame māsi so 'śnute \|*  
*dvijabhojyaṃ piṇḍadānaṃ kṛtaṃ śrāddhaṃ sutena yat \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. He, then, leaves for Taptapura. Having reached Taptapura in the ninth month he eats the rice-ball and the sraddha which his son or relatives have gifted in his favour.

## \|\| 16.40 \|\|

*māsi vai daśame prāpte raudraṃ sthānaṃ sa gacchati \|*  
*daśame māsi yaddattaṃ tadbhuktvā ca prayāti saḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. In the tenth month he reaches Raudrapura. He eats whatever his son or relatives give in his favour.

## \|\| 16.41 \|\|

*daśaikamāsikaṃ bhuktvā payovarṣaṇamṛcchati \|*  
*meghāstatra pravarṣanti pretānāṃ duḥ khadāyakāḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. After eating the tenth monthly rice-ball in Raudrapura he goes to Payovarsana where the clouds rain heavily and cause distress to the departed.

## \|\| 16.42 \|\|

*(tataḥ pracalito poto bahurghamatṛṣārditaḥ) \|*  
*dvādaśe māsi yacchrāddhaṃ tatra bhuṅkte suduḥ śitaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Then suffering from heat and thirst he partakes of the eleventh-monthly meal gifted by his relatives.

## \|\| 16.43 \|\|

*kiñcinnyūne tato varṣe sārdhe caikādaśe 'tha vā \|*  
*yāti śītapuraṃ tatra śītaṃ yatrātiduḥ khadam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. A little before a year has passed or at the end of eleven and a half month he reaches a ltapura-city of extreme cold and distress.

## \|\| 16.44 \|\|

*śītārtaḥ kṣudhitaḥ so 'tha vīkṣate hi diśo daśa \|*  
*tiṣṭhettu bāndhavaḥ ko 'pi yo me duḥkhaṃ vyapohati \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Tormented by cold and hunger he looks in all directions and specks: О "I wish I had a relative who would have removed my distress."

## \|\| 16.45 \|\|

*kiṅkarāstaṃ vadantyevaṃ kva te puṇyaṃ hi tādṛśam \|*  
*śrutvā teṣāṃ tu tadvākyaṃ hā daiva iti bhāṣate \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. The attendants of Yama speak to him thus: "Where is thy holy merit that it may give relief to thee." On hearing their words he cries: 'O my fate.

## \|\| 16.46 \|\|

*daivaṃ hi pūrvasukṛtaṃ tanmayā naiva sañcitam \|*  
*evaṃ sañcintya bahuśo dhairyamālambate punaḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Fate is nothing but a result of accumulated merit or sin. T did no good acts, hence this trouble'-pondering over the matter thus, he takes up courage for the time being.

## \|\| 16.47 \|\|

*catvāriṃśadyojanāni caturyuktāni vai tataḥ \|*  
*dharmarājapuraṃ ramyaṃ gandharvāpsarākulam \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. At the distance of forty four Yojanas from Sltapura, there is a beautiful city of Dharmaraja (Yama) where live the celestial musicians and the heavenly nymphs.

## \|\| 16.48 \|\|

*caturaśītilakṣaiśca mūrtāmūrtairadhiṣṭhitam \|*  
*trayodaśa pratīhārā dharmarājapure sthitāḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. There live eightyfour lacs of people in human and divine forms. The guards are put at the thirteen gates of the city.

## \|\| 16.49-52 \|\|

*śubhāśubhaṃ tu yatkarma te vicārya punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*śravaṇā brahmaṇaḥ putrā manuṣyāṇāṃ ca ceṣṭitam \|*  
*kathayanti tadā loke pūjitāḥ pūjitāḥ svayam \|\| 49 \|\|*

*naraistuṣṭaiśca puṣṭaiśca yatproktaṃ ca kṛtaṃ ca yat \|*  
*sarvamāvedayanti sma citragupte yame ca tat \|\| 50 \|\|*

*dūrācchravaṇavijñānā dūrāddarśanagocarāḥ \|*  
*evañceṣṭāstu te hyaṣṭau svarbhūpātālacāriṇaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

*teṣāṃ patnyastathai vogrā śravaṇyaḥ pṛthagāhvayāḥ \|*  
*evaṃ teṣāṃ śaktirasti yartye martyādhikāriṇaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

49-52. There abide honourable and respectable ravanas, the sons of Brahma who know and report to Citragupta whatever good or bad actions are performed by the mortals.

The Havanas are eight in number. They move about in heaven, hell and on earth. They can see and hear from afar. Their women are known as Shravanls who are identifiable by their individual names. They are the presiding deities of mortals and have full knowledge of their activities.

## \|\| 16.53 \|\|

*vratairdānaistavairyaśca pūjayediha mānavaḥ \|*  
*jāyante tasya te saumyāḥ sukhamṛtyupradāyinaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. A mortal should worship them with vows, gifts and prayers. They become cordial to him and cause death in an easy manner.

## Chapter 17

## \|\| 17.1-2 \|\|

*śrīgaruḍamāhāpurāṇam- 17*  
*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*eko me saṃśayo deva hṛdaye samprabādhate \|*  
*śramaṇāḥ kasya putrāśca kathaṃ yamapure sthitāḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

*mānuṣaiśca kṛtaṃ karma kasmājjānanti te prabho \|*  
*kathaṃ śṛṇvanti te sarve kasmājjñānaṃ samāgatam \|\| 2 \|\|*

1-2. Garuda said: — О lord, I want to know about the parentage of Havanas. How are they stationed In the city of Yama? How do they know about the activities of mortals? How do they hear what the people talk about among themselves? From what source is that knowledge derived by them?

## \|\| 17.3 \|\|

*kutra bhuñjanti deveśa krathayasva prasādataḥ \|*  
*pakṣirājavacaḥ śrutvā bhagavānvākyamabravīt \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. О lord, where do they eat? Favour me with your answers to my queries. On hearing the words of the king of birds, the lord spoke.

## \|\| 17.4 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇuṣva vacanaṃ satyaṃ sarveṣāṃ saukhyadāyakam \|*  
*tadahaṃ kathayiṣyāmi śravaṇānāṃ viceṣṭitam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Sri Krsna said: — Now hear, what I am going to speak about Havanas is both truthful and pleasant. I shall tell you about the activities of Havanas.

## \|\| 17.5 \|\|

*ekībhūtaṃ yadā sarvaṃ jagatsthāvarajaṅgamam \|*  
*kṣīrodasāgare pūrvaṃ mayi supte jagatpatau \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. When the entire world of moveables and immoveables became one uniform whole and I, the lord of beings, was reposing in sleep in the milky ocean.

## \|\| 17.6-7 \|\|

*nābhisthojastapastepe varṣāṇi subahūnyapi \|*  
*ekībhūtaṃ jagatsṛṣṭaṃ bhūtagrāmacaturvidham \|\| 6 \|\|*

*brahmaṇā nirmitaṃ pūrvaṃ viṣṇunā pālitaṃ tadā \|*  
*rudraḥ saṃhāramūrtiśca nirmito brahmaṇā tataḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

6-7. The self-born Brahma seated in my navel- lotus practised penance for many years. The universe consisting of fourfold creation was reduced to a single entity-the universe which is created by Brahma, preserved by Visnu and annihilated by Rudra,

## \|\| 17.8 \|\|

*vāyuḥ sarvagataḥ sṛṣṭaḥ sūryastejobhivṛddhimān \|*  
*dharmarājastataḥ sṛṣṭaścitraguptena saṃyuta) \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Brahma created the all-pervading wind. He created the refulgent sun. He created Yama along with Citragupta.

## \|\| 17.9 \|\|

*sṛṣṭvaitadādikaṃ sarvaṃ tapastepe tu padmajaḥ \|*  
*gatāni bahuvarṣāṇi brahmaṇo nābhipaṅkaje \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. He created the universe afterwards, and practised penance for many years, sitting in the navel-lotus of Visnu.

## \|\| 17.10-11 \|\|

*yoyo hi nirmitaḥ pūrvaṃ tattatkarma samācaret \|*  
*kasmiṃścitsamaye tatra brahmā lokasamanvitaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

*rudro viṣṇustathā dharmaḥ śāsayanti vasundharām \|*  
*na jānīmo vayaṃ kiñcillokakṛtyamihocyatām \|\| 11 \|\|*

10-11. He created the universe and assigned duties. The gods were anxious to know what time the creator created the universe, how Rudra, Visnu and Dharma ruled over the earth.

## \|\| 17.12 \|\|

*iti cintāparāḥ sarve devā vimamṛśustadā \|*  
*saṃcintya brahmaṇo mantraṃ vibudhaiḥ preritastadā \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Thus lost in anxiety, the gods pondered, over the Problem. They discussed the matter with Brahma.

## \|\| 17.13-14 \|\|

*gṛhītvā puṣpapatrāṇi sosṛjaddvādaśātmajān \|*  
*tejorāśīnviśālākṣānbrahmaṇo vacanāttu te \|\| 13 \|\|*

*yoyaṃ vadati lokesmiñchubhaṃ vā yadi vāśubham \|*  
*prāpayanti tataḥ śīghraṃ brahmaṇaḥ karṇagocaram \|\| 14 \|\|*

13-14. Brahma who was urged by the gods created twelve sons by the chemical process out of flowers and leaves. They possess lustrous, wide eyes. At the behest of Brahma, they carry all tidings of Man's activities whether good or bad to the ears of Brahma.

## \|\| 17.15 \|\|

*dūrācchravaṇavijñānaṃ dūrāddarśanagocaram \|*  
*sarve śṛṇvanti yatpakṣiṃstenaiva śravaṇā matāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. They can hear from afar imd see far-off things. О bird, they are, therefore, called Havanas.

## \|\| 17.16 \|\|

*sthitvā caiva tathākāśe jantūnāṃ ceṣṭitaṃ ca yat \|*  
*tajjñātvā dharmarājāgre mṛtyukāle vadanti ca \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. They roam about in the firmament and watch the activities of the people which they reveal to Yama at the time of their death.

## \|\| 17.17 \|\|

*dharmaṃ cārthaṃ ca kāmaṃ ca mokṣaṃ ca kathayanti te \|*  
*eko hi dharmamārgaśca dvitīyaścārthamārgakaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. They also declare on the fitness of he departed soul for a particular goal, due to his activities.

## \|\| 17.18 \|\|

*aparaḥ kāmamārgaśca mokṣamārgaścaturthakaḥ \|*  
*uttamā dhamamārgeṇa vainateya prayānti hi \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. There are four paths in man's life, viz., paths of Dharma, Artha, Kama and Moksa/ О son of Vinata, a man can go by any one of these paths, superior or inferior.

## \|\| 17.19 \|\|

*arthadātā vimānaistu aśvaiḥ kāmapradāyakaḥ \|*  
*haṃsayuktavimānaiśca mokṣākāṅkṣī visarpati \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. One whobestows riches goes by a celestial car. One who fulfils wishes rides the horse. One who desires liberation travels by swan-vehicles.

## \|\| 17.20 \|\|

*itaraḥ pādacāreṇa tvasipatravanāni ca \|*  
*pāṣāṇaiḥ kaṇṭakaiḥ kliṣṭaḥ pāśabaddho 'tha yāti vai \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. A sinful person walks bare-footed on rows of sword-edged leaves. He is struck with stones, pierced with thorns and bound by nooses.

## \|\| 17.21-22 \|\|

*yaḥ kaścinmānuṣe loke śravaṇānpūjayediha \|*  
*vardhanyā jalapātrema pakvānnaparipūrṇayā \|\| 21 \|\|*

*śravaṇānpūjayettatra mayā saha khageśvara \|*  
*tasyāhaṃ tatpradāsyāmi yatsurairapi durlabham \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. He who in the world of mortals propitiates Havanas, offering them water from a jar and food cooked in a vessel, worships Havanas and me. О lord of birds, I shall grant him that position which is inaccessible even to the immortals.

## \|\| 17.23 \|\|

*saṃbhojya brāhmaṇān bhaktyā tvekādaśa śubhāñchucīn \|*  
*dvādaśaṃ sakalatraṃ ca mama prītyai prapūjayet \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. With devotion he should feed eleven Brahmanas who are pious and virtuous and the twelfth together with his wife, just for my propitiation.

## \|\| 17.24 \|\|

*devaiḥ sarvaiśca saṃpūjya svargaṃ yānti sukhepsayā \|*  
*taiḥ pūjitairaha tuṣṭaścitraguptena dharmarāṭ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Men should worship Sravanas together with the gods. By doing so, they who seek for pleasure can get to heaven. By worshipping Havanas they propitiate me, Citragupta and Yama.

## \|\| 17.25-26 \|\|

*taistuṣṭairmatpuraṃ yānti lokā dharmapārāyaṇāḥ \|*  
*śravaṇānāṃ ca māhātmyamutpattiṃ ceṣṭitaṃ śubham \|\| 25 \|\|*

*śṛṇoti pakṣiśārdūla sa ca pāpairna lipyate \|*  
*iha loke sukhaṃ bhuktvā svargaloke mahīyate \|\| 26 \|\|*

25-26. With Sravanas pleased, the pious people go to Visnuloka. О best of birds, whosoever hears about the glory pf Sravanas, their origin and pious activities is not covered up by sins. He enjoys pleasures of this world and attains glory hereafter.

## Chapter 18

## \|\| 18.1-2 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*śravaṇānāṃ vacaḥ śrutvā kṣaṇaṃ dhyātvā punastataḥ \|*  
*yatkṛtaṃ tu manuṣyaiścapuṇyaṃ pāpamaharniśam \|\| 1 \|\|*

*tatsarvaṃ ca parijñāya citragupto nivedayet \|*  
*citraguptastataḥ sarvaṃ karma tasmai vadatyatha \|\| 2 \|\|*

1-2. Sri Krsna said: — On hearing the words of Sravanas and thinking about the problem for a while, Citragupta informs about all those actions pious or impious performed by the people. He repeats them also to the person concerned.

## \|\| 18.3 \|\|

*vācaiva yatkṛtaṃ karma kṛtaṃ caiva tu kāyikam \|*  
*mānasaṃ ca tathā karma kṛtaṃ bhuṅkte śubhāśubham \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Whatever action a person undertakes, by word of mouth, body or mind, whether good or bad, he reaps the fruits thereof.

## \|\| 18.4 \|\|

*evaṃ te kathitastārkṣya pretamārgasya nirṇayaḥ \|*  
*viśrānti dāni sarvāṇi sthānāni kathitāni te \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. О lord, thus I have told you about the path which the departed soul has to traverse. I have also told you about the places of rest for the departed soul.

## \|\| 18.5 \|\|

*tamuddiśya dadātyannaṃ sukhaṃ yāti mahādhvani \|*  
*divā rātrau tamuddiśya sthāne dīpaprado bhavet \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. If food is gifted in his favour he travels in comfort on the High Way. To illuminate the path, an earthen lamp should be kindled, put in the appropriate place and kept burning by day and night.

## \|\| 18.6 \|\|

*andhakāre mahāghore śvapūrṇe lakṣyavarjite \|*  
*dīpte 'dhvani ca te yānti dīpo dattaśca yairnaraiḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. The path, though dark, dreadful, doginfested and void of resting-place, becomes luminous and pleasant for those who have gifted an earthen lamp.

## \|\| 18.7 \|\|

*kārtike ca caturdaśyāṃ dīpadānaṃ sukhāya vai \|*  
*atha vakṣyāmi saṃkṣepādyamamārgasya niṣkṛtim \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. If an earthen, jar is,gifted on the fourteenth day or the month of Karttika, it gives comfort to the. deceased. Now, I shall tell, you in brief about the path of Yama for the departed soul.

## \|\| 18.8 \|\|

*vṛṣotsargasya puṇyena pitṛlokaṃ sa gacchati \|*  
*ekādaśāhapiṇḍena śuddhadeho bhavettataḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Her goes to the world of manes as a result of the.merit accruing from the performance of Vrsotsarga. By the rice-ball offered on the eleventh, he attains physical purity,

## \|\| 18.9 \|\|

*udakumbhapradānena kiṅkarāstṛptimāpnuyuḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. The servants of Yama are satiated by the gift of water-jar.

## \|\| 18.10 \|\|

*śayyādānādbimānastho yāti svargeṣu mānavaḥ \|*  
*tadahni dīyate sarvaṃ dvādaśāhe viśeṣataḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. By gifting bed. the deceased goes to heaven by the aerial car. The gift of bed can be made on any day or on the twelfth day from death.

## \|\| 18.11 \|\|

*padāni sarvavastūni variṣṭhāni trayodaśe \|*  
*yo dadāti mṛtasyeha jīvannapyātmahetave \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. On the thirteen day he should give precious articles of different varieties in favour of the deceased or the person himself, when alive, should gift these articles for himself.

## \|\| 18.12-13 \|\|

*tadāśrito mahāmārge vainateya sa gacchati \|*  
*eka evāsti sarvatre vyavahāraḥ khagādhipa \|\| 12 \|\|*

*uttamādhamamadhyānāṃ tattadāvarjanaṃ bhavet \|*  
*yāvadbhāgyaṃ bhavedyasya tāvanmārge 'tiricyate \|\| 13 \|\|*

12-13. О Garuda, by the merit accruing from these gifts he goes comfortably on that path. О lord of birds, there prevails a uniform law for all: the higher, lower and the middling classes. О bird, you should know that a person reaps the fruits, sour or sweet, in proportion to the nature and extent of actions done by him.

## \|\| 18.14 \|\|

*svayaṃ svasyena yaddattaṃ tattatrādhikaroti tam \|*  
*mṛte yadbāndhavairdattaṃ tadāśritya sukhī bhavet \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Whatever gifts he or his relatives make in his favour stand in good stead while he is on journey to, the world of Yama

## \|\| 18.15 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*kasmātpadāni deyāni kiṃvidhāni trayodaśa \|*  
*dīyate kasya deveśa tadvadasva yathātatham \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Garuda said: — О lord, why should they give padas and of what nature.. Who shall receive the gifts? О god of gods, let me know the facts.

## \|\| 18.16 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*chattropānahavastrāṇi mudrikā ca kamaṇḍaluḥ \|*  
*āsanaṃ bhājanaṃ caiva padaṃ saptavidhaṃ smṛtam \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Sri Bhagawan said:-An umbrella, shoes, clothes, a ring, a water-jar, a chair or stool and a vessel-these seven are called padas.

## \|\| 18.17 \|\|

*ātapastatra yo raudro dahyate yena mānavaḥ \|*  
*chatradānena succhāyā jāyate pretatuṣṭidā \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. There is a terrible heat in the way which scorches the departed soul. By the gift of umbrella, the deceased receives shelter of shade and feels comfortable.

## \|\| 18.18 \|\|

*asipatravanaṃ ghoraṃ so 'tikrāmati vai dhruvam \|*  
*aśvārūḍhāśca gacchanti dadate ya upānahau \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. By the gift of shoes the deceased passes over the sword-edged forest, mounted on the horse.

## \|\| 18.19 \|\|

*āsane svāgate (bhojane) caiva dattaṃ tasmai dvijāyate \|*  
*sukhena bhuṅkte sa pretaḥ pathi gacchañchanaiḥ śanaiḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. By the gift of a seat (asana) to a Brahmin, the deceased obtains tasty meals in the interval of time while he walks over the path at his leisure..

## \|\| 18.20 \|\|

*bahudharmasamākīrṇe nirvāte toyavarjite \|*  
*kamaṇḍalupradānena sukhī bhavati niścitam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. By the gift of a water-jar, he gets enough water on the way which is full of heat, devoid of air and water.

## \|\| 18.21 \|\|

*mṛtoddeśena yo dadyādudapātraṃ tu tāmrajam \|*  
*prapādānasahasrasya tatphalaṃ so 'śnute dhruvam \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. A person, gifting a water-jar made of copper, receives, assuredly, the merit of constructing a thousand wells.

## \|\| 18.22 \|\|

*yamadūtā mahāraudrāḥ karālāḥ kṛṣṇapiṅgalāḥ \|*  
*na pīḍayanti dākṣiṇyādvastrābhāraṇadānataḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. The messengers of Yama are dreadful to look at, dark and yellow is appearance. They are courteous too, if propitiated by the gift of clothesand ornaments. When satisfied they do not torn-lent the deceased.

## \|\| 18.23 \|\|

*sāyudhā dhāvamānāśca na mārge dṛṣṭigocarāḥ \|*  
*prayānti yamadūtāste mudrikāyāḥ pradānataḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. They wield terrible weapons, run here and there. By the gift of a ring they vanish from the presence of the deceased.

## \|\| 18.24 \|\|

*bhājanāsanadānena āmānnabhojanena ca \|*  
*ājyayajñopavītābhyāṃ padaṃ sampūrṇatāṃ vrajet \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The pada is completed by the gift of a vessel, a seat, raw food, clarified butter and a sacred thread.

## \|\| 18.25 \|\|

*evaṃ mārge gacchamānastṛṣārtaḥ śramapīḍitaḥ \|*  
*mahiṣīratha (dugdha) dānācca sukhī bhavati niścitam \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Tired and thirsty, the traveller feels comfortable if he has gifted the buffalo's milk in his life-time.

## \|\| 18.26 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*mṛtoddeśena yatkiñciddīyate svagṛhe vibho \|*  
*sa gacchati mahāmārge taddattaṃ kena gṛhyate \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Garuda said: — О lord, things are gifted by the relatives at home in favour of the deceased. How do they reach the deceased and who receives them?

## \|\| 18.27 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*gṛhṇāti varuṇo dānaṃ mama haste prayacchati \|*  
*ahaṃ ca bhāskare deve bhāskarātso 'śnute sukham \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Sri Krsna said: — God Vanina receives those gifts and hands them over to me. I give them to god Bhaskara (the sun), from Bhdskara the deceased person obtains them.

## \|\| 18.28 \|\|

*vikarmaṇaḥ prabhāveṇa vaṃśacchede kṣitāviha \|*  
*sarve te narakaṃ yānti yāvatpāpasya saṃkṣayaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. As a result of sinful activities when no descendant is left on the earth, the deceased person goes to hell until his sin is exhausted.

## \|\| 18.29-31 \|\|

*kasmiṃścitsamaye pūrṇe mahiṣāsanasaṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*narakānvīkṣya dharmātmā nānākrandasamākulān \|\| 29 \|\|*

*caturaśītilakṣāṇāṃ narakāṇāṃ sa īśvaraḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ madhye śreṣṭhatamā ghorā yā ekaviṃśatiḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

*tāmistraṃ lohaśaṅkuśca mahārauravaśālmalī \|*  
*rauravaṃ kuḍvalaṃ kālasūtrakaṃ pūtimṛtikā \|\| 31 \|\|*

29-31. After his term has expired, the pious soul is allowed by Yama, the lord of eightyfour lacs of hells, to have a look at hells and hear the cries of pretas (the departed souls). In the midst of hells, there are 21 notorious hells, viz. TamiSra, Lohasanku, Maharaurava, Shalmali, Raurava, Kudvala, Kalasutraka, Putimrttika.

## \|\| 18.32-33 \|\|

*saṅghātaṃ lohatodaṃ ca saviṣaṃ sampratāpanam \|*  
*mahānarakakālolaḥ sajīvanamahāpathaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

*avīcirandhatā mistraḥ kumbhopākastathaiva ca \|*  
*asipatravanaṃ caiva patanaścaikaviṃśatiḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

32-33. Saugata, Lohatoda, Savisa, Pretapana, Mahanaraka, Kalola, Sajivana, Mahapatha, Avici, Andhatamisra, Kumbhlpaka, Asipatra- vana and Patana.

## \|\| 18.34 \|\|

*yeṣāṃ tu narake ghore bahvabdāni gatāni vai \|*  
*santātarnaiva vidyate dūtatvaṃ te tu (pretya) yānti hi \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Those who have passed several years in the dreadful hell and have no descendants (to offer gifts) in their favour become messengers of Yama.

## \|\| 18.35 \|\|

*yamena preṣitāste vai mānuṣasya mṛtasya tu \|*  
*dinedine pragṛhṇanti dattamannādyapānakam \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Yama sends them on errand and they share with the dead the food and drink which their relatives offer them from time to time.

## \|\| 18.36-37 \|\|

*pretasyaiva viluṇṭhanti madhye mārge bubhukṣitāḥ \|*  
*māsānte bhojanaṃ piṇḍameke yacchanti tatra vai \|\| 36 \|\|*

*tṛptiṃ prayānti te sarve pratyahaṃ caiva vatsaram \|*  
*evamādikṛtaiḥ puṇyaiḥ kramātsauripuraṃ vrajet \|\| 37 \|\|*

36-37. Being extremely hungry they seize the dead man's gifts on the way and eat them. A rice-ball gifted as food to the dead at the end of a month satiates them for a year.

## \|\| 18.38 \|\|

*tataḥ saṃvatsarasyānte pratyāsanne yamālaye \|*  
*bahubhītakare preto hastamātraṃ samutsṛjet \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. By such deeds of merit, gradually he reaches Sauripura. Then by the end of a year, he reaches the vicinity of dreadful Yamaloka.

## \|\| 18.39 \|\|

*divasairdaśabhirjātaṃ taṃ dehaṃ daśapiṇḍajam \|*  
*jāmadagnyasyeva rāmaṃ dṛṣṭvā tejaḥ prasarpati \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Here, he gives up his dreadful body, of the measure of arm, which he derived within ten days from the ten pin as gif ted. to him by his descendants. His spirits droop at the sight of Yama as the spirit of Parasurama at the sight of Rama (Dasarathi).

## \|\| 18.40 \|\|

*karmajaṃ dehamāśritya pūrvadehaṃ samutsajet \|*  
*aṅguṣṭhamātro vāyuśca śamīpatraṃsamāruhet \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. He gives up his previous body for the one derived from his actions. He receives an airy body of the size of a thumb and reaches the sword-edged hell.

## \|\| 18.41 \|\|

*vrajaṃstiṣṭhanpadaikena yathaivaikena gacchati \|*  
*yathā tṛṇajalaukeva dehī karmānugo 'vaśaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Here, he walks on single foot bound helplessly by his actions as a caterpillar by reeds.

## \|\| 18.42 \|\|

*vāsāṃsi jīrṇāni yathā vihāya navāni gṛhṇāti naro 'parāṇi \|*  
*tathā śarīrāṇi vihāya jīrṇānyanyāni saṃyāti navāni dehī \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Just as a person casts off his old dress and puts on a new one, so the soul gives up his old body and assumes a new one.

## Chapter 19

## \|\| 19.1 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*vāyubhūtaḥ kṣadhāviṣṭaḥ karmajaṃ dehamāśritaḥ \|*  
*te dehaṃ sa samāsādya yamena saha gacchati \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Assuming an airy form derived from his previous actions and suffering from hunger he moves along with Yama.

## \|\| 19.2 \|\|

*citraguptapuraṃ tatra yojanānāṃ tu viṃśatiḥ \|*  
*kāyasthāstatra paśyanti pāpapuṇyāni sarvaśaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. There is a town of Citragupta covering twenty yojanas. There the embodied beings observe their virtue and sin all around.

## \|\| 19.3 \|\|

*mahādāneṣudatteṣu gatastatra sukhī bhavet \|*  
*yojanānāṃ caturviṃśatpuraṃ vaivasvataṃ śubham \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. If he had made substantial gifts in his lifetime he derives pleasure therefrom: From here Vaivasvata town of Yama is situated at the distance of twentyfovr Yojanas.

## \|\| 19.4 \|\|

*lohaṃ lavaṇakārpāsaṃ tilapātraṃ ca yairnaraiḥ \|*  
*dattaṃ tenaiva tṛpyanti yamasya puracāriṇaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The residents of Vaivasvatapura are satiated by the gifts of iron, salt, cotton and a vessel of gingelly seeds, offered by the relatives of the deceased.

## \|\| 19.5 \|\|

*gatvā ca tatra te sarve pratīhāraṃ vadanti hi \|*  
*dharmadhvajapratīhārastatra tiṣṭhati sarvadā \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The dead repair to Vaivasvata town and inform Dharmadhvaja, the keeper at Yama's gate (about the gifts). Dharmadhvaja is always present at the gate of Yama.

## \|\| 19.6 \|\|

*saptadhānyasya dānena prīto dharmadhvajo bhavet \|*  
*tatra gatvā pratīhāro brūte tasya śubhāśubham \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Dharmadhvaja is pleased with the gift of seven grains. Propitiated by that, he tells the dead about their virtues and sins.

## \|\| 19.7 \|\|

*dharmarājasya yadrūpaṃ santaḥ sukṛtino janāḥ \|*  
*paśyanti ca durātmāno yamarūpaṃ subhīṣaṇam \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Holy and pious people see Dharmaraja. as a god of noble countenance, while the sinful and the wicked see him as a god of dreadful and terrible aspect.

## \|\| 19.8 \|\|

*taṃ dṛṣṭvā bhayabhītastu hāheti vadate janaḥ \|*  
*kṛtaṃ dānaṃ ca yairmartyaisteṣāṃ nāsti bhayaṃ kvacit \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The deceased person is terribly afraid at his sight and laments bitterly. Those who have offered gifts in their life-time should entertain no fear.

## \|\| 19.9 \|\|

*prāptaṃ sukṛtinaṃ dṛṣṭvā sthānācca lati sūryajaḥ \|*  
*eṣa me maṇḍalaṃ bhittvā brahmalokaṃ prayāsyati \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Yama moves from his seat as soon as he sees a holy man. He thinks that he will supersede him in status and reach Brahmaloka.

## \|\| 19.10 \|\|

*dānena sulabho dharmo yamamārgaḥ sukhāvahaḥ \|*  
*eṣa mārgo viśālo 'tra na kenāpya nugamyate \|*  
*dānapuṇyaṃ vinā vatsa na gaccheddharmamandiram \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Virtue can easily be procured by offering gifts. The path to Yama's region can easily be traversed by acts of Charity. This High Way cannot be easily covered otherwise. О dear, none can reach the city of Yama without doing charitable acts.

## \|\| 19.11 \|\|

*tasminmārge tu raudre vai bhīṣaṇā yamakiṅkarāḥ \|*  
*ekaikasya purasyāgre tiṣṭhatyekasahasrakam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. That dreadful path is full of terror- striking servants of Yama, Each of the cities is guarded by a thousand of these servants.

## \|\| 19.12 \|\|

*pacanti pāpinaṃ prāpya udake yātanākarāḥ \|*  
*gṛhṇanti māsamāsānte pādaśeṣaṃ tu tadbhavet \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The messengers of Yama torture the sinner in (hot) waters and take off his skin till he becomes a skeleton.

## \|\| 19.13 \|\|

*aurdhvadaihikadānāni yairna dattāni kāśyapa \|*  
*mahākaṣṭena te yānti tasmāddeyāni śaktitaḥ \|*  
*adattvā paśuvadyānti gṛhīto vandhabandhanaiḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. The deceased for whom no obsequial rites have been made traverse the path with great difficulty. They are led like animals bound with ropes.

## \|\| 19.14-15 \|\|

*evaṃ kṛtena sampaśyetsa naraḥ bhūtakarmaṇā \|*  
*daivikīṃ paitṛkīṃ yoniṃ mānuṣīṃ vātha nārakīm \|\| 14 \|\|*

*dharmarājasya vacanānmuktirbhavati vā tataḥ \|*  
*mānuṣyaṃ tattvataḥ prāpya sa puttraḥ puttratāṃ vrajet \|\| 15 \|\|*

14-15. The person may become a god, a mare, a man of low species, or as Yama ordains he may attain salvation or be born as a human being as a son to his father.

## \|\| 19.16 \|\|

*yathāyathā kṛtaṃ karma tāntāṃ yoniṃ vrajennaraḥ \|*  
*tattathaiva ca bhuñjāno vicaretsarvalokagaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. He obtains birth according to his activities. He passes through a series of births in this world.

## \|\| 19.17 \|\|

*aśāśvataṃ parijñāya sarvalokottaraṃ sukham \|*  
*yadā bhavati mānuṣyaṃ tadā dharmaṃ samācaret \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Knowing that even the highest happiness is non eternal, he should perform acts of righteousness when he has received a human body.

## \|\| 19.18 \|\|

*kṛmayo bhasma viṣṭhā vā dehānāṃ prakṛtiḥ sadā \|*  
*andhakūpe mahāraudre dīpahastaḥ pātettu vai \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Human body is either reduced to worms, ashes, or feces. Though he carries a lantern in hand, he may fall in a dreadful hell full of darkness.

## \|\| 19.19 \|\|

*mahāpuṇyaprabhāveṇa mānuṣyaṃ janma labhyate \|*  
*yastatprāpya careddharmaṃ sa gacchetparamāṃ gatim \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. He can acquire human body as a result of his pious acts. He who performs holy deeds in human body acquires supreme position.

## \|\| 19.20 \|\|

*api jānanvṛthā dharmaṃ duḥ khamāyāti yāti ca \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. If he neglects Dharma, he comes to grief.

## \|\| 19.21 \|\|

*jātīśatena labhate kila mānuṣatvaṃ tatrāpi durlabhataraṃ khaga bho dvijatvam \|*  
*yastatra pālayati lālayati vratāni tasyāmṛtaṃ bhavati hastagataṃ prasādāt \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The soul obtains human body after passing through series of births. О bird, in human body too, his birth as a Brahmana is a rare event. He who observes vows natural to his caste becomes immortal after death by the blessing of God.

## Chapter 20

## \|\| 20.1 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*ye kecitpretarūpeṇa kutra vāsaṃ labhanti te \|*  
*pretalokādvinirmuktāḥ kathaṃ kutra vrajanti te \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Garuda said: — Having obtained a new body where does a preta shelter? Released from pretahood, where does he go to live in?

## \|\| 20.2-3 \|\|

*caturyuktāśīti lakṣairnarakaiḥ paryupāsitāḥ \|*  
*yamena rakṣitāstatra bhūtaiścaiva sahasraśaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

*vicaranti kathaṃ loke narakācca vinirgatāḥ \|*  
*garuḍodīritaṃ śrutvā lakṣmīnātho 'bravīdidam \|\| 3 \|\|*

2-3. Passing through torments in hells which number eigmy four lacs and guarded by Yama and his thousand attendants how do the pretas get release and how do they roam about in this mortal world?

## \|\| 20.4 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*pakṣirāja śṛṇuṣva tvaṃ yatra pretāścaranti vai \|*  
*parārthadāragrahaṇāccha (ba) lāddrohānniśācarāḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Sri Krsna said: — О king of birds, hear. I shall tell you about the region where the pretas live. Men attain pretahood by stealing other man's riches, by indulging in sexual intercourse with other man's wife or by doing acts of treachery.

## \|\| 20.5 \|\|

*tathaiva sarvapāpiṣṭhaḥ svātmajānveṣaṇe ratāḥ \|*  
*vicarantyaśarīrāste kṣuptipāsārditā bhṛśam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Having incurred sins, they seek for redemption in -their progeny. Being bodiless as well as suffering from hunger and thirst they roam about here and there.

## \|\| 20.6 \|\|

*bandīgṛhavi nirmuktā yebhyo naśyanti jantavaḥ \|*  
*te vyavasyanti ca pretā vadhopāyaṃ ca bandhuṣu \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Even, the captives released from prison are frightened at their sight. They seek for means to kill their kith and kin.

## \|\| 20.7 \|\|

*pitṛdvadārāṇi rundhanti tanmārgocchedakāstathā \|*  
*pitṛbhā gānvigṛhṇanti pānthebhyastaskarā iva \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. They bolt doors on their ancestors, put obstacles in the way of manes. Like thieves, they snatch the mane's food in the way before it reaches them.

## \|\| 20.8 \|\|

*svaṃ veśma punarāgatya mitrasthāne viśanti te \|*  
*tatra sthitā nirīkṣante rogaśokādibandhanāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. They return home, stay on the roof and watch the activities of their kips. They cause disease afid grief to their relatives.

## \|\| 20.9-11 \|\|

*pīḍayanti jvarībhūya ekāntaramiṣeṇa tu \|*  
*tṛtīyakajvarā bhūtvā śītavātādipīḍayā \|\| 9 \|\|*

*anyāṃśca vividhānrogāñchiro 'rtiṃ ca viṣūcikām \|*  
*cinta yanti sadā teṣāmucchiṣṭādisthalasthitāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

*ātmajānāṃ chalāllokā bhūtasaṅghaiśca rakṣitāḥ \|*  
*pibanti te ca pānīyaṃ bhojanocchiṣṭayojitam \|\| 11 \|\|*

9-11. Having assumed the form of tertian and such-like fevers they cause diseases due to cold or wind like head-ache or cholera. They stay at the place of leavings or refuse, in the company of other ghosts and partake of food and drink left over by their relatives.

## \|\| 20.12 \|\|

*evaṃ pretāḥ pravartante nānādoṣairvikarmiṇaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. In this way, the sinning pretas move about freely.

## \|\| 20.13-14 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*kathaṃ kurvanti te pretāḥ kena rūpeṇa kasya kim \|*  
*jñāyate kena vidhinā jalpanti na vadanti vā \|\| 13 \|\|*

*enaṃ chindhi manomohaṃ mama cedicchasi priyam \|*  
*kalikāle hṛṣīkeśa pretatvaṃ jāyate bahu \|\| 14 \|\|*

13-14. Garuda said: — How do the pretas behave and in what form? How is it possible to know their attitude, since the pretas do not speak to us? If you are pleased to do me a favour, you clear off my doubts. О lord, I hear that in the Kali age many people become ghosts.

## \|\| 20.15 \|\|

*śrīviṣṇuruvāca \|*  
*svakulaṃ pīḍayetpetaḥ paracchidreṇa pīḍayet \|*  
*jīvansa dṛśyate snehī mṛto duṣṭatvamāpnuyāt \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Sri Visnu said: — The ghost torments his family people through the enemy. While he I was inhuman body he was affectionate to his people, now that he is dead he becomes hostile to them.

## \|\| 20.16 \|\|

*rudrajāpī dharmarato devatātithipūjakaḥ \|*  
*satyavāk priyavādī ca na pretaiḥ sa hi pīḍyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. He who is devoted to Rudra, follows the path of righteousness, propitiates gods, satiates guests, speaks truth and pleasant words, is not tormented by the pretas.

## \|\| 20.17 \|\|

*sarvakriyāparibhraṣṭo nāstiko dharmanindakaḥ \|*  
*asatyavādanirato naraḥ pretaiḥ sa pīḍyate \|*  
*kalau pretatvamāpnoti tārkṣyāśuddhakriyāparaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. He who does not observe rites, has no faith in the sanctity of the Vedas, hates righteous acts and indulges in falsehood, is tormented by the pretas. By doing unrighteous acts, О Garuda, he becomes a preta in this Kali age.

## \|\| 20.18 \|\|

*kṛtādau dvāparānte ca na preto naiva pīḍanam \|*  
*bahūnāmekajātānāmekaḥ saukhyaṃ samaśnute \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. From the beginning of Satya yuga to the end of Dvapara nobody became a preta and nobody suffered from preta.

## \|\| 20.19-20 \|\|

*eko duṣkṛtakarmā ca ekaḥ santatimāñjanaḥ \|*  
*ekaḥ sampīḍyate pretairekaḥ sutadhanānvitaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

*ekasya putranāśaḥ syādeko duhitṛmān bhavet \|*  
*virodho bandhubhiḥ sārdhaṃ pretadoṣeṇa kāśyapa \|\| 20 \|\|*

19-20. It is observed that of the many children born of one and the same mother, one is happy, one is addicted to bad habits, one is blessed with progeny, one is tormented by pretas, one abounds in wealth, one gets sons who die young, one is burdened with the offspring of daughters, one is at daggersdrawn with his relatives. This all is due to the bad intentions of the preta, О Garuda.

## \|\| 20.21 \|\|

*santatirdṛśyate naiva samutpannā vinaśyati \|*  
*paśudravyavināśaśca sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. A woman becomes barren in life or if she gives birth to children they die at an early age. There is a loss of wealth and cattle. These sufferings are caused by preta.

## \|\| 20.22 \|\|

*prakṛteḥ parivartaḥ syādvidveṣaḥ saha bandhubhiḥ \|*  
*akasmādvyasanaprāptiḥ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. If there is a sudden change in, his nature or an enmity with his relatives or an unexpected calamity, the suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.23 \|\|

*nāstikyaṃ vṛttilopaśca mahālobhastathaiva ca \|*  
*syāddhantakalaho nityaṃ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. If a person loses faith in religion or if he loses the means of his livelihood or if he feels greedy in excess or if there is a regular quarrel at home, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.24-25 \|\|

*pitṛmātṛnihantā ca devabrāhmaṇanindakaḥ \|*  
*ityādoṣamavāpnoti sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 24 \|\|*

*nityakarmaviniṃmukto japahomavivarjitaḥ \|*  
*paradravyāṇāṃ ca hartā sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 25 \|\|*

24-25. If he slays his parents or reproaches gods and Brahmins and is found guilty of murder that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.26 \|\|

*suvṛṣṭau kṛṣināśaśca vyavahāro vinaśyati \|*  
*loke kalahakārī ca sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. When crops do not grow up, though die rains are abundant; when the expenditure goes up and income is reduced; when quarrels rise in gravity, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.27 \|\|

*mārge jaṅgamyamānaṃ taṃ pīḍayedvātamaṇḍalī \|*  
*pretapīḍā tu sā jñeyā satyaṃsatyaṃ khageśvara \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. When, on travel to a foreign land, he is distressed by me onrush of wind, О lord of birds, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.28 \|\|

*hīnajātyā ca sambandho hīnakarma karoti yaḥ \|*  
*adharme ramate nityaṃ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. When he associates with the people of low caste or when be performs disreputable acts or when he is interested in acts of unrighteousness, that suffering is due to preta,

## \|\| 20.29 \|\|

*vyasanairdravyanāśaḥ syādupakrāntaṃ vinaśyati \|*  
*caurāgnirājabhirhāniḥ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. When the hoarded wealthis destroyed by misfortune or when the work in operation bears no fruit or when there is loss of wealth due to undue taxation or due to fire or theft, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.30 \|\|

*mahārogopalabdhiśca bālakānāṃ ca pīḍanam \|*  
*jāyā saṃpīḍhyate yacca sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. When an incurable disease sets in, or when children suffer from pain or when wife suffers immensely, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.31 \|\|

*śrutismṛtipurāṇeṣu dharmaśāstrasamudbhave \|*  
*abhāvo jāyate dharme sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. When one loses faith in the Vedas, Smrtis, Puranas, and Dharmasastras, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.32 \|\|

*devatīrthadvijānāṃ tu nindāṃyaḥ kurute naraḥ \|*  
*pratyakṣaṃ vā parokṣaṃ vā sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. When one abuses gods, gurus and Brahmins in their presence or absence, that aberration of nature is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.33 \|\|

*svavṛttiharaṇaṃ yacca svapratiṣṭhāhatistathā \|*  
*vaṃśacchedaḥ nadṛśyeta pretadoṣādvinānyathā \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. This is due to preta and to no other course when a person suffers from loss of livelihood or break in social position or break in the continuity of lineage.

## \|\| 20.34 \|\|

*strīṇāṃ garbhavināśaḥ syānna puṣpaṃ dṛśyate tathā \|*  
*bālānāṃ maraṇaṃ yatra sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. When women suffer from abortion or do not conceive or when children die at an early age that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.35 \|\|

*bhāvaśuddhyā na kurute śrāddhaṃ sāṃvatsarādikam \|*  
*svayameva na kurvīta sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. When he does not perform the annual sraddha in sincerity and has no inclination either-that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.36 \|\|

*tīrthe gattvā parāsaktaḥ svakṛtyaṃ ca parityajet \|*  
*dharmakārye na sampattiḥ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. When on pilgrimage he indulges in sexual intercourse or neglects his duties or when lie fails to prosper though he has done acts of piety that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.37 \|\|

*dampatyoḥ kalahaścaiva bhojane kopasaṃyutaḥ \|*  
*paradrohe matiścaiva sā pīḍā pretasaṃbhavā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. When both husband and wife quarrel at meals, when there is a strong inclination to harm others that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.38 \|\|

*puṣpaṃ yatra na dṛśyena phalaṃ tathā \|*  
*viraho bhāryayā yatra sā pīḍā pretasagbhavā \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. When trade does not prosper though he has gone abroad where he lives in separation from wife, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.39 \|\|

*yeṣāṃ vai jāyate cihnaṃ sadoccāṭaparaṃ nṛṇām \|*  
*svakṣetre niṣphalaṃ tejaḥ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. When he lives in foreign lands or when he loses position at home, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.40 \|\|

*svagotraghātakaścaiva hanti śatrumivātmajam \|*  
*na prītirnāpi saukhyaṃ ca sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. When he is inimical to his people, regards his son as his enemy, when he has no interest in home and feels uncomfortable there, that suffering is due to preta.

## \|\| 20.41 \|\|

*pitṛvākyaṃ na kurute svapatnīṃ ca na sevate \|*  
*sadā krūramatirvyagraḥ sā pīḍā pretasambhavā \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. When he refuses to obey his parents and has no love for his wife, is of cruel nature, is lost in his own affairs, that suffering is due to preta

## \|\| 20.42 \|\|

*vikarmā jāyate preto hyavidhikriyayā tathā \|*  
*tatkāladuṣṭasaṃsargādvṛṣotsargādṛte tathā \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. If the funeral rites are not performed in the prescribed way, the soul of the deceased (in rebirth) deviates from the righteous path and falls in the company of the wicked. Then Vrsotsarga is the only rite to redeem him.

## \|\| 20.43 \|\|

*dṛṣṭagṛtyuvaśādvāpi adagdhavapuṣastathā \|*  
*pretatvaṃ jāyate tārkṣya pīḍyante yena jantavaḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. О Garuda, a person becomes a ghost and undergoes sufferings if he dies an accidental death or if his body is not cremated properly.

## \|\| 20.44 \|\|

*evaṃ jñātvā khagaśreṣṭha pretamuktiṃ samācaret \|*  
*yo vai na manyate pretānmṛtaḥ pretatvamāpnuyāt \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. О best of birds, when the descendent knows all this, he should conduct those rites which may release the deceased from ghosthood. If he does not perform rites for the ghost he himself turns a ghost after death.

## \|\| 20.45 \|\|

*pretadoṣaḥ kule yasya sukhaṃ tasya na vidyate \|*  
*matiḥ prītī ratirbuddhirlakṣmīḥ pañcavināśanam \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. The person whose house is haunted by a ghost does not feel happy or comfortable. He loses faith, pleasure, devotion, discrimination as well as wealth.

## \|\| 20.46 \|\|

*tṛtīye pañcame puṃsi vaṃśacchedo hi jāyate \|*  
*daridro nirdhanaścaiva pāpakarmā bhavebhave \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. His lineage breaks either at the third or at the fifth generation. In each and every birth he lives a wretched, poor and sinful life.

## \|\| 20.47 \|\|

*ye kecitpetarūpā vikṛtamukhadṛśo raudrarūpāḥ karālā manyante naiva gotraṃ sutaduhitṛpitṝn bhrātṛjāyāṃ vadhūṃ vā \|*  
*kṛtvā kāmyaṃ ca rūpaṃ sukhagatirahitā bhāṣamāṇā yatheṣṭaṃ hā kaṣṭaṃ bhoktukāmā vidhivaśapatitāḥ saṃsmaranti svapākam \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. There are people who have fierce, dreadful, disfigured and ghostly appearance, who have no regard or honour for their caste, progeny, parents, or womanfolk, who put on fashions, go an unpleasant way and talk loosely. Alas, it is painful to see them suffer, under the force of fate, from the recollection of their past sinful deeds.

## Chapter 21

## \|\| 21.1 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*muktiṃ yānti kathaṃ pretāstadahaṃ praṣṭumutsukaḥ \|*  
*yanmuktau ca manuṣyāṇāṃ na pīḍā jāyate punaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. Garuda said: — О lord, I am desirous of asking you how the ghosts ultimately get free and when men are no longer afflicted by them.

## \|\| 21.2 \|\|

*etaiśca lakṣaṇairdeva pīḍoktā pretajā tvayā \|*  
*teṣāṃ kadā bhavenmuktiḥ pretatvaṃ na kathaṃ bhavet \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. How do the symptoms of ghostly affliction decisively vanish? How can ghosthood be warded off lest it should recur again?

## \|\| 21.3 \|\|

*pretatve hi pramāṇaṃ ca kati varṣāṇi saṃkhyayā \|*  
*ciraṃ pretatvamāpannaḥ kathaṃ muktimavāpnuyāt \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. What is the time-limit, if any, of ghosthood? In how many years does a longstanding ghosthood disappear utterly?

## \|\| 21.4 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*muktiṃ prāyanti te pretāstadahaṃ kathayāmi te \|*  
*yadaiva manujo 'vaiti mama pīḍā kṛtā tviyam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The Lord said: — I shall tell you how the ghosts become free and also how the person knows that he is tormented by a ghost.

## \|\| 21.5-7 \|\|

*pṛcchārthaṃ hitamanvicchandai vajñe vinivadayet \|*  
*svapne dṛṣṭaḥ śubho vṛkṣaḥ phalitaścūtacampakaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

*vipro vā vṛṣabho devo bhramate tīrthago yadi \|*  
*evaṃ dṛṣṭo yadā svapno mṛtaḥ ko 'pisvagotrajaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

*svapne satyaṃ parijñāya dṛṣṭaṃ pretaprabhāvataḥ \|*  
*adbhutāni pradṛśyante pretadoṣādviniścitam \|\| 7 \|\|*

5-7. Не (the ghost-afflicted man) shall explain the signs and symptoms to the astrologer. If he dreams of a holy plant like a Campaka or of a mango tree laden with fruits or if he dreams of a Brahmin or of a bull or of himself in a place of pilgrimage or of the death of 'a kinsman and if in dream he takes this as truth, this is all due to pretadosa. Mysterious events do often occur if the ghost has bad intentions.

## \|\| 21.8 \|\|

*tīrthasnāne matiryāvaccittaṃ dharmaparāyaṇam \|*  
*dharmāpāyaṃ prakurute pretapīḍā tadā vrajet \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. If a person desires to visit a holy place and his heart is set upon it, but somehow there is a break in carrying out his desire, that is due to the bad intention of a ghost.

## \|\| 21.10 \|\|

*aśreyasi pravṛtti ca prerayanti punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*uccāṭanaṃ ca krūratvaṃ sarvaṃ pretakṛtaṃ khaga \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The evil intentions of the ghost come in the way of holy man whose pious activities are disturbed at each step or take an evil course or if a person falls a victim of eradication and turns cruel, О lord of birds, that is due to the bad intentions of a ghost.

## \|\| 21.11 \|\|

*sarvavighnāni santyajya muktyupāyaṃ karoti yaḥ \|*  
*tasya karmaphalaṃ sādhu pretavṛttiśca śāśvatī \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. If a person performs holy rites for the redemption of a ghost, he will find his actions fruitful. The ghost shall be satiated permanently.

## \|\| 21.12-13 \|\|

*sa bhavettena muktastu dattaṃ śreyaskaraṃ param \|*  
*svayaṃ tṛpyati bhoḥ pakṣinyasyoddeśena dīyate \|\| 12 \|\|*

*śṛṇu satyamidaṃ tārkṣya yaddadāti bhunakti saḥ \|*  
*ātmānaṃ śreyasā yuñjyātpretastṛptiṃ ciraṃ vrajet \|\| 13 \|\|*

12-13. О Tarksya, take this as truth that he who performs such rites becomes contented. He will make his soul permanently wedded to weal; the ghost will have a long-standing satiety. When the ghost is satisfied he will wish his kinsmen well for ever.

## \|\| 21.14 \|\|

*te tṛptāḥ śubhamicchanti nijabandhuṣu sarvadā \|*  
*ajñātayastu ye duṣṭāḥ pīḍayanti svavaṃśajān \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. There are certain sinful, wicked ghosts who harass their descendants. But, they too, when propitiated, cease to harass them.

## \|\| 21.15 \|\|

*nivārayanti tṛptāste jāyamānānukampakāḥ \|*  
*paścātte muktimāyanti kāle prāpte svaputrataḥ \|*  
*sadā bandhuṣu yacchanti vṛddhimṛddhiṃ khagādhipa \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. О lord of birds, they too, when their time comes, become free due to their sons' rites and they bless their kinsmen with opulence and flourishing wealth.

## \|\| 21.16 \|\|

*darśanādbhāṣaṇādyastu ceṣṭātaḥ pīḍanādgatim \|*  
*na prāpayati mūḍhātmā pretaśāpaiḥ sa lipyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. The wretch of deluded soul who in spite of seeing, hearing and feeling the depredations of the ghost does not succour, is tarnished with the curse of the ghost.

## \|\| 21.17-18 \|\|

*aputrako 'paśuścaiva daridro vyādhitastathā \|*  
*vṛttihīnaśca hīnaśca bhavejjanmanijanmani \|\| 17 \|\|*

*evaṃ bruvanti te pretāḥ punaryābhyaṃ samāśritāḥ \|*  
*tatrasthānāṃ bhavenmuktiḥ svakāle karmasaṃkṣaye \|\| 18 \|\|*

17-18. In every birth he takes lie will become extremely indigent, sick, devoid of progeny and cattle and he may not get proper livelihood either. The ghosts perpetrate all these things. Then they go back to Yama's abode. From that place, when their evil actions wear off, and the appointed time arrives, they get release.

## \|\| 21.19-20 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*nāma gotraṃ na dṛśyeta pratītirnaiva jāyate \|*  
*kecidvadanti daivajñāḥ pīḍāṃ pretasamudbhavām \|\| 19 \|\|*

*na svapnaścaiṣṭitaṃ naiva darśanaṃ na kadācana \|*  
*kiṃ kartavyaṃ suraśreṣṭha tatra me brūhi niścitam \|\| 20 \|\|*

19-20. Garuda said: — Sometimes astrologers say that there is an affliction of ghosts but no specific indication is there as to the name of the ghost, his clan, etc; neither bad dream nor harassing activity is observed. О lord of deities, what should be done then? Please tell me precisely.

## \|\| 21.21-22 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*satyaṃ vāpyanṛtaṃ vāpi vadanti kṣitidevatāḥ \|*  
*tadā sañcintya hṛdaye satyametaddvijeritam \|\| 21 \|\|*

*bhāvabhaktiṃ puraskṛtya pitṛbhaktiparāyaṇaḥ \|*  
*kṛtvā kṛṣṇabaliṃ caiva puraścaraṇa pūrvakam \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. The Lord said'.-Brahmins say only what is true. They never tell lies. Fully believing in what the Brahmins say the person (advised and warned of ghosts) shall devoutly pray' to the manes, perform purascarana rite and offer oblations to Visnu.

## \|\| 21.23 \|\|

*japahomaistathā dānaiḥ prakuryāddehasodhanam \|*  
*kṛtena tena vighnāni vinaśyanti khageśvara \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. By means of japas, homos and dams he should sanctify his body. О lord of birds, if this is performed, all obstacles and hindrances are dispelled.

## \|\| 21.24 \|\|

*bhūtapretapiśācairvā sa cedanyaiḥ prapīḍyate \|*  
*pitruddeśena vai kuryānnārāyaṇabaliṃ tadā \|*  
*vimuktaḥ sarvapīḍābhya iti satyaṃ vaco mama \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. He is never afflicted by Bhutas and Pisacas or other sorts of ghosts. By performing rites of oblations to Narayana with the ancestors in view he shall be freed of all sorts of affliction. This is my sworn' statement. farjMUi ч^пг tj^ii

## \|\| 21.25-27 \|\|

*pitṛpīḍā bhavedyatra kṛtyairanyairna mucyate \|*  
*tasmātsarvaprayatnena pitṛbhaktiparo bhavet \|\| 25 \|\|*

*navame daśame varṣe pikṣuddeśena vai pumān \|*  
*gāyattrīmayutaṃ japtvā daśāṃśena ca homayet \|\| 26 \|\|*

*kṛtvā kṛṣṇabaliṃ pūrvaṃ vṛṣotsargādikāḥ kriyāḥ \|*  
*sarvopadravahīnastu sarvasaukhyamavāpnuyāt \|*  
*uttamaṃ lokamāpnoti jñātiprādhānyameva ca \|\| 27 \|\|*

25-27. If there is an affliction of ghosts, the victim is never relieved by any other activity. Hence, one shall with due endeavour devoutly pray to the manes. He who repeals Gayatri mantra with the manes in view, either in the ninth or tenth year, ten thousand times and performs homas a thousand times, after having previously performed Narayana Bali, Vrsotsarsa and other rites shall be free from all sorts of tortures. He will attain all sorts of pleasure and the best of worlds. He shall be honoured by his cousins and kinsmen.

## \|\| 21.28 \|\|

*pitṛmā tṛsamaṃ loke nāstyanyaddaivataṃ param \|*  
*tasmātsarvaprayatnena pūjayetpitarau sadā \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. There is no godhead on par with either one's father or mother. Therefore, one should always worship the manes with hearty devotion.

## \|\| 21.29-31 \|\|

*hitānāmupadeṣṭā hi pratyakṣaṃ daivataṃ pitā \|*  
*anyā yā devatā loke na dehaprabhavo hi tāḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

*śarīrameva jantūnāṃ svargamokṣaikasādhanam \|*  
*deho datto hi yenaivaṃ ko 'nyaḥ pūjyatamastataḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

*iti sañcintyahṛdaye pakṣinyadyatprayacchati \|*  
*tatsarvamātmanā bhuṅkte dānaṃ vedavido viduḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

29-31. One's father is the instructor in wholesome things and he is a visible deity. Other deities may be the lords of bodies (but not the progenitors). Verily, this physical body can yield either hell or heaven or salvation. Who is more worthy of respect and honour than he (i.e. father) due to whose favour one is born m bodily form? After pondering over this, if one gives anything to the pitrs, О bird, that returns to him which he enjoys. This is what those who know the Vedas say.

## \|\| 21.32 \|\|

*punnāmanarakādyasmātpitaraṃ trāyate sutaḥ \|*  
*tasmātputtra iti prokta iha cāpi paratra ca \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. He who saves his father from the hell named put is called putra here and hereafter.

## \|\| 21.33 \|\|

*apamṛtyumṛtau syātāṃ pitarau kasyacitkhaga \|*  
*vratatīrthāvivāhādiśrāddhaṃ saṃvatsaraṃ tyajet \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. If one's father or mother dies either due to accident or due to a foul play, one shall have to adjourn religious rites, pilgrimages, nuptial rites and the annual sraddha.

## \|\| 21.34 \|\|

*svapnādhyāyamimaṃ yastu preta liṅganidarśakam \|*  
*yaḥ paṭhecchṛṇuyādvāpi pretacihnaṃ na paśyati \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. He who reads this chapter entitled Svapnadhyaya or hears it, wherein the various dream symbols of ghosts have been indicated, will never see the same himself. \[He will not be afflicted by ghosts\].

## Chapter 22

## \|\| 22.1-2 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*sambhavanti kathaṃ pretāḥ kena teṣāṃ gatirbhavet \|*  
*kīdṛkteṣāṃ bhavedrūpaṃ bhojanaṃ kiṃ bhavetprabho \|\| 1 \|\|*

*suprītāste kathaṃ pretāḥ kva tiṣṭhanti sureśvara \|*  
*prasannaḥ kṛpayā deva praśramenaṃ vadasva me \|\| 2 \|\|*

1-2. Garuda said: — How do these ghosts come into being? How are they redeemed from pretahood? What are their features. What is their diet, О lord? How aret he ghosts propitiated? О lord of deities, where do they stay? Please favour me, О lord, with an answer to these queries.

## \|\| 22.3 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*pāpakarmaratā ye vai pūrvakarmavaśānugāḥ \|*  
*jāyante te mṛtāḥ pretāstāñchṛṇuṣva vadāmyaham \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Shri Bhagavan said: — It is the men of sinful actions actuated by their previous misdeeds who become ghosts after death. Please listen to me, I shall tell you in detail.

## \|\| 22.4-5 \|\|

*vāpīkūpataḍāgāṃśca ārāmaṃ suramandiram \|*  
*prapāṃ sadma suvṛkṣāṃśca tathā bhojanaśālikāḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

*pitṛpaitāmahaṃ dharmaṃ kikrīṇāti sa pāpabhāk \|*  
*mṛtaḥ pretatvamāpnoti yāvadābhūtasaṃplavam \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. Не who desecrates wells, tanks, lakes, parks, temples, water sheds, groves of trees, alms- houses etc., and misdirects any one in religious rites for monetary gain is a sinner. After death he becomes a ghost and remains as such till the final deluge.

## \|\| 22.6 \|\|

*gocaraṃ grāmasīmāṃ taḍāgārāmagahvaram \|*  
*karṣayanti ca ye lobhātpretāste vai bhavanti hi \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Out of greed if people upset the boundaries of villages and destroy pasture lands, tanks, parks, underground drainage, etc., they become ghosts.

## \|\| 22.7 \|\|

*caṇḍālādudakātsarpādbrāhmaṇādbaidyutāgnitaḥ \|*  
*daṃṣṭribhyaśca paśubhyaśca maraṇaṃ pāpakarmiṇām \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Sinful persons meet with death at the hands of Candalas, infuriated Brahmins, serpents, animals with curved teeth or in watery graves or struck by lightning.

## \|\| 22.8-9 \|\|

*udbandhanamṛtā ye ca viṣaśastrahatāśca ye \|*  
*ātmopaghātino ye ca viṣūcyādihatāstathā \|\| 8 \|\|*

*mahārogairmṛtā ye ca pāparogaiśca dasyubhiḥ \|*  
*asaṃskṛtapramītā ye vihitācāravarjitāḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

8-9. Those who meet with foul death such as committing suicide by hanging from a tree, by poison or weapon, those who die of cholera,those who are burnt to death alive, those who die of foul and loath-some diseases or at the hands of robbers, those who are not cremated duly after death, those who do not follow sacred rites and conduct.

## \|\| 22.10 \|\|

*vṛṣotsargādiluptāścaluptamāsikapiṇḍakāḥ \|*  
*yasyānayati śūdrogniṃ tṛṇakāṣṭhahavīṃṣi saḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Those who do not perform Vrsotsarga and monthly pinda rites, those who allow sudras to bring sacrificial grass, twigs and other articles of homa.

## \|\| 22.11-13 \|\|

*patanātparvatānāṃ ca bhittipātena ye mṛtāḥ \|*  
*rajasvalādidoṣaiśca na ca bhūmau matāśca ye \|\| 11 \|\|*

*antarikṣe mṛtā ye ca viṣṇusmaraṇavarjitāḥ \|*  
*sūtakaiḥ śvādisaṃparkaiḥ pretabhāvā iha kṣitau \|\| 12 \|\|*

*evamādibhiranyaiśca kumṛtyuvaśagāśca ye \|*
