## \|\| 46.35 \|\|

*svadharmeṇaivordhvagatiradharmeṇāpyadhogatiḥ \|*  
*jāyate sarvavarṇānāṃ svadharmacalanāt khaga \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. О bird, all these take place in all castes according to their previous actions. In deityhood or manhood the acts of benevolence and indulgence recur due to their previous karman.

## \|\| 46.36-37 \|\|

*devatve mānuṣatve ca dānabhogādikāḥ kriyāḥ \|*  
*yā dṛśyante vainateya tatsarvaṃ karmajaṃ phalam \|\| 36 \|\|*

*akarmavihite ghore kāmakrodhārjite 'śubhe \|*  
*patedvai narake bhūyo tasyottāro na vidyate \|\| 37 \|\|*

36-37. О son of Vinata, whatever is observed is the fruit of action. If a person indulges habitually in evil actions due to passion, it is certain that he will fall into a terrible hell from which there is no escape.

## Chapter 47

## \|\| 47.1 \|\|

*garuḍa uvāca \|*  
*bhagavandevadeveśa kṛpayā parayā vada \|*  
*dānaṃ dānasya māhātmyaṃ vaitaraṇyāḥ pramāṇakam \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. О lord of gods, please tell me the mode of gifts, their glory and greatness and the I magnitude of Vaitarani.

## \|\| 47.2 \|\|

*śrīkṛṣṇa uvāca \|*  
*yā sā vaitaraṇī nāma yamamārge mahāsarit \|*  
*agādhā dustarā pāpairdṛṣṭamātrā bhayāvahā \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Listen to the magnitude of the dreadful Vaitaranl, the mighty river at the threshold of Yama's city.

## \|\| 47.3 \|\|

*pūyaśoṇitatoyāḍhyā māṃsakardamasakuṃlā \|*  
*pāpinañcāgatāndṛṣṭvā nānābhayasamāvṛtā \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. That river is hundred yojanas wide. It is impassable and foul-smelling. To the sinner it is terrifying even at the very first sight

## \|\| 47.4 \|\|

*kvāthyate satvaraṃ toyaṃ pātramadhye ghṛtaṃ yathā \|*  
*krimibhiḥ saṅkulaṃ pūyaṃ vajratuṇḍaiḥ samāvṛtam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. It is full of putrid blood with sediments and marshy deposits of flesh. On seeing a sinner it assumes the form of melted gh! in a vessel. It abounds in worms and flesh brought by vultures.

## \|\| 47.5 \|\|

*śiśumāraiśca makarairvajrakartarikāyutaiḥ \|*  
*anyaiśca jalajīvaiśca hiṃsakairmāṃsabhedibhiḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. It is full of crocodiles, fishes with adaman- tine blade-like tails. It abounds in aquatic creatures capable of piercing through the flesh.

## \|\| 47.6 \|\|

*udyānti dvādaśādityāḥ pralayānte tathā hi te \|*  
*tapanti tatra vai martyāḥ kranda mānāstu pāpinaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. There blaze as many as twelve suns as it were the time of Deluge. The sinful people groan aloud and fall into it.

## \|\| 47.7 \|\|

*hā bhrātaḥ putra tāteti pralapanti muhurmuhuḥ \|*  
*vicaranti nimajjanti glāniṃ gacchanti jantavaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. "Alas brother, alas son, alas mother", I they shout frequently. They try to swim about but sink in it.

## \|\| 47.8 \|\|

*caturvidhaiḥ prāṇigaṇairdṛṣṭā vyāptā mahānadī \|*  
*taranti gopradānena tvanyathā ca patanti te \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. It is incumbent on all mortals to witness the mighty river. Persons who had made gifts in their life-time can cross it easily, otherwise they sink into it.

## \|\| 47.9 \|\|

*māṃ narā ye 'vamanyante cācāryaṃ gurumeva ca \|*  
*vṛddhānanyāṃścāpi mūḍhāsteṣāṃ vāsastu tatra vai \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Those who have slighted their mothers, preceptors or priests can stay there permanently.

## \|\| 47.10 \|\|

*pativratāṃ sādhuśīlāmūḍhāṃ dharmeṣu niścalām \|*  
*parityajanti ye mūḍhāsteṣāṃ vāsastu santatam \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. So also those who forsake their chaste, virtuous and noble wives without any fault.

## \|\| 47.11 \|\|

*viśvāsapratipannānāṃ svāmimitratapasvinām \|*  
*strībālavikalādīnāṃ vadhaṃ kṛtvā patanti hi \|*  
*pacyante tatra madhye tu krandamānāstu pāpinaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Those who deceive their credulous masters, friends and sages, women, children, cripples and others get submerged in the putrid slough and groan there painfully.

## \|\| 47.12 \|\|

*śāntaṃ bubhukṣitaṃ vipraṃyo vighnāyopasarpati \|*  
*krimibhirbhakṣyate tatra yāvadābhūtasaṃplavam \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. One who attacks a hungry Brahmin or reproaches him is eaten up by worms so long as this world lasts.

## \|\| 47.13-15 \|\|

*brāhmaṇāya pratīśrutya yamārthaṃ na dadāti tam \|*  
*āhūya nāsti yo brūyāttasya vāsastu tatra vai \|\| 13 \|\|*

*agnido garadaścaiva kūṭasākṣī ca madyapaḥ \|*  
*yajñavidhvaṃsakaścaiva rājñīgāmī ca paiśunaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

*kathābhaṅgakaraścaiva svayandattā pahārakaḥ \|*  
*kṣetrasetuvibhedī ca paradārapradharṣakaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

13-15. One who promises a Brahmin to give him a sum but does not give, one who defiles a sacrifice, one who rapes a noble woman, one who slanders others, one who interrupts religious discourses, one who is guilty of perjury, one who is a wine-addict or one who invites Brahmins but refuses to feed them these stay there permanently.

## \|\| 47.16-17 \|\|

*brāhmaṇo rasavikretā tathā yo vṛṣalīpatiḥ \|*  
*godhanasya tṛṣārtasya vāpyā bhedaṃ karoti yaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

*kanyāvidūṣakaścaiva dānaṃ dattvānutāpakaḥ \|*  
*śūbadrastu kapilāpāyī brāhmaṇo māṃsabhojanaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. One who acts as an incendiary, one who poisons others, one who seizes what is given, one who destroys fields and breaks bonds, one who defiles other men's wives or being a Brahmin sells wine or marries a slave girl, one who harasses cattle oppressed by thirst, one who outrages the modesty of a virgin, one who terrorises persons worthy of receiving gifts, a sudra drinking the milk of a brown cow, a Brahmin eating at — these stay there permanently.

## \|\| 47.19-21 \|\|

*sadāmarṣo sadā krodhī nijavākyapramāṇakṛt \|*  
*paroktyucchedako nityaṃ vaitaraṇyā vasecciram \|\| 19 \|\|*

*yastvahaṅkāravānpāpī svavikatthanakārakaḥ \|*  
*kṛtaghno garbhasantāpī vaitaraṇyāṃ sa majjati \|\| 20 \|\|*

*kadāpi bhāgyayogena taraṇecchā bhavedyadi \|*  
*sānukūlā bhavedyena tadākarṇaya kāśyapa \|\| 21 \|\|*

19-21. A miser, an atheist, a worthless wretch, a person extremely furious and irritated, a person who considers his own words authoritative, a person who contradicts what others say, haughty egotist, a swaggerer, an ungrateful, treacherous, fellow-all these persons stay at Vaitaram for an indefinite period.

## \|\| 47.21 \|\|

*kadāpi bhāgyayogena taraṇecchā bhavedyadi \|*  
*sānukūlā bhavedyena tadākarṇaya kāśyapa \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. О son of Казуара, if one is fortunate to cross it, it is due to these reasons which are favourable. To these you will listen now

## \|\| 47.22-23 \|\|

*ayane viṣuve puṇye vyatīpāte dinodaye \|*  
*candrasūryoparāge vā saṃkrāntau darśavāsare \|\| 22 \|\|*

*anyeṣu puṇyakāleṣu dīyate dānamuttamam \|*  
*yadā tadā bhavedvāpi śrāddhā dānaṃ prati dhruvam \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. In the equinoxes, in the holy Vyatipata, at the end of a day, during eclipses, during the transit of the sun to a different zodiacal sign, on. the new moon day and other auspicious occasions if a thing is gifted to a Brahmin it is laudable. Whenever gifts are made with faith the riches becomeeverlasfing.

## \|\| 47.24 \|\|

*tadaiba dānakālaḥ syādyataḥ sampattirasthirā \|*  
*anityāni śarīrāṇi vibhavo naiva śāśvataḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Bodies are perishable, riches are transitory, death, is ever present. Hence, virtue should be accumulated.

## \|\| 47.25-29 \|\|

*nityaṃ sannihito mṛtyuḥ kartavyo dharmasaṃgahaḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇāṃ vā pāṭalāṃ vāpi kuryādvaitaraṇīṃ śubhām \|\| 25 \|\|*

*svarṇaśṛṅgīṃ raupyakhurāṃ kāṃsyapātropadohanīm \|*  
*kṛṣṇavastrayugācchannāṃ saptadhānyasamanvitām \|\| 26 \|\|*

*kārpāsadroṇaśikhare āsīnaṃ tāmrabhājane \|*  
*yamaṃ haimaṃ prakurvīta lohadaṇḍasamanvitam \|*  
*ikṣudaṇḍamayaṃ baddhvā plavaṃ sudṛḍhabandhanaiḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

*uḍupopari tāṃ dhenuṃ sūryadehasamudbhavām \|*  
*kṛtvā prakalpayedvipraśchatropānahasaṃyutam \|\| 28 \|\|*

*aṅgulīyakavāsāṃsi brāhmaṇāya nivedayet \|*  
*imamuccārayenmantraṃ saṃgṛhya sajalānkuśān \|\| 29 \|\|*

25-29. The gift of Vaitarani cow should be made as follows:

The colour of the cow should be either black or tawny. Its horns should be covered in gold, its hoofs in silver. A copper vessel should be given representing the milking vessel. Two. black clothes should adorn the cow. Seven kinds of grains, should be kept in vessels for gift. A golden idol of Yama should be made with a copper rod in hand. A canoe should be made with sugar cane. The cow should be brought over that raft. It should be meditated as born out of the sun. An umbrella, a pair of sandais, a ring and a pair of clothes should be gifted, to a Brahmin. Holding the kusa grass and water in the hand, the person should recite the following mantra —

## \|\| 47.30-32 \|\|

*yamadvāre mahāghore śrutvā vaitaraṇīṃ tadīm \|*  
*tartukāmo dadāmyenāṃ tubhyaṃ vaitaraṇīṃ namaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

*gāvo me agrataḥ santu gāvo me santu pārśvataḥ \|*  
*gāvo me hṛdaye santu gavāṃ madhye vasāmyaham \|\| 31 \|\|*

*viṣṇurūpa dvijaśreṣṭha māmuddhara mahīsura \|*  
*sadakṣiṇā mayā dattā tubhyaṃ vaitaraṇīnamaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

30-32. "I have heard that there is a river Vaitarani at Yama's abode. I wish to cross it. Hence I give you this symbolic Vaitarani cow. О Brahmin, in the form of Visnu, thou sanchtifiest the cow, thou art a god on earth. This Vaitarani cow is given to thee along with daksina. May cows stand in front of me. May cows stand at my back. May cows abide in. my heart. I stay in the midst of cows."

## \|\| 47.33 \|\|

*dharmarājañca sarveśaṃ vaitaraṇyākhyadhenukām \|*  
*sarvaṃ pradakṣiṇīkṛtya brāhmaṇāya nivedayet \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. The person circumambulates the idol of Yama and the Vaitarani cow and gives it to the Brahmin.

## \|\| 47.34-35 \|\|

*pucchaṃ saṃgṛhya dhenvāśca agre kṛtvā tu vai vdijam \|*  
*dhenuke tvaṃ pratīkṣasva yamadvāre mahābhaye \|\| 34 \|\|*

*uttāraṇāya deveśi vaitaraṇye namo 'stu te \|*  
*anuvrajettu gacchantaṃ sarvaṃ tasya gṛhaṃ nayet \|\| 35 \|\|*

34-35. The Brahmin stands in front while the householder holds the tail of the cow saying "O cow, you wait for me at the terrific threshold of Yama for lifting me up Obeisance to Vaitarani, Obeisance". Thereafter, he follows the Brahmin with the articles of gift to his house.

## \|\| 47.36 \|\|

*evaṃ kṛte vainateya sā saritsutarā bhavet \|*  
*sarvānkāmānavāpnoti yo dadyādbhuvi mānavaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. О son of Vinata, by making gifts, the river becomes, easy to cross and the giftmaker obtains all that he wishes to possess.

## \|\| 47.37 \|\|

*sukṛtasya prabhāveṇa sukhañceha paratra ca \|*  
*svasthe sahasraguṇitamāture śatasaṃmitam \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. As a result of his noble actions one obtains pleasure here and hereafter. Its efficacy is increased a thousandfold if a healthy man makes this gift. If a sick man makes the gift its efficacy is only a hundredfold.

## \|\| 47.38 \|\|

*mṛtasyaiva tu yaddānaṃ parokṣe tatsamaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*svahastena tato deyaṃ mṛte kaḥ kasya dāsyatī \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. If a thing is gifted on behalf of the dead by his son or descendent, the gift is indirect and its efficacy is rendered normal. Hence, gifts should be made by one's own self. After death who will care al gift for him?

## \|\| 47.39-40 \|\|

*dānadharmavihīnānāṃ kṛpaṇairjīvetakṣitaiḥ \|*  
*asthireṇa śarīreṇa sthiraṃ karma samācaret \|\| 39 \|\|*

*avaśyameva yāsyanti prāṇāḥ prāghuṇi (ghūrṇi) kā iva \|\| 40 \|\|*

39-40. The life of a person devoid of gifts and virtue is pitiable. Then why not achieve a permanent fruit with the help of perishable body Vital airs are only guests and they go away for certain sooner or later.

## \|\| 47.41 \|\|

*itīdamuktaṃ tava pakṣirāja viḍambanaṃ jantugaṇasya sarvam \|*  
*pretasya mokṣāya tadauddhvandaihikaṃ hitāya lokasya carecchubhāya tu \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. O lord of birds, thus I have told you all about the delusion of living beings. The rites of obsequies are performed, for the redemption of the dead. If men understand this auspicious advice it bestows benefit on them.

## \|\| 47.42-43 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*evaṃ viprāḥ samāddiṣṭo viṣṇunā prabhaviṣṇunā \|*  
*garuḍa pretacaritaṃ śrutvā santuṣṭimāgataḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

*vratatīrthādikaṃ sarvaṃ punaḥ papraccha keśavam \|*  
*dhyātvā manasi sarveśaṃ sarvakāraṇakāraṇam \|\| 43 \|\|*

42-43. О brahmins, this is what the omnipotent Visnu has ordained Garuda was delighted on hearing the details of the dead- He asked the lord again about various rites and holy centres after meditating on the lord who is the cause of all causes.

## \|\| 47.44 \|\|

*ṛṣayaḥ sarvamevaitajjantūnāṃ prabhavādikam \|*  
*maraṇaṃ janma ca tathā pretatvañcaurdhvadaihikam \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. О sages, these details on the origin of creatures which I have mentioned to you are conducive to salvation, as also the rites of obsequies. I shall now mention the great panacea for the removal of ills and sufferings of this mortal world.

## \|\| 47.45 \|\|

*mayā proktaṃ vai muktyai nidānaṃ caiva sarvaśaḥ \|*  
*lābhasteṣāṃ jayasteṣāṃ kutasteṣāṃ parājayaḥ \|*  
*yeṣāmindīvaraśyāmo hṛdayastho janārdanaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. Profit is theirs, success is theirs whose heart is set on Visnu whose body has the hue of a blue lotus. How can there be a failure in store for those persons?

## \|\| 47.46 \|\|

*dharmo jayati nādharmaḥ satyaṃ jayati nānṛtam \|*  
*kṣamā jayati na krodho viṣṇurjayati nāsurāḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Dharma wins, not adharma; truth wins not falsehood; forgiveness wins not anger; Visnu wins not demons.

## \|\| 47.47 \|\|

*viṣṇurmātā pitā viṣṇurviṣṇuḥ svajanabāndhavāḥ \|*  
*yeṣāmeva sthirā buddhirna teṣāṃ durgatirbhavet \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. Visnu is mother, Visnu, is father, Visnu is kin. No mishap accrues to those whose heart is set on Visnu.

## \|\| 47.48 \|\|

*maṅgalaṃ bhagavānviṣṇurmaṅgalaṃ garuḍadhvajaḥ \|*  
*maṅgalaṃ puṇḍarīkākṣo maṅgalāyatanaṃ hariḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Auspicious is lord Visnu. Auspicious is the lord who has Garuda for his banner. Auspicious is the lord whose eyes resemble the blue lotus. The lord is the store-house of suspiciousness.

## \|\| 47.49 \|\|

*harirbhāgīrathī viprā viprā bhāgīrathī hariḥ \|*  
*bhāgīrathī harirviprāḥ sārametajjagattraye \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Meditation on lord Visnu, worship of the holy river Ganga and the Brahmins-the three constitute the quintessence of merit in the three worlds.

## \|\| 47.50 \|\|

*iti sūtamukhodgīrṇāṃ sarvaśāstrārthamaṇḍitām \|*  
*vaiṣṇaviṃ vāksudhāṃ pītvā ṛṣayastuṣṭimāyayuḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. On drinking the nectar in the form of hearing the lord's sermon breathing the essence of all sastras, through the-mouth of Shuta sage баипака and others were highly satisfied.

## \|\| 47.51 \|\|

*praśaśaṃsustathānyonyaṃ sūtaṃ sarvārthadarśinam \|*  
*praharṣamatulaṃ prāpurmunayaḥ śaunakādayaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. They lauded Suta, well-versed in the brahmanical scriptures and were highly delighted on hearing the Puranic lore.

## \|\| 47.52 \|\|

*apavitraḥ pavitro vā sarvāvasthāṃ gatopi vā \|*  
*yaḥ smaretpuṇḍarīkākṣaṃ sa bāhyābhyantaraṃ śuciḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. A person achieves purity externally as well as internally if he meditates on the lotuseyed Visnu, no matter an whatever state, pure or impure he may be passing through.

## Chapter 48

## \|\| 48.1 \|\|

*tārkṣya uvāca \|*  
*ye martyaloke nivasanti mānavāste sarvajātau nidhanaṃ prayānti \|*  
*kāle svakīye nijapuṇyasaṃkhyayā vadanti lāka kathasva tanme \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. The people of all castes who live in this mortal world die at their own time and obtain different worlds according to the magnitude of their pious deeds.

## \|\| 48.2 \|\|

*gacchanti mārgeṇa sudustareṇa vidhātṛniṣpāditavartmani sthitāḥ \|*  
*kenaiva puṇyena mudaṃ prayānti tiṣṭhanti kenaiva kulaṃ balaṃ vayaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. They go on different paths ordained by God. Through what virtue they obtain pleasure and through what merit they get family, strength and age? Tell me О lord.

## \|\| 48.3 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*śrutvātha devo garuḍaṃ tvavocat smṛtvā vapuḥ karmabhayañca rūpam \|*  
*sṛṣṭā dharā yena carācaraṃ jagatsa yena śastā vihito yamo vibhuḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. On hearing this, the lord said to Garuda, explaining in detail how the body functions, how it is bound by actions, how this world mobile and immobile-goes how it is created, and how and by whom it is administered.

## \|\| 48.4-6 \|\|

*śrībhagavānuvāca \|*  
*dharmārthakāmaṃ ciramokṣasañcayamanyaṃ dvitīyaṃ yamamārgagāminām \|*  
*praviśyacāṅguṣṭhasame sa tatra vai taṃ prāpya dehaṃ svamandiram? \|\| 4 \|\|*

*gṛhītapāśo rudate punaḥ punardeśe supuṇye dvija dehasaṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*devendrapūjā pitṛdevatṛptidaṃ mohānna ceṣṭaṃ na ca puttrasantatiḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

*na me 'sti bandhuryamamārgagāmino mayā na kṛtyaṃ dvijadehalipsayā \|*  
*samprāpya vipratvamatīva durlabhaṃ nādhītavānvedapurāṇasaṃhitāḥ \|*  
*prāptaṃ suratnaṃ karasaṃsthitaṃ gataṃ dehankvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 6 \|\|*

4-6. For those walking on the path of Yama, the four vargas — dharma, artha, kama and moksa — are secondary. Having entered the body measuring a thumb of his own hand and being held by the noose, he weeps again and again and cries: "I was having the body of a brahmin in the pious country of, Bharata, yet due to infatuation I did not worship lord Indra or perform rites for propitiating the manes and deities. I had no relations, no sons and no progeny. Due to fondness for my body, I did not act in right direction. I got the rare brahmana-hood yet I did not.study the Vedas and Puranas. The gem that I got and which I hold in my palm was lost. О my soul, now suffer for whatever I have done in my previous life.

## \|\| 48.7 \|\|

*yaḥ kṣattriyo bāhubalena saṃyuge lalāṭadeśādrudhiraṃ mukhe papau \|*  
*tatsomapānaṃ hi kṛtaṃ mahāmakhe jīvanmṛtaḥ so 'pi hi yāti muktik \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. The ksatriya who has drunk blood from his forehead in the battle, has as well drunk Soma in die sacrifice; dead or alive he gets, release.

## \|\| 48.8 \|\|

*sthānānyanekāni kṛtāni tāni pītānyanekānyapi garhitāni \|*  
*śastraṃ gṛhītvā samare ripūṇāṃ yaḥ saṃmukhaṃ yāti sa muktapāpaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Though he may have done many impious acts and drunk many undesirable drinks, if he takes weapons and faces the enemy in the war he is released from sins immediately.

## \|\| 48.9-10 \|\|

*kṣattrānvayo vāpi viśonvayo vā śūdrānvayo vāpi hi nīcavarṇaḥ \|*  
*saṃgrāmadevadvijabālaghātī strīvṛddhahā dīnatapasvihantā \|\| 9 \|\|*

*upadruteṣveṣu parāṅmukho yaḥ syustasya devāḥ sakalāḥ parāṅmukhāḥ \|*  
*tilodakaṃ naiva pibanti pūrve hutaṃ na gṛhṇāti hutāśanopi tat \|\| 10 \|\|*

9-10. One may be a ksatriya or a vaisya or a sudra or one may belong to a low caste, if he kills in war the nobles of the cultured, children, women or old men, the poor or the ascetics or remains indifferent when these are in trouble, the deities become indifferent to him. The manes do not receive his libations of gingelly water and the fire does not receive his offering of oblation.

## \|\| 48.11 \|\|

*dveṣādbhayādvā samare samāgate śastraṃ gṛhītvā parasainyasaṃmukhaḥ \|*  
*na yāti pakṣīndra mṛśca paścātkṣāttraṃ balaṃ tasya gataṃ tathaiva \|*  
*dvijāya dattvā kanakaṃ mahīmimāṃ bhūyaḥ sa paścādbhavatīha loke \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Due to policy or fear, if a person does not face the foe in war, О bird, know it for certain that he is sure to die one day afterwards but before that his ksatrahood goes in vain. If he donates gold or earth to a brahmana, he is born in human form, in this world in a noble and illustrious family.

## \|\| 48.12 \|\|

*dānaṃ pradattaṃ grahaṇe dvijendre snānaṃ kṛtaṃ tena sadā sutīrthe \|*  
*gatvā gayāyāṃ pitṛpiṇḍadānaṃ kṛtaṃ sadā yo mriyate tu yuddhe \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. He who dies in war is deemed to have given gifts during eclipse, to have bathed in a holy place, to have gone to Gaya and offered rice-ball to the manes.

## \|\| 48.13 \|\|

*yaḥ kṣāttradehantu vihāya śocate raṇāṅgaṇe svāmivadhe ca gograhe \|*  
*strībālaghāte pathi sārthahetave mayā svakośaṃ na hataṃ na pātitam \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. A ksatriya repents that in the battlefield, at the-time of his master's murder or when the cows were seized or forcibly carried away by the foe, when women and children were killed or when his companions were in trouble he did not use his sword.

## \|\| 48.14 \|\|

*vaiśyaḥ svakarmāṇi viśocate tadā gṛhītapāśo na mayāpi sañcitam \|*  
*satyaṃ na coktaṃ kraya vikrayeṇa mohādvimūḍhena kuṭumbahetave \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. When a vaisya is caught in the noose, he repents that he did not cherish truth in business transactions due to greed in support of his family.

## \|\| 48.15 \|\|

*śūdraṃ vapuḥ prāpya yaśaskaraṃ sadā dānaṃ dvijebhyo na kṛtaṃ dvijārcanam \|*  
*cdṛdadyatdadvaḍḍha ḍhaddhadṛthr dadṛdhḍḍhathrḍaḍḍhaddha*  
*jalāśayo naiva kṛto dharātale asaṃskṛto vipravaro na saṃskṛtaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. A sudra repents that having obtained body he neither gave reputable gifts to the brahmanas nor worshipped them nor built a tank on the earth.

## \|\| 48.16 \|\|

*tyaktvā svakarmāṇi madena susthitaṃ mayā sutīrthe svavapurna cojjhitam \|*  
*dharmorjito naiva na devapūjanaṃ kṛtaṃ mayā caiva vimuktihetave \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. \[The Jiva that has left the body thinks thus:\] "I abandoned my Family profession. I lived in pride. 1 did not give up my ghost in a holy place. I did not earn virtue or worship God for release."

## \|\| 48.17 \|\|

*dehaṃ samāsādya tathaiva piṇḍajaṃ varṇāṃstathaivāntyajamlecchasaṃjñitān \|*  
*marunmayaṃ dehamime viśanti naivehamānāḥ pathi dharmasaṃkule \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Such people are born as mlecchas, outcastes, etc. Having given up their physical bodies they enter into airy bodies and become averse to religious activities.

## \|\| 48.18 \|\|

*parasparaṃ dharmakṛntaṃ svakīyaṃ sampādya lakṣyaṃ pathi sañcarantsvam \|*  
*pakṣīndra vākyāni śṛṇuṣva tāni manoramāṇi pravadanti yāni \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Whatever religious acts they have done keeping them in view and moving in the way, hear О bird, what they speak about among themselves.

## \|\| 48.19 \|\|

*sārā hi lokeṣu bhavettrilokī dvīpeṣu sarveṣu ca jambukākhyam \|*  
*deśeṣu sarveṣvapi devadeśaḥ jīveṣu sarveṣu manuṣya eva \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. The three in the world are the best of all: Jambu-dvlpa among the dvipas, the land of the bright among the countries of the world and human beings amongst all creatures on the earth.

## \|\| 48.20 \|\|

*varṇāśca catvāra iha praśastāḥ varṇeṣu dharmiṣṭhanarāḥ praśastāḥ \|*  
*dharmeṇa saukhyaṃ samupaiti sarvaṃ jñānaṃ samāpnoti mahāpathe sthitaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. There are four castes: brahmin, ksatriya, vaisya and sudra Among these' the brahmin caste is the best. People can derive pleasure from religion. When they start on the High way, leaving their bodies after death, they revive their previous assoiciatfons.

## \|\| 48.21 \|\|

*dehaṃ parityajya yadā gatāyuḥ pakṣin sthito 'haṃ kṛmikīṭasaṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*sarīsṛpo 'haṃ maśako vinirmitaścatuṣpado 'haṃ vanasūkaro 'ham \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. I stayed as worm and insect. I was a reptile. I was a mosquito. I was a quadruped. I was a wild boar/

## \|\| 48.22 \|\|

*sarvaṃ vijānāti hi garbhasaṃsthito jātaśca sadyastadidañca vismaret \|*  
*yaccintitaṃ garbhasamāgatena vai bālo yuvā vṛddhavayā babhūva \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Staying in the womb, he recollects everything. But coming out of the womb he forgets whatever he thought while he was in the womb. After birth he passes through three stages: childhood, youth and old age.

## \|\| 48.23 \|\|

*mohādvināṣṭaṃ yadi garbhacintitaṃ smṛtaṃ punarmṛtyugate cadehe \|*  
*tasminpranaṣṭe hṛdi cintitaṃ gataṃ smṛtaṃ punargarbhagate ca dehe \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Through infatuation the thoughts of the womb are soon forgotten but they revive when the body succumbs to death. When the body is destroyed, thoughts remain with the self. They revive when the self enters into the womb and takes up another body.

## \|\| 48.24 \|\|

*tasminpranaṣṭe hṛdi cintitaṃ punarmayā svakośe paravañcanaṃ kṛtam \|*  
*dyūtaiśchalenāpi ca cauryavṛttyā dharmaṃ vyatikramya śarīrarakṣaṇe \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. When that is again destroyed, thoughts remain in the self. The process goes on till the eternal release is accomplished.

"In my body I cheated others, gambling, cheating and stealing. I lived by transgressing religion."

## \|\| 48.25 \|\|

*kṛcchreṇa lakṣmīḥ samupārjitā svayaṃ mayā na bhuktaṃ manasepsitaṃ dhanam \|*  
*tāmbūlamannaṃ madhuraṃ sagorasaṃ dattvāgnidevātithibandhuvarge \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. "I struggled hard for earning money. I did not enjoy riches to my satiety. I did not offer betel, com, milk to fire, deities, guests and relatives."

## \|\| 48.26 \|\|

*somagrahe sūryasamāgamepi vā na sevitaṃ tīrthavariṣṭhamuttamam \|*  
*kośaṃ svakīyaṃ malamūtrapūritaṃ dehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. "Even during the solar or limar eclipse I did not visit holy places. My body was full of waste and urine. Now, О soul, suffer for what you did in your previous body."

## \|\| 48.27 \|\|

*mayā na dṛṣṭā na natā na pūjitā traivikramī mūrtiriha sthitā bhuvi \|*  
*prabhāsanātho na ca bhaktisaṃstuto dehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. "I did not see nor bowed to nor worshipped lord Visnu's idoi on the earth. I also did not devoutly praise the lord of Prabhasa, Hence, О soul, suffer for what you did in your previous body."

## \|\| 48.28 \|\|

*gatvā variṣṭhe bhuvi tīrthasannidhau dhanaṃ na dattaṃ viduṣāṃ kare mayā \|*  
*āplutya dehaṃ vidhinā dvije gurau dihinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. "Having gone to the admirable land near a holy place, I did not put money in the scholar's hand, nor gave it to a preceptor after taking ablution in the holy water. Hence, О soul, now suffer for what you did in your previous body."

## \|\| 48.29 \|\|

*na mātṛpūjā na ca viṣṇuśaṅkarau gaṇeśacaṇaḍyau na ca bhāskaro 'pi vā \|*  
*yañcopacārairbaliyuktacandanairdehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. "I did not worship the mother Goddess, nor Visnu nor Sankara, nor Ganesa, nor Candl nor the sun with due rites by offering sandal paste, etc. Now, О soul suffer for what you did in your previous body."

## \|\| 48.30 \|\|

*labdhā mayā mānavadevatopamā mohādgatā sarvamidañca pārthiva \|*  
*gatiṃ na vīkṣeta sa vai vimūḍhadhīrdehinkvacinnistara yattvayā kṛtam \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. "I obtained the title of deity even as a man. But due to infatuation I lost that glory. I was 4 fool not to own my infirmities. О soul, now suffer for what you did in your previous body."

## \|\| 48.31 \|\|

*etāni pakṣinmanasā vicintya vākyāni dharmārthayaśaskarāṇi \|*  
*muktiṃ samāyānti manuṣyaloke vasanti ye dharmaratāḥ sudeśe \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Having thought over these points, О bird, which grant virtue, wealth and fame, man obtains release perpetually.

## \|\| 48.32 \|\|

*iti bruvāṇairyamadhūtavargairvihanyate kālamayaiśca mudgaraiḥ \|*  
*hā daiva hā daiva iti smaran vai dhanaṃ na dattaṃ svayamarjitaṃ yat \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Being addressed thus by the messengers of Yama the dead are struck with clubs. They cry 'O fate, О fate' and curse themselves that the money earned by them was not gifted to the deserving brahmins.

## \|\| 48.33 \|\|

*na bhūmidānaṃ na ca gopradānaṃ na vāridānaṃ na ca vastradānam \|*  
*phalaṃ satāmbūlavilepanaṃ vā tvayā na dattaṃ bhuvi śocase katham \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. The emissaries of Yama tell again 'you neither gifted the earth nor cow nor water nor cloth nor fruit nor betel nor ointment in your life on this earth. Then why do you lament?

## \|\| 48.34 \|\|

*pitā mṛtaste ca pitāmahaḥ sā yayā dhṛto vāpyudare svakīye \|*  
*mṛto 'pyasau bandhujanaḥ samasto dṛṣṭaṃ tvayā sarvamidaṃ gatāyaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Your father died, your grandfather died. She also died who bore you in her womb. Your relations also died. You saw them all dead.

## \|\| 48.35 \|\|

*kośaṃ tvadīyaṃ jvalitañca vahninā puttrairgṛhīto dhanadhānya sañcayaḥ \|*  
*subhāṣitaṃ dharmacayaṃ kṛtañca yattadeva gacchettava pṛṣṭhasaṃstham \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Your body has been burnt by fire. Your wealth and corn are taken over by your sons. Whatever good and virtuous actions you did those only will go along with you.

## \|\| 48.36 \|\|

*na dṛśyate ko 'pi mṛtaḥ samāgato rājā yatirvā dvijapuṅgavo 'pi vā \|*  
*yo vai mṛtaḥ sāhasikaḥ sa martyako nāśaṃ yo 'pi dharātale sthitaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. None who is dead can ever come back, may he be a king, a mendicant or a brahmana. He who dies in the battle-field is also dead and he who survives is also dead.

## \|\| 48.37 \|\|

*evaṃ gaṇāste bruvate sakinnarā dhairyaṃ samālambya vipādapūritaḥ \|*  
*śrutvā gaṇānāṃ vacanaṃ mahādbhutaṃ bravīti pakṣīndra manuṣyatāṃ gataḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Thus speak those ganas along with the kinnaras and he though sad at heart hears but patiently their strange utterances. Envested with an aerial body and sitting in the aerial car due to the influence of gifts, he gives out in speech his impassioned thought.

## \|\| 48.38 \|\|

*dānaprabhāveṇa vimānasaṃsthito dharmaḥ pitā mātṛdayānurūpiṇī \|*  
*vāṇī kalatraṃ madhurārthabhāṣiṇī snānaṃ sutīrthe ca subandhavargaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. "Dharma is father, compassion is mother, speech is sweet-tongued wife, bath in a holy place is equal to relatives.

## \|\| 48.39 \|\|

*karārpitaṃ yatsukṛtaṃ samastaṃ svargastadā syāttava kiṅkaropamaḥ \|*  
*yo dharmavān prāpsyati so 'tisaukhyaṃ pāpī samastaṃ vividhañca duḥkham \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Whatever good is done by hand the same is heaven. A religious person is a symbol of happiness and a sinner is all misery.

## \|\| 48.40 \|\|

*yo dharmaśīlo jitamānaroṣo vidyāvinīto na paropatāpī \|*  
*svadāratuṣṭaḥ paradāradūraḥsa vai naro no bhuvi vandanīyaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. That man on the earth deserves praise who is religious, who has conquered pride and anger, who is humble though learned, who does not trouble others in vain, who is satisfied with his wife and keeps away from unlawful sexual desire.

## \|\| 48.41 \|\|

*miṣṭānnadātā caritāgnihotro vedāntaviccandrasahasrajīvī \|*  
*māsopavāsī ca pativratā caṣaḍ jīvaloke mama vandanīyāḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. He who offers sweets, he who performs Agnihotra, has studied Vedanta, has performed religious rites fasts for a month within a year and remains chaste-these six in this world are worthy of honour.

## \|\| 48.42 \|\|

*evaṃ samācārayuto naro 'pi vāpīṃ sakūpāṃ sajalaṃ taḍāgam \|*  
*prapāśubhaṃ hṛdgṛhadevamandiraṃ kṛtaṃ nareṇaiva sa dharmauttamaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. A man of good conduct can also be put in this category. A VapI (an oblong reservoir of water) a well, a tank, a cistern and temple of a deity in the heart of a devotee constitute

## \|\| 48.43 \|\|

*varṣāśanaṃ vedavide ca dattaṃ kanyāvivāhastvṛṇamocanaṃ dvije \|*  
*bhūmiḥ sukṛṣṭāpi tṛṣārtihetostadevametaṃ sukṛtat samastam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Feeding a Vedic scholar for a year, arranging the marriage of a Brahmin's daughter, freeing a brahmin family from debt, tilling land and digging well to meet the need of a thirsty and hungry person constitutes a virtuous act.

## \|\| 48.44 \|\|

*adhyāyamenaṃ sukṛtasya sāraṃ śṛṇoti gāyatyapi bhāvaśuddhyā \|*  
*sa vai kulīnaḥ sa ca dharmayukto viśvālayaṃ yāti paraṃ sa nūnam \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Whosoever with a pure mind hears or recites this chapter on the essence of virtue is considered to be noble and religious. He goes to the highest world after death.

## Chapter 49

## \|\| 49.1 \|\|

*adhunā śrotumicchāmi mokṣopāyaṃ sanātanam \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. О ocean of mercy! I have heard that this world of creatures is born out of ignorance. Now, I wish to hear the infallible method of Final Release.

## \|\| 49.2 \|\|

*bhagavandevadeveśa śaraṇāgatavatsala \|*  
*asāre ghorasaṃsāre sarvaduḥ khamalīmase \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. О God, О deity of deities, О lover of refugees in this insignificant world filled with the filth of sorrows!

## \|\| 49.3 \|\|

*nānāvidhaśarīrasthā anantā jīvarāśayaḥ \|*  
*jāyante ca mriyante ca teṣāmanto na vidyate \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. There arc creatures staying in many bodies, being born and dying. There is no end to this process.

## \|\| 49.4 \|\|

*sadā duḥ khāturā eva na sakhī vidyate kkacit \|*  
*kenopāyena mokṣeśa mucyante vada me prabho \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. They are always suffering, none of them is happy. О lord of moksa, tell me how one is released.

## \|\| 49.5 \|\|

*śrībhagavānubāca \|*  
*śṛṇu tārkṣya pravakṣyāmi yanmāṃ tvaṃ paripṛcchasi \|*  
*yasya śravaṇamātreṇa saṃsārānmucyate naraḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Hear, О bird, I shall tell you what you ask about. Simply by hearing the same, you will get release from the world of mortals.

## \|\| 49.6-7 \|\|

*asti devaḥ parabrahmasvarūpo niṣkalaḥ śivaḥ \|*  
*sarvajñaḥ sarvakartā ca sarveśo nirmalo 'dvayaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

*svayañjyotiranādyanto nirvikāraḥ parātparaḥ \|*  
*nirguṇaḥ saccidānandastadaṃśā jīvasaṃjñakāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

6-7. There is God, transcendental self, indivisible Shiva, all-knowing, all-doing, lord of all, pure, without a second, self-luminous, without beginning, without end, unchangeable the highest of the high, attribuleless and of the nature of existence, consciousness and bliss.

## \|\| 49.8 \|\|

*anādyavidyopahatā yathāgnau visphuliṅgakāḥ \|*  
*dehādyupādhisambhinnāste karmabhiranādibhiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The creatures are his parts and parcels. Like sparks of fire being struck by the beginning less knowledge, they separate into different bodies, through beginningless actions.

## \|\| 49.9 \|\|

*sukhaduḥ khapradaiḥ puṇyapārūpairniyantritāḥ \|*  
*tattajjātiyutaṃ dehamāyurbhogañca karmajam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. They are controlled by virtues and vicc,s in the form of bliss and sorrow. Their bodies have different castes, age and enjoyment born of their different actions.

## \|\| 49.10 \|\|

*pratijanma prapadyante teṣāmapi paraṃ punaḥ \|*  
*sasūkṣmaliṅgaśarīramāmokṣādakṣaraṃ khaga \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Then again in every birth, О bird, they obtain subtle bodies and after attaining moksa they acquire indestructible frame.

## \|\| 49.11 \|\|

*sthāvarāḥ kṛmayaścājāḥ pakṣiṇaḥ paśavo nagaḥ \|*  
*dhārmikāstridaśāstadvanmokṣiṇaśca yathākramam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. The departed souls enter into insentient objects, worms, birds, animals, men, deities but after release do not enter into any object or any body at all.

## \|\| 49.12 \|\|

*caturvidhaśarīrāṇi dhṛtvā muktvā sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*sukṛtānmā navo bhūtvā jñānī cenmokṣamāpnuyāt \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Passing through four types of bodies in order of their karman and leaving are body after the other a thousand limes, taking birth in human form and acquiring knowledge due to good acts one obtains release.

## \|\| 49.13 \|\|

*caturaśītilakṣeṣu śarīreṣu śarīriṇām \|*  
*na mānuṣaṃ vinānyatra tattvajñānantu labhyate \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. In the eightyfour lacs of bodies of creatures one does not acquire true knowledge anywhere unless one is born as man.

## \|\| 49.14 \|\|

*atra janmasahasrāṇāṃ sahasrairapi koṭibhiḥ \|*  
*kadācillabhate janturmānuṣyaṃ puṇyasañcayāt \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Here, after thousands of crores of births a creature obtains human form only sometime due to the aggregate of virtue.

## \|\| 49.15 \|\|

*sopānabhūtaṃ mokṣasya mānuṣyaṃ prāpya durlabham \|*  
*yastāra yati nātmānaṃ tasmātpāpataro 'tra kaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Having obtained a rare human form he should endeavour for moksa. If he does not endeavour for it, there can be no greater sinner in the world.

## \|\| 49.16 \|\|

*naraḥ prāpyetarajjanma labdhvā cendriyasauṣṭhavam \|*  
*na vettyātmahitaṃ yastu sa bhavedbrahmaghātakaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Born in the most beautiful human form he incurs the sin of slaying a brahmin if he neglects his self.

## \|\| 49.17 \|\|

*vinā dehena kasyāpi puruṣārtho na vidyate \|*  
*tasmāddehaṃ dhanaṃ rakṣetpuṇyakarmāṇi sādhayet \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Without human body it is not possible to obtain the supreme goal. One should be, therefore, very cautious to guard wealth in the form of his body and perform good actions.

## \|\| 49.18 \|\|

*rakṣeccasarvadātmānamātmā sarbvasya bhājanam \|*  
*rakṣaṇe yatnamātiṣṭhejjīvan bhadrāṇi paśyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. One should always protect self which is the receptacle of virtue. One should always try to look after the body at any cost.

## \|\| 49.19 \|\|

*punargrāmaḥ punaḥ kṣetra punarvittaṃ punargṛham \|*  
*punaḥ śubhāśubhaṃ karma na śarīraṃ punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. If a live, he may reap the result of his good actions. He may get a village, a field, a house or a wealth. But he may not get human body again.

## \|\| 49.20 \|\|

*śarīrarakṣaṇopāyāḥ kri yante sarvadā budhaiḥ \|*  
*necchanti ca punastyāgamapi kuṣṭhādirogiṇaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. A wise man finds out means to preserve his body. Even a leper does not wish to discard it.

## \|\| 49.21 \|\|

*tadgopitaṃ syāddharmārthaṃ dharmo jñānārthameva ca \|*  
*jñānaṃ tu dhyānayogārthamacirātpravimucyate \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Body is useful for Dhanna, Dharma for knowledge, knowledge fur meditation and meditation for immediate release.

## \|\| 49.22 \|\|

*ātmaiva yadi nātmānamahītebhyo nivārayet \|*  
*ko 'nyo hitakarastasmādātmānaṃ sukhayiṣyati \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. If a person cannot protect himself from evil then who else will do the same?

## \|\| 49.23 \|\|

*ihaiva narakavyādheścikitsāṃ na karoti yaḥ \|*  
*gatvā nirauṣadhaṃ deśaṃ vyādhimthaḥ kiṃ kariṣyati \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. If he cannot treat the disease here itself how shall he cure himself hereafter, at a place where there is no medicine?

## \|\| 49.24 \|\|

*vyāghrīvāste jarā cāyuryāti bhinnaghaṭāmbuvat \|*  
*nighnanti ripuvadrogāstasmācchreyaḥ samabhyaseta \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Old age is like a tigress. Age runs away like water from a leaking pot. The disease kills like an enemy. Hence, one should practice virtue alone.

## \|\| 49.25 \|\|

*yāvannāśrayate duḥ khaṃ yāvannāyānti cāpadaḥ \|*  
*yāvannendriyavaikalyaṃ tāvacchreyaḥ samabhyaset \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. So far as sorrow does not come, calamities do not approach, organs do not defunct, one should practice virtue.

## \|\| 49.26 \|\|

*yāvattiṣṭhati deho 'yaṃ tāvattattvaṃ samabhyaset \|*  
*sandīptakośabhavane kūpaṃ khanati durmatiḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. So far as this body remains intact he should practice virtue. One is a perfect fool who digs a well only when the house is on fire.

## \|\| 49.27 \|\|

*kālo na jñāyate nānākāryaiḥ saṃsārasambhavaiḥ \|*  
*sukhaṃ duḥkhaṃ jano hanta na vetti hitamātmanaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Time fleets while man is ignorant due to the pressure of work he is engrossed. People do not realize what is harmful or what is wholesome for them. They are deaf to their own interest.

## \|\| 49.28 \|\|

*jātānārtānmṛtānāpadbhaṣṭāndṛṣṭvā ca duḥ khitān \|*  
*loko mohasurāṃ pītvā na bibheti kadācana \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Even after seeing the distressed, the dead, the fallen and the aggrieved people do not ever fear having drunk the wine of infatuation.

## \|\| 49.29 \|\|

*sampadaḥ svapnasaṃkāśā yauvanaṃ kusumopamam \|*  
*taḍiccapalamāyuṣyaṃ kasya syājjānato dhṛtiḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Wealth is evanescentlike a dream, youth is fading like a flower, age h fickle like a lightning. Knowing this who can entertain fortitude?

## \|\| 49.30 \|\|

*śataṃ jīvitamatyalpaṃ nidrālasyaistadardhakam \|*  
*bālyarogajarāduḥ khairalpaṃ tadapi niṣphalam \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. A life of hundred years is too little. Half of that goes in sleep or idleness. Whatever little is left is wasted due to childhood, disease, old age and sorrows.

## \|\| 49.31 \|\|

*prārabdhavye nirudyogī jāgartavye prasuptakaḥ \|*  
*viśvastaśca bhayasthāne hā naraḥ ko na hanyate \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Alas! Is not that man dead who is idle at a place of action, sleeps at a place of awakening and is confident at a place of fear.

## \|\| 49.32 \|\|

*toyaphenasame dehe jīvenākramya saṃsthite \|*  
*anityāprayasavāse katha tiṣṭhati nirbhayaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. When the soul comes and stajrs in the body like the foam of water, when the company of the beloved is but temporary, how can a person stay fearless

## \|\| 49.33 \|\|

*ahite hitasaṃjñaḥ syādadhruve dhruvasaṃjñakaḥ \|*  
*anarthe cārthavijñānaḥ svamarthaṃ yo na vetti saḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. He who does not know reality calls as useful what is useless, as permanent what is impermanent and as meaningful what is meaningless.

## \|\| 49.34 \|\|

*paśyannapi praskhalati śṛṇvannapi na budhyati \|*  
*paṭhannapi na jānāti devamāyāvimohitaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Being infatuated by divine illusion he falters even seeing, misunderstands even, hearing and misses sense even reading.

## \|\| 49.35 \|\|

*tannimajjajjagadidaṃ gambhīre kālasāgare \|*  
*mṛtyurogajarāgrāhairna kaścidapi budhyate \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Even when the crocodiles in the form of death, disease, old age are drowning this world in the ocean of time, he does not realize the Truth.

## \|\| 49.36 \|\|

*pratikṣaṇabhayaṃ kālaḥ kṣīyamāṇo na lakṣyate \|*  
*āmakuṃbha ivāṃbhaḥ stho viśīrṇo na vibhāvyate \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. He does not observe that Time is running out at every moment, just as a pot of unbaked clay is not seen as broken inside water.

## \|\| 49.37 \|\|

*yujyate veṣṭanaṃ vāyorākāśasya ca khaṇḍanam \|*  
*grathanañca taraṅgāṇāmāsthā nāyuṣi yujyate \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. It may be possible to wrap the wind," rend the ether, knot the waves but it is not possible to maintain perpetuity of the age.

## \|\| 49.38 \|\|

*pṛthivī dahyate yena meruścāpi viśīryate \|*  
*śuṣyate sāgarajalaṃ śarīrasya ca kā kathā \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Since even this wide earth is burnt, even the lofty Meru is shattered, even the deep water of the ocean is dried, what can be said about the insignificant body?

## \|\| 49.39 \|\|

*apatyaṃ me kalatraṃ me dhanaṃ me bāndhavāśca me \|*  
*jalpantamiti martyājaṃ hanti kālavṛko balāt \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. I have a son, wife, wealth and relations. Thus, while the goat of man thinks, the wplf of time takes him away by force.

## \|\| 49.40 \|\|

*idaṃ kṛtamidaṃ kāryamidamanyatkṛtākṛtam \|*  
*evamīhāsamāyuktaṃ kṛtāntaḥ kurute vaśam \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. This has been done, this is to be done, this other is half done. Thus thinking one is taken by Yama.

## \|\| 49.41 \|\|

*śvaḥ kāryamadya kurvīta pūrvāhne cāparāhnikam \|*  
*na hi mṛtyuḥ pratīkṣeta kṛtaṃ vāpyatha vākṛtam \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. One shall do to-day what is to be done tomorrow, before noon what is to be done aftemoon-but whether done or not done. Death does not wait whether a person has completed the task or left it incomplete.

## \|\| 49.42 \|\|

*jarādarśitapanthānaṃ pracaṇḍavyādhisainikam \|*  
*adhiṣṭhito mṛtyuśatruṃ trātāraṃ kiṃ na paśyati \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Death-fire is there. Old age has shown him the way. Fierce diseases are his accompanying soldiers. The man attacked sees no protection.

## \|\| 49.43 \|\|

*tṛṣṇāsūcīvinirbhinnaṃ siktaṃ viṣayasarpiṣā \|*  
*rāgadveṣānale pakvaṃ mṛtyuraśrāti mānavam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Split with the needle of greed, soaked in. the oil of passions, cooked in the fire of angel and envy, man is eaten up by death.

## \|\| 49.44 \|\|

*bālāṃśca yauvanasthāṃśca vṛddhāna garbhagatānapi \|*  
*sarvānāviśate mṛtyurevambhūmidaṃ jagat \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Death takes away even children, young people, old men and those in womb-such is this world.

## \|\| 49.45 \|\|

*svadehamapi jīvo 'yaṃ muktvā yāti yamālayam \|*  
*strīmātṛpitṛputtrādisambandhaḥ kena hetunā \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. Not to speak of wife, mother, father, son and other relatives, the soul leaves even his own body and goes to the abode of Yama.

## \|\| 49.46 \|\|

*duḥ khamūlaṃ hi saṃsāraḥ sa yasyāsti sa duḥ khitaḥ \|*  
*tasya tyāgaḥ kṛto yena sa sukhī nāparaḥ kvacit \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. This world has sorrow as the root. Whosoever possesses the same is sorrowful. Whosoever leaves it is happy.

## \|\| 49.47 \|\|

*prabhavaṃ sarvaduḥ khānāmālayaṃ sakalāpadām \|*  
*āśrayaṃ sarvapāpānāṃ saṃsāraṃ varjayetkṣaṇāt \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. So leave in a moment this world which is the source of all sorrows, abode of all calamities and shelter for all sins.

## \|\| 49.48 \|\|

*lohadārumayaiḥ pāśaiḥ pumānbaddho vimucyate \|*  
*puttradāramayaiḥ pāśairmucyate na kadācana \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Man can get rid of fetters of iron and wood but not the fetters in the form of his son and wife.

## \|\| 49.49 \|\|

*yāvataḥ kurute jantuḥ sambandhānmanasaḥ priyān \|*  
*tāvanto 'sya nikhanyante hṛdaye śokaśaṅkavaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. So far as a being makes relations dear to heart, the cones of sorrow are being ypegged in his; heart.

## \|\| 49.50 \|\|

*vañcitāśeṣavittaistairnityaṃ loko vināśitaḥ \|*  
*hā hanta viṣayāhārairdehasthondriyataskaraiḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Eternally this world is destroyed by the thieves in the form of organs staying in the body who feed on the objects of pleasure and take away all wealth by deception.

## \|\| 49.51 \|\|

*māṃsalubdho yathā matsyo lohaśaṅkuṃ na paśyati \|*  
*sukhalubdhastathā dehī yamavādhāṃ na paśyati \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Just as the fish tempted by flesh -does not see the iron-cone, so also a creature, tempted by enjoyment does not anticipate Yama's torture.

## \|\| 49.52 \|\|

*hitāhitaṃ na jānanto nityamunmārgagāminaḥ \|*  
*kukṣipūraṇaniṣṭhā ye te narā nārakāḥ khaga \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. The people going on the wrong path do not distinguish between good and evil. These men deserve hell, О bird, who are engaged only in filling up their bellies.

## \|\| 49.53 \|\|

*nidrābhīmaithunāhārāḥ sarveṣāṃ prāṇināṃ samāḥ \|*  
*jñānavānmānavaḥ prokto jñānahīnaḥ paśuḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. Sleep, fear, sex and food are equal for all creatures, fie who possesses knowledge is a man and he who is without knowledge is an animal.

## \|\| 49.54 \|\|

*prabhāte malamūttrābhyāṃ kṣuttṛḍbhyāṃ madhyage ravau \|*  
*rātrau madananidrābhyāṃ bādhyante mūḍhamānavāḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Foolish people are troubled by natural call in the morning, by hunger and thirst at midday and by sex and sleep at night.

## \|\| 49.55 \|\|

*svadehadhanadārādiniratāḥ sarvajantavaḥ \|*  
*jāyante ca mriyante ca hā hantājñānamohitāḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. People love their bodies, wealth, wives, etc. Alas! being infatuated by ignorance they are born and they die.

## \|\| 49.56 \|\|

*tasmātsaṅgaḥ sadā tyājyaḥ sacettyaktuṃ na śakyate \|*  
*mahadbhiḥ saha kartavyaḥ santaḥ saṅgasya bheṣajam \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. Therefore, one should ahvays shun company. If it is not possible, one should associate with the great.

## \|\| 49.57 \|\|

*satsaṅgaśca vivekaśca nirmalaṃ nayanadvayam \|*  
*yasya nāsti naraḥ so 'ndhaḥ kathaṃ na syādamārgagaḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. Association with the good and discrimination are two clear eyes. Whosoever lacks them is a blind man who can go astray from the right path.

## \|\| 49.58 \|\|

*svasvavarṇāśramācāraniratāḥ sarvamānavāḥ \|*  
*na jānanti paraṃ dharmaṃ vṛthā naśyanti dāmbhikāḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. Men are busy with their own affairs devolved on them by their ancestral profession or by their paticular stage in life. They do not know about true religion. Being deceitful they perish.

## \|\| 49.59 \|\|

*kimāyāsaparāḥ kecidvratacaryādisaṃyutāḥ \|*  
*ajñānasaṃvṛtātmānaḥ sañcaranti pracārakāḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Why should many preachers practising vows, but with their vision blinded by ignorance laboriously move here and there.

## \|\| 49.60-61 \|\|

*nāmamātreṇa santuṣṭāḥ karmakāṇḍaratā narāḥ \|*  
*mantroccāraṇahomādyairbhrāmitāḥ kratuvistaraiḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

*ekabhuktopavāsādyairniyamaiḥ kāyaśoṣaṇaiḥ \|*  
*mūḍhāḥ parokṣamicchanti mama māyāvimohitāḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

60-61. Men engaged in ritual practices are satisfied with very little; being misguided they conduct sacrifices accompanied by mantras and oblations. Some fools infatuated by my illusion wish for moksa by torturing their bodies by fasts, taking cores but once in a day.

## \|\| 49.62 \|\|

*dehadaṇḍanamātreṇa kā muktiravivekinām \|*  
*valmīkatāḍanādeva mṛtaḥ kinnu mahoragaḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. Can the ignorant fools get release by torturing their body? Can a serpent die simply by beating the hole wherein it dwells 9 w

## \|\| 49.63 \|\|

*jaṭābhārājinairyuktā dāmbhikā veṣadhāriṇaḥ \|*  
*bhramanti jñānivalloke bhrāmayanti janānapi \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. The imposters who guise themselves with matted hair and deer-skins and pretend to be pious move about deceiving people.

## \|\| 49.64 \|\|

*saṃsārajasukhāsaktaṃ brahmajño 'smītivādinam \|*  
*karmabrahmobhayabhraṣṭaṃ taṃ tyajedantyajaṃ yathā \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. For him who takes delight in the pleasures of the world and pretends that he knows Brahman, both Korman and Brahman are far distant.

## \|\| 49.65 \|\|

*gṛhāraṇyasamā loke gatavrīḍā digambarāḥ \|*  
*caranti gardabhādyāśca viraktāste bhavanti kim \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. Alike at home and in forest, naked and shameless, the donkeys move here and there. Do they become unattached?

## \|\| 49.66 \|\|

*mṛdbhasmoddhūlanādeva muktāḥ syuryadi mānavāḥ \|*  
*mṛdbhasmavāsī nityaṃ śvā sa kiṃ mukto bhaviṣyati \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. If men achieve release by anointing mud and ash will they be released?

## \|\| 49.67 \|\|

*tṛṇaparṇodakāhārāḥ satataṃ vanavāsinaḥ \|*  
*jambūkākhumṛgādyāśca tāpasāste bhavanti kim \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. Jackal, mouse and dear live in the forest and consume grass, leaves and water. Are they also ascetics?

## \|\| 49.68 \|\|

*ājanmamaraṇāntañca gaṅgāditaṭinīsthitāḥ \|*  
*maṇḍūkamatsyapramukhā yoginaste bhavanti kim \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. From their birth to their death, frogs and fish stay in the river such as Ganga. Do they become Yogins?

## \|\| 49.69 \|\|

*pārāvatāḥ śilāhārāḥ kadācidapi cātakāḥ \|*  
*na pibanti mahītoyaṃ vratinaste bhavanti kim \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. Doves, $ilaharas and Catakas do not drink water from the earth. Are they Vratins?

## \|\| 49.70 \|\|

*tasmānnityādikaṃ karma lokarañjanakārakam \|*  
*mokṣasya kāraṇaṃ sākṣātattvajñāna khageśvara \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. People are content with their routine work. But that does not help them to reach the goal. It is the knowledge of truth or reality that effects release.

## \|\| 49.71 \|\|

*ṣarḍśanamahākūpe patitāḥ paśavaḥ khaga \|*  
*paramārthaṃ na jānanti paśupāśaniyantritāḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

71\. О lord of birds, ignorant fools, fallen in the dark well of six darsanas and bound by the noose of attachment, fail to realize the truth in the form of para-brahman.

## \|\| 49.72 \|\|

*vedaśāstrārṇavairgherairuhyamānā itastataḥ \|*  
*ṣaḍūrminigrahagrastāstiṣṭhanti hi kutārkikāḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

72\. Floating on the surface of the ocean in the form of Veda-sastra and caught by the waves of six mgrahas the bad logicians suffer miserably.

## \|\| 49.73 \|\|

*vedāgamapurāṇajñaḥ paramārthaṃ na vetti yaḥ \|*  
*viḍambakasya tasyaiva tatsarvaṃ kākabhāṣitam \|\| 73 \|\|*

73\. A person well versed in the Vedas, Agamas and Puranas but ignorant of Reality is not distinct from a magician whose utterances resemble the caw-caw sound of a crow.

## \|\| 49.74 \|\|

*idaṃ jñānamidaṃ jñeyamiti cintāsamākulāḥ \|*  
*paṭhantyaharniśaṃ śāstraṃ paratattvaparāṅmukhāḥ \|\| 74 \|\|*

74\. Those who are worried about the sources and objects of knowledge take recourse to Shastras which they study day and niht but they are miles away from the goal of Ultimate Truth.

## \|\| 49.75 \|\|

*vākyacchandonibandhena kāvyālaṅkāraśobhitāḥ \|*  
*cintayā duḥkhitā mūḍhāstiṣṭhanti vyākulendriyāḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

75\. Literary compositions arc decorated by the figures of speech, syntactical arrangement of words and by variety of meters. The fools who are worried cannot derive any solace from them.

## \|\| 49.76 \|\|

*anyathā paramaṃ tattvaṃ janāḥ kliśyanti cānyathā \|*  
*anyathā śāstrasadbhāvo vyākhyāṃ kurvanti cānyathā \|\| 76 \|\|*

76\. Reality is something else and people suffer due to something else. The meaning of the scriptures is something else and people define something else.

## \|\| 49.77 \|\|

*kathayantyuvanmanībhāvaṃ svayaṃ nānubhavanti ca \|*  
*ahaṅkārastāḥ kecidupadeśādivārjitāḥ \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. A few proud people without traditional knowledge misinterpret the Vedas which they do not rightly understand.

## \|\| 49.78 \|\|

*paṭhanti vedaśāstrāṇi bodhayanti parasparam \|*  
*na jānanti paraṃ tattvaṃ darvī pākarasaṃ yathā \|\| 78 \|\|*

78\. They study the Vedas and discuss. But they do not realize the Ultimate Reality just as a spoon does not know the taste of food.

## \|\| 49.79 \|\|

*śiro vahati puṣpāṇi gandhaṃ jānāti nāsikā \|*  
*paṭhanti vedaśāstrāṇi durlabho bhāvabodhakaḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

79\. The head carries i he flowers, the nose knows the scent. The people study the Vedas. But. very few persons understand the same.

## \|\| 49.80 \|\|

*tattvamātmasthamajñātvā mūḍhaḥ śāstreṣu muhyati \|*  
*gopaḥ kakṣāgate cchāge kūpaṃ paśyati durmatiḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. Not knowing the Reality of the self, a fool is infatuated by the sastras. When the goat stands in the shed, the shepherd seeks for it in the well in vain.

## \|\| 49.81 \|\|

*saṃsāramohanāśāya śābdabodho na hi kṣamaḥ \|*  
*na nivarteta timiraṃ kadāciddīpavārtayā \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. The knowledge of the Sastras is not competent to destroy the infatuation accruing from worldly affairs. The wick of a lamp cannot remove darkness which light alone can do.

## \|\| 49.82 \|\|

*prajñāhīnasya paṭhanaṃ yathāndhasya ca darpaṇam \|*  
*ataḥ prajñāvatāṃ śāstraṃ tattvajñānasya lakṣaṇam \|\| 82 \|\|*

82\. For the ignorant person the study of sastras is useless as a mirror is useless for the blind. But for the wise the same works as the means of true knowledge.

## \|\| 49.83 \|\|

*idaṃ jñānamidaṃ jñeyaṃ sarvantu śrotumicchati \|*  
*divyavarṣasahasrācca śāstrāntaṃ naiva gacchati \|\| 83 \|\|*

83\. Sastras arc the source of knowledge which one desires to attain. But that is not an easy affair. One may not achieve knowledge even in one thousand divine years.

## \|\| 49.84 \|\|

*anekāni ca śāstrāṇi svalpāyurvighnakoṭayaḥ \|*  
*tasmātsāraṃ vijānīyātkṣīraṃ haṃsa ivāmbhasi \|\| 84 \|\|*

84\. Scriptures arc many, age is short. Obstacles come in battalion. One should pick up truth from falsehood as a goose picks up milk from water.

## \|\| 49.85 \|\|

*abhyasya vedaśāstrāṇi tattvaṃ jñātvātha budbhimān \|*  
*palālamiva dhānyārthī sarvaśāstrāṇi santyajet \|\| 85 \|\|*

85\. Having studied the Vedas and realized their essence the wise man should leave all the sastras just as one desiring corn leaves the husk.

## \|\| 49.86 \|\|

*yathāmṛtena tṛptasya nāhāreṇa prayojanam \|*  
*tattvajñasya tathā tārkṣya na śāstreṇa prayojanam \|\| 86 \|\|*

86\. just as one satiated with nectar has no use of food, no one who is in search of Reality has anything to do with the Mstras.

## \|\| 49.87 \|\|

*na vedādhyayanānmuktirna śāstrapaṭhanādapi \|*  
*jñānādeva hi kaivalyaṃ nānyathā vinatātmajaḥ \|\| 87 \|\|*

87\. One cannot obtain release by reading the Vedas or the sastras. Release comes from experience, not otherwise, О son of Vinata.

## \|\| 49.88 \|\|

*nāśramaḥ kāraṇaṃ mukterdarśanāni na kāraṇam \|*  
*tathaiva sarvakarmāṇi jñānameva hi kāraṇam \|\| 88 \|\|*

88\. A particular stage (asramd) in life is not conducive to release; nor any system of philosophy, nor any ritual nor the combined.

## \|\| 49.89 \|\|

*muktidā guruvāgekā vidyāḥ sarvā viḍambikāḥ \|*  
*śāstrabhārasahasreṣu hyekaṃ sañjīvanaṃ param \|\| 89 \|\|*

89\. The word of Guru alone can grant release. All knowledge is in vain. Among thousands of scriptures the word of Guru alone is vivifying.

## \|\| 49.90 \|\|

*advaitaṃ hi śivaṃ proktaṃ kriyayāparivarjitam \|*  
*guruvaktreṇa labhyeta nādhītāgamakoṭibhiḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

90\. The knowledge of the non-dual entity derived from the word of Guru can eti ect release. The practice of the ritual or the study of the crores of scriptures is quite in vain.

## \|\| 49.91 \|\|

*āgamoktaṃ vivekotthaṃ dvidhā jñānaṃ pracakṣate \|*  
*śabdavrahmāgamamayaṃ paraṃ brahma vivekajam \|\| 91 \|\|*

91\. Knowledge is twofold: One arising from the study of scriptures, the other arising from discrimination. Sabda-brahna is known from the scriptures and Parabrahma is known from discrimination

## \|\| 49.92 \|\|

*advaitaṃ kecidicchanti dvaitamicchanti cāpare \|*  
*samaṃ tattvaṃ na jānanti dvaitāddvaitavivarjitam \|\| 92 \|\|*

92\. Some seek for the knowledge of nondual brahman (i.e. brahman without Maya) and some for that of the Dual (brahman with Maya). But they do not realize reality devoid of dvaita and advaita.

## \|\| 49.93 \|\|

*dve pade bandhamokṣāya namameti mameti ca \|*  
*mameti badhyate janturnamameti pramucyate \|\| 93 \|\|*

93\. Two words mine (mama) and not mine (na mama) signify bondage and release. By mine the person is bound and by not mine he is released.

## \|\| 49.94 \|\|

*tatkarma yanna bandhāya sā vidyā yā vimuktidā \|*  
*āyāsāyāparaṃ karma vidyānyā śilpanaipuṇam \|\| 94 \|\|*

94\. That is the right action which does not put one into bondage. That is the right knowledge which brings him release. All oilier action is but a labour and all other knotledge is but an artisanship.

## \|\| 49.95 \|\|

*yāvatkarmāṇi dīpyante yāvatsaṃsāravāsanā \|*  
*yāvadindriyacāpalyaṃ tāvattattvakathā kutaḥ \|\| 95 \|\|*

95\. So far as actions thrive, so far as desire subsists, so far as organs are active, there can be little talk of realizing the Self.

## \|\| 49.96 \|\|

*yāvaddehābhimānaśca mamatā yāvadeva hi \|*  
*yāvatprayatnavego 'sti yāvatsaṃkalpakalpanā \|\| 96 \|\|*

96\. So far as one takes pride in body, so far as affection for worldly objects is there, so far there is persistence in efforts, so far as desire to do is there.

## \|\| 49.97 \|\|

*yāvanno manasaḥ sthairyaṃ na yāvacchāstracintanam \|*  
*yāvanna gurukāruṇyaṃ tāvattattvakathā kutaḥ \|\| 97 \|\|*

97\. So far as mind is not steady, so far as one does not meditate on scriptures, so far as Guru's blessing is not there, there can be little talk of realizing the Self.

## \|\| 49.98 \|\|

*tāvattapo vrataṃ tīrthaṃ japahomārcanādikam \|*  
*vedaśāstrāgamakathā yāvattattvaṃ na vindati \|\| 98 \|\|*

98\. Penance, vow, pilgrimage, muttering of mantras sacrifice, worship, talk of the Vedas and Shastras are meaningful only when one knows Reality.

## \|\| 49.99 \|\|

*tasmātsarvaprayatnena sarvāvasthāsu sarvadā \|*  
*tattvaniṣṭho bhavettārkṣya yadīcchenmokṣamātmanaḥ \|\| 99 \|\|*

99\. Therefore, by all efforts and in all conditions, focus your attention on atman, О bird, if you desire release.

## \|\| 49.100 \|\|

*dharmajñānaprasūnasya svargamokṣaphalasya ca \|*  
*tāpatrayādisantaptaśchāyāṃ mokṣataroḥ śrayet \|\| 100 \|\|*

100\. Tortured by the threefold suffering one should take shelter in the shade of the tree of moksa which has blossoms of dharma and knowledge and fruits of heaven and release.

## \|\| 49.101 \|\|

*tasmājjñānenātmatattvaṃ vijñeyaṃ śrīgurormukhāt \|*  
*sukhena mucyate janturghorasaṃsārabandhanāt \|\| 101 \|\|*

101\. Therefore, one should derive knowledge of Reality from one's preceptor. Thus one is easily released from the bondage of one's actions.

## \|\| 49.102 \|\|

*tattvajñasyāntimaṃ kṛtyaṃ śṛṇu vakṣyāmi te 'dhunā \|*  
*yena mokṣamavāpnoti brahma nirvāṇasaṃjñakam \|\| 102 \|\|*

102\. Now, hear, I shall tell you the last step by which one can attain the final goal.

## \|\| 49.103 \|\|

*antakāle tu puruṣa āgate gatasādhvasaḥ \|*  
*chindyādasaṃgaśastreṇa spṛhāṃ dehe 'nu yā ca tam \|\| 103 \|\|*

103\. When the hour of great departure arrives he should without fear cut off all attachments with the weapon of detachment.

## \|\| 49.104 \|\|

*gṛhātpravrājito dhīraḥ puṇyatīrthajalāplutaḥ \|*  
*śucau vivikta āsīno vidhivatkalpitāsane \|\| 104 \|\|*

104\. The calm man should leave the house, start on pilgrimage and bathe in the holy waters. Then having prepared a seat as prescribed he should sit upon it with detachment.

## \|\| 49.105 \|\|

*abhyasenmanasā śuddhaṃ trivṛdbrahmākṣaraṃ param \|*  
*mano yaṣchejjitaśvāso brahma bījamavismaran \|\| 105 \|\|*

105\. With a pure mind he should meditate upon the pure, three-syliabled От signifying brahman. Without forgetting the brahma-blja one should conquer the breath and control

## \|\| 49.106 \|\|

*niyacchedviṣayebhyo 'kṣānmanasā buddhi sārathiḥ \|*  
*manaḥ karmabhirākṣiptaṃ śubhārthe dhārayeddhiyā \|\| 106 \|\|*

106\. He should control the organs from the objects of senses with intellect as his companion. The mind drawn away by the actions should be possessed for the good by the intellect.

## \|\| 49.107 \|\|

*ahaṃ brahma paraṃ dhāma brahmāhaṃ paramaṃ padam \|*  
*evaṃ samīkṣya cātmānamātmanyādhāya niṣkale \|\| 107 \|\|*

107\. "I am brahman the supreme shelter, I am brahman the highest stage," having thus concluded he should concentrate his personal self on the universal self.

## \|\| 49.108 \|\|

*omityekākṣaraṃ brahma vyāharanmāmanusmaran \|*  
*yaḥ prayāti tyajandehaṃ sa yāti paramāṃ gatim \|\| 108 \|\|*

108\. Pronouncing Om the one-syllabled brahman and remembering me whosoever leaves his body obtains the highest state.

## \|\| 49.109 \|\|

*na yatra dāmbhikā yānti jñānavairāgyavarjitāḥ \|*  
*sudhiyastāṃ gatiṃ yānti tānahaṃ kathayāmi te \|\| 109 \|\|*

109\. Wiere the hypocrites devoid of knowledge and self-control do not reach, the wise obtain that state.

## \|\| 49.110 \|\|

*nirmānamohā jitasaṃgadoṣā adhyātmanityā vinivṛttakāmāḥ \|*  
*dvandvairvimuktāḥ sukhaduḥ khasaṃjñairgacchantyamūḍhāḥ padamavyayaṃ tat \|\| 110 \|\|*

110\. The wise who are without ego and infatuation who have left attachment and vices, who contemplate on soul, whose desires have turned back, who are free from eas the effects of joy and sorrow, attain that imperishable state.

## \|\| 49.111 \|\|

*jñānahrade satyajale rāgadveṣamalāpahe \|*  
*yaḥ snāti mānase tīrthe sa vai mokṣamavāpnuyāt \|\| 111 \|\|*

111\. He obtains release who bathes in the holy tirtha of mind whose pond is knowledge, water is truth.and which is devoid of filth ©f attachment and envy.

## \|\| 49.112 \|\|

*prauḍhavairāgyamāsthāya bhajate māmananyabhāk \|*  
*pūrṇadṛṣṭiḥ prasannātmā sa vai mokṣamavāpnuyāt \|\| 112 \|\|*

112\. Whosoever meditates on me with full devotion without attachment for any worldly object, who has imbibed complete awareness and whose mind is all pleasure.

## \|\| 49.113 \|\|

*tyaktvā gṛhaṃ ca yastīrthe nivasenmaraṇotsukaḥ \|*  
*muktikṣetreṣu mriyate sa vai mokṣamavāpnuyāt \|\| 113 \|\|*

113\. With a desire to die whosoever leaves his house and stays in a holy place where he breathes his last, can obtain release.

## \|\| 49.114 \|\|

*ayodhyā mathurā māyā kāśī kāñcī avantika \|*  
*purī dvāravatī jñeyāḥ saptaitā mokṣadāyikāḥ \|\| 114 \|\|*

114\. Ayodhya, Mathura, Maya, KasI, KancI, Avantika Purl and Dvaravati-these seven places of pilgrimage can grant release

## \|\| 49.115 \|\|

*iti te kathitaṃ tārkṣya mokṣadharmaṃ sanātanam \|*  
*jñānavairāgyasahitaṃ śrutvā mokṣamavāpnuyāt \|\| 115 \|\|*

115\. Thus I have told you, О Garuda, about the ways of release. Preaching the same with knowledge and control, one may obtain release.

## \|\| 49.116 \|\|

*mokṣaṃ gacchanti tattvajñā dhārmikāḥ svargatiṃ narāḥ \|*  
*pāpino durgatiṃ yānti saṃsaranti khagādayaḥ \|\| 116 \|\|*

116\. Those who have realized self can get release. Those who resort to the ritual can go to heaven. Those who commit sins go to hell. Others rotate on the wheel of birth and death.

## \|\| 49.117 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*svapraśrottararāddhāntamevaṃ bhagavato mukhāt \|*  
*śrutvā hṛṣṭatanustārkṣyo nanāma jagadīśvaram \|\| 117 \|\|*

117\. Thus, having received the answer of his queries from the mouth of the lord, Garuda was delighted and he bowed to the lord of the world.

## \|\| 49.118 \|\|

*sandeho me mahānnaṣṭo bhavadvākyavirocanāt \|*  
*ityuktvā viṣṇumāmantrya sa gataḥ kaśyapāśramam \|\| 118 \|\|*

118\. "My doubts have been cleared, О lord, by your words"-so saying he took leave of Visnu and went to the hermitage of Kasyapa

## \|\| 49.119 \|\|

*sadyo dehāntaraṃ yāti yathā yāti vilambataḥ \|*  
*anayorubhayoścaiva na virodhastathaiva vaḥ \|\| 119 \|\|*

119\. One may assume body sooner or later after death. There is no inconsistency in tins approach.

## \|\| 49.120 \|\|

*sarvamākhyātavāṃstāta śruto bhagavato yathā \|*  
*mārīco 'pi mudaṃ lebhe śrutvā vākyaṃ ramāpateḥ \|\| 120 \|\|*

120\. Garuda repeated what he had heard from the lord. Marlca was also delighted on hearing the words of Lord Visnu.

## \|\| 49.121 \|\|

*apākṛtastu sandeho brāhmaṇā bhavatāṃ mayā \|*  
*uktaṃ suparṇasaṃjñantu purāṇaṃ paramādbhutam \|\| 121 \|\|*

121\. О Brahmanas, I have removed your doubts and told you the most wonderful Purana known as Garuda.

## \|\| 49.122-123 \|\|

*idamāpa harestārkṣyastārkṣyādāpa tato bhṛguḥ \|*  
*bhṛgorvasiṣṭhaḥ saṃprāpa vāmadevastataḥ punaḥ \|\| 122 \|\|*

*parāśaramuniḥ prāpa tasmādvyāsastato hyaham \|*  
*mayā tu bhavatāṃ proktaṃ paraṃ guhyaṃ hareridam \|\| 123 \|\|*

122-123. Garuda got it from Visnu. Bhrgu. got it from Garuda, Vasistha from Bhrgu, Vamadcva from Vasistha Parasara from Vamadeva, Vyasa from Parasara and I from Vyasa, Thus I have told you the secret of lord Visnu.

## \|\| 49.124 \|\|

*ya idaṃ śṛṇuyānmartyo yo vāpyabhidadhāti ca \|*  
*ihāmutra ca loke sa sarvatra sukhamāpnuyāt \|\| 124 \|\|*

124\. A man who hears the same or narrates the same is blessed with pleasure here as well as hereafter.

## \|\| 49.125 \|\|

*vrajataḥ saṃyamanyāṃ yadduḥ khamatra nirūpitam \|*  
*asya śravaṇataḥ puṇyaṃ tanmukto jāyate tataḥ \|\| 125 \|\|*

125\. Whatsoever sorrows having been told about them who go to the city of Yama, whosoever hears about them gets release.

## \|\| 49.126 \|\|

*atroktakarmapākādiśravaṇācca nṛṇāmiha \|*  
*vairāgyamāvahedyasmāttasmācchrotavyameva ca \|\| 126 \|\|*

126\. After hearing about the results of actions described here, men may become averse to pleasures. The account is, therefore, meaningful for the listener.

## \|\| 49.127 \|\|

*bhajata jitahṛṣīkāḥ kṛṣṇamenaṃ munīśaṃ samajani bata yasmādgīḥ sudhāsāradhārā \|*  
*pṛṣatamapi yadīyaṃ varṇarūpaṃ nipīya śrutipuṭaculukena prāpnuyādātmanaikyam \|\| 127 \|\|*

127\. О ye that have controlled your organs, praise the lord from whom this voice has come out like a stream of nectar, by drinking (or hearing) even a drop or a handful of syllables of which a man may attain union with Paramatman.

## \|\| 49.128 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*iti sūtamukhodgīrṇāṃsarvaśāstrārthamaṇḍitām \|*  
*vaiṣṇavīṃ vāksudhāṃ pītvā ṛṣayastuṣṭimāyayuḥ \|\| 128 \|\|*

128\. The sages were fully satisfied having drunk the Vaisnavl nectar of words coming out of the mouth of SIta, replete with the essence of scriptures.

## \|\| 49.129 \|\|

*praśaśaṃsustathānyonyaṃ sūtaṃ sarvārthadarśinam \|*  
*praharṣamatulaṃ prāpurmunayaḥ śaunakādayaḥ \|\| 129 \|\|*

129\. They praised the Seta who knew the essence of the sastras.

## \|\| 49.130 \|\|

*iti harivacanāni sūtavācā khagapatisaṃśayabhedakāni yāni \|*  
*sa munirapi niśamya śaunakendro bahutaramānayati sma cātmani svam \|\| 130 \|\|*

130\. Thus having heard through the mouth of SIta the words of lord Visnu allaying the doubts of Garuda the sage Shaunaka was fully safisfied.

## \|\| 49.131 \|\|

*apūjayaṃste munayastadānīmudākhāgbhirmuhureva sūtam \|*  
*dhanyo 'si sūta tvamihetyudairayanvyasarjayaṃstaṃ ca nivartite 'dhvare \|\| 131 \|\|*  
*purāṇaṃ gāruḍaṃ puṇyaṃ pavitraṃ pāpanāśanam \|\|*

131\. The sages honoured Suta with liberal praise saying, "O Suta you deserve a very high praise." Then they bade him farewell, the sacrifice being over.

## \|\| 49.132 \|\|

*śṛṇvatāṃ kāmanāpūraṃ śrotavyaṃ sarvadaiva hi \|\| 132 \|\|*

132\. This holy Garuda Purana destroys sins committed by the listeners. This should be heard therefore.

## \|\| 49.133 \|\|

*śrutvā dānāni deyāni vācakāyākhilāni ca \|*  
*pūrvoktaśayanādīni nānyathā saphalaṃ bhavet \|\| 133 \|\|*

133\. Having heard the Purana, gifts of bed, etc., should be given in charity or else it would not be fruitful.

## \|\| 49.134 \|\|

*purāṇaṃ pūjayetpūrvaṃ vācakaṃ tadanantaram \|*  
*vastrālaṅkāragodānairdakṣiṇābhiśca sādaram \|\| 134 \|\|*

134\. First of all, this Purana should be worshipped, then the reciter with the fee in cash, clothes, ornaments and cows.

## \|\| 49.135 \|\|

*annadānairhemadānairbhamidānaiśca bhūribhiḥ \|*  
*pūjayedvācakaṃ bhaktyā bahupuṇyaphalāptaye \|\| 135 \|\|*

135\. To obtain merit the reciter should be honoured devoutly with gifts of gold, com, earth and other things.

## \|\| 49.136 \|\|

*yaścedaṃ śṛṇuyānmartyo yathāpi parikīrtayet \|*  
*vihāya yātanāṃ ghorāṃ dhūtapāpo divaṃ vrajet \|\| 136 \|\|*

136\. The man who hears it or narrates it gets rid of fierce tortures in hell and shaking off his sins aside enters heaven where he enjoys the company of celestial nymphs.
